## I / 一

===Original===

Because of the crowd they held Chandler’s trial in the all-purpose room of the high school. It smelled of leather and stale sweat. He walked up the three steps to the stage, with the bailiff’s hand on his elbow, and took his place at the defendant’s table.

Chandler’s lawyer looked at him without emotion. He was appointed by the court. He was willing to do his job, but his job didn’t require him to like his client. All he said was, “Stand up. The judge is coming in.”

Chandler got to his feet and leaned on the table while the bailiff chanted his call and the chaplain read some verses from John. He did not listen. The Bible verse came too late to help him, and besides he ached.

When the police arrested him they had not been gentle. There were four of them. They were from the plant’s own security force and carried no guns. They didn’t need any; Chandler had put up no resistance after the first few moments﻿—that is, he stopped as soon as he could stop﻿—but the police hadn’t stopped. He remembered that very clearly. He remembered the nightstick across the side of his head that left his ear squashed and puffy, he remembered the kick in the gut that still made walking painful. He even remembered the series of blows about the skull that had knocked him out.

The bruises along his rib cage and left arm, though, he did not remember getting. Obviously the police had been mad enough to keep right on subduing him after he was already unconscious.

Chandler did not blame them﻿—exactly. He supposed he would have done the same thing.

The judge was having a long mumble with the court stenographer apparently about something which had happened in the Union House the night before. Chandler knew Judge Ellithorp slightly. He did not expect to get a fair trial. The previous December the judge himself, while possessed, had smashed the transmitter of the town’s radio station, which he owned, and set fire to the building it occupied. His son-in-law had been killed in the fire.

Laughing, the judge waved the reporter back to his seat and glanced around the courtroom. His gaze touched Chandler lightly, like the flick of the hanging strands of cord that precede a railroad tunnel. The touch carried the same warning. What lay ahead for Chandler was destruction.

===Chinese===

由于旁听的人太多，钱德勒（Chandler）的审判改在中学的多功能室里举行。屋里有一股皮革和馊汗的气味。他走上通向舞台的三级台阶，法警的手扶着他的臂肘，在被告席的桌子前就了位。

钱德勒的律师毫无表情地看着他。他是法庭指定的。他愿意履行职责，但职责并不要求他喜欢自己的当事人。他只说了一句：“站起来。法官进来了。”

钱德勒站起身，倚在桌上，法警拖着长腔宣唱开庭，牧师则诵读了《约翰福音》里的几节经文。他没有听。圣经经文来得太迟，帮不了他，何况他浑身作痛。

警察逮捕他的时候可没有手下留情。他们一共四个人，都是厂里自设保安队的，没带枪。他们也用不着带；起先那么几下之后，钱德勒就没有再抵抗——也就是说，他一能停下就立刻停下了——可警察没有停。这一点他记得清清楚楚。他记得抡在他头侧、把耳朵打得又扁又肿的那一警棍，记得踢在肚子上、至今仍让他走路作痛的那一脚。他甚至记得劈头盖脑落在脑壳上、把他打昏过去的那一阵击打。

不过，肋侧和左臂上的那些淤伤是怎么来的，他就不记得了。显然，警察气昏了头，在他早已不省人事之后还接着对他继续“制伏”。

钱德勒并不怪他们——也算不上怪。他想，换了是他自己，多半也会干出同样的事来。

法官正跟法庭速记员低声咕哝个没完，说的显然是前一天晚上工会会馆（Union House）里发生的什么事。钱德勒与埃利索普法官（Judge Ellithorp）有过一面之交。他不指望得到一场公正的审判。去年十二月，法官本人在被附身期间砸了镇上电台的发报机——那家电台就是他自己的产业——还放火烧了电台所在的那栋房子。他的女婿就丧生在那场火里。

法官笑着挥手让那名记者回到座位上去，然后环视法庭。他的目光轻轻擦过钱德勒，就像火车驶近铁路隧道前，垂挂在洞口的绳索梢头拂身而过。那一拂也带着同样的警告：等着钱德勒的，是毁灭。

===Original===

“Read the charge,” ordered Judge Ellithorp. He spoke very loudly. There were more than six hundred persons in the auditorium; the judge didn’t want any of them to miss a word.

The bailiff ordered Chandler to stand and informed him that he was accused of having, on the seventeenth day of June last, committed on the person of Margaret Flershem, a minor, an act of rape﻿—“Louder!” ordered the judge testily.

“Yes, Your Honor,” said the bailiff, and inflated his chest. “An Act of Rape under Threat of Bodily Violence,” he cried; “and Did Further Commit on the Person of Said Margaret Flershem an Act of Aggravated Assault﻿—”

Chandler rubbed his aching side, looking at the ceiling. He remembered the look in Peggy Flershem’s eyes as he forced himself on her. She was only sixteen years old, and at that time he hadn’t even known her name.

The bailiff boomed on: “﻿—and Did Further Commit on that Same Seventeenth Day of June Last on the Person of Ingovar Porter an Act of Assault with Intent to Rape, the Foregoing Being a True Bill Handed Down by the Grand Jury of Sepulpas County in Extraordinary Session Assembled, the Eighteenth Day of June Last.”

Judge Ellithorp looked satisfied as the bailiff sat down, quite winded. While the judge hunted through the papers on his desk the crowd in the auditorium stirred and murmured.

A child began to cry.

===Chinese===

“宣读指控。”埃利索普法官命令道。他说得非常响亮。礼堂里有六百多人，法官不想让其中任何一个人漏掉一个字。

法警命令钱德勒起立，并告知他：他被指控于去年六月十七日，在未成年人玛格丽特·弗勒舍姆（Margaret Flershem）身上实施了强奸罪行——“大声点！”法官不耐烦地喝道。

“是，法官大人。”法警说着挺起了胸膛。“以身体暴力相威胁实施强奸罪，”他高声念道；“并对前述玛格丽特·弗勒舍姆之人身进一步实施加重伤害罪——”

钱德勒揉着隐隐作痛的侧身，望着天花板。他记得自己强暴佩吉·弗勒舍姆（Peggy Flershem）时，她眼中的那种神情。她只有十六岁，而当时他连她的名字都不知道。

法警瓮声瓮气地继续念着：“——并于同一日，即去年六月十七日，在英戈瓦·波特（Ingovar Porter）之人身实施意图强奸之伤害罪；以上为塞帕尔帕斯县（Sepulpas County）大陪审团于去年六月十八日非常会议议定之正式起诉书。”

埃利索普法官看上去很满意，法警念完坐下，累得直喘。法官在桌上的纸堆里翻找的时候，礼堂里的人群骚动起来，嗡嗡低语。

一个孩子哭了起来。

===Original===

---

The judge stood up and pounded his gavel. “What is it? What’s the matter with him? You, Dundon!” The court attendant the judge was looking at hurried over and spoke to the child’s mother, then reported to the judge.

“I dunno, Your Honor. All he says is something scared him.”

The judge was enraged. “Well, that’s just fine! Now we have to take up the time of all these good people, probably for no reason, and hold up the business of this court, just because of a child. Bailiff! I want you to clear this courtroom of all children under﻿—” he hesitated, calculating voting blocks in his head﻿—“all children under the age of six. Dr. Palmer, are you there? Well, you better go ahead with the﻿—prayer.” The judge could not make himself say “the exorcism.”

“I’m sorry, madam,” he added to the mother of the crying two-year-old. “If you have someone to leave the child with, I’ll instruct the attendants to save your place for you.” She was also a voter.

Dr. Palmer rose, very grave, as he was embarrassed. He glared around the all-purpose room, defying anyone to smile, as he chanted: “Domina Pythonis, I command you, leave! Leave, Hel! Leave, Heloym! Leave, Sother and Thetragrammaton, leave, all unclean ones! I command you! In the name of God, in all of His manifestations!” He sat down again, still very grave. He knew that he did not make nearly as fine a showing as Father Lon, with his resonant *in nomina Jesu Christi et Sancti Ubaldi* and his censer, but the post of exorcist was filled in strict rotation, one month to a denomination, ever since the troubles started. Dr. Palmer was a Unitarian. Exorcisms had not been in the curriculum at the seminary and he had been forced to invent his own.

Chandler’s lawyer tapped him on the shoulder. “Last chance to change your mind,” he said.

“No. I’m not guilty, and that’s the way I want to plead.”

The lawyer shrugged and stood up, waiting for the judge to notice him.

Chandler, for the first time, allowed himself to meet the eyes of the crowd.

He studied the jury first. He knew some of them casually﻿—it was not a big enough town to command a jury of total strangers for any defendant, and Chandler had lived there most of his life. He recognized Pop Matheson, old and very stiff, who ran the railroad station cigar stand. Two of the other men were familiar as faces passed in the street. The forewoman, though, was a stranger. She sat there very composed and frowning, and all he knew about her was that she wore funny hats. Yesterday’s had been red roses when she was selected from the panel; today’s was, of all things, a stuffed bird.

===Chinese===

---

法官站起身，敲响了法槌。“怎么回事？他怎么了？你，邓登（Dundon）！”被法官望着的那名庭吏赶紧走过去跟孩子的母亲说了几句，然后向法官回话。

“我说不上，法官大人。他只说有什么东西吓着他了。”

法官勃然大怒。“好啊，真是好极了！现在就因为一个孩子，咱们就得占用这些好人的时间——多半还是毫无来由地占用——耽误本庭的公务。法警！我要你把法庭上所有不满——”他迟疑了一下，脑子里盘算着票仓——“所有不满六岁的孩子都清出去。帕尔默医生（Dr. Palmer），你在吗？那么，你最好就开始那个——祷告吧。”法官没法让自己把“驱邪”两个字说出口。

“对不起，夫人。”他又对那个哭闹的两岁孩子的母亲说，“要是您有地方可以安顿孩子，我会吩咐庭务员给您留座的。”她也是一位选民。

帕尔默医生站起身来，神情极其严肃，因为他很窘迫。他环视多功能室，瞪着全场，摆出一副谁敢发笑就跟谁算账的架势，朗声诵道：“蟒之女主，我命令你，离开！离开吧，赫尔！离开吧，赫洛伊姆！离开吧，索特尔与忒特拉格拉玛顿，离开吧，一切不洁之物！我命令你们！奉上帝之名，奉祂的一切显相！”他又坐下了，依旧神情极其严肃。他知道，自己这一套远不如朗神父（Father Lon）排场好看——人家有洪亮的*in nomina Jesu Christi et Sancti Ubaldi*，还有香炉——可自打乱子开始以来，驱邪师一职就严格轮值充任，一个教派轮一个月。帕尔默医生是个一位论派。神学院的课程里没有教过驱邪，他只得自创一套。

钱德勒的律师拍了拍他的肩膀。“改变主意的最后机会。”他说。

“不。我没有罪，我就要这么答辩。”

律师耸了耸肩，站起身，等法官注意到他。

钱德勒头一回允许自己去迎视人群的目光。

他先打量陪审团。其中几个人他不熟不识地认识——镇子不够大，没法给哪个被告凑出一批全然陌生的陪审员来，而钱德勒在这里住了大半辈子。他认出了马西森老爹（Pop Matheson），上了年纪，腰板僵直，在火车站经营雪茄摊。另外两个人面熟，是街上打过照面的脸。可陪审团团长是张生面孔。她坐在那里，神态从容，眉头微蹙，他对她全部的了解就是：她戴的帽子很滑稽。昨天从候选名单里选定她时，那顶帽子上是红玫瑰；今天这顶，居然缀着一只鸟标本。

===Original===

He did not think that any of them were possessed. He was not so sure of the audience.

He saw girls he had dated in high school, long before he met Margot; men he worked with at the plant. They all glanced at him, but he was not sure who was looking out through some of those familiar eyes. The visitors reliably watched all large gatherings, at least momentarily; it would be surprising if none of them were here.

“All right, how do you plead,” said Judge Ellithorp at last.

Chandler’s lawyer straightened up. “Not guilty, Your Honor, by reason of temporary pandemic insanity.”

The judge looked pleased. The crowd murmured, but they were pleased too. They had him dead to rights and it would have been a disappointment if Chandler had pleaded guilty. They wanted to see one of the vilest criminals in contemporary human society caught, exposed, convicted and punished; they did not want to miss a step of the process. Already in the playground behind the school three deputies from the sheriff’s office were loading their rifles, while the school janitor chalked lines around the handball court to mark where the crowd witnessing the execution would be permitted to stand.

===Chinese===

他认为他们当中谁也没有被附身。对旁听席上的观众，他就不那么有把握了。

他看见高中时约会过的姑娘们——那还是他认识玛戈（Margot）很久以前的事；还有厂里跟他一起干活的男人们。他们都朝他望，可他拿不准，从那些熟悉的眼珠后面往外望出去的是谁。那些访客（the visitors）必定会光顾一切大型集会，哪怕只是片刻工夫；要是它们一个也不在这里，那才奇怪。

“好，你如何答辩？”埃利索普法官终于开口。

钱德勒的律师直起身来。“无罪，法官大人——以暂时性瘟疫式精神失常为由。”

法官看上去很满意。人群窃窃私语，但他们也很满意。人赃并获、铁证如山，要是钱德勒直接认了罪，反倒要叫人失望。他们要亲眼看到当代人类社会最卑劣的罪犯之一被捉拿、被揭穿、被定罪、被惩处；这过程一步也不想错过。学校后面的操场上，治安官公署的三名副治安官已经在往步枪里装填子弹，校工则围着手球场用粉笔画线，标出围观行刑的人群获准站立的地方。

===Original===

---

The prosecution made its case very quickly. Mrs. Porter testified that she worked at McKelvey Bros., the antibiotics plant, where the defendant also worked. Yes, that was him. She had been attracted by the noise from the culture room last﻿—let’s see﻿—“Was it the seventeenth day of June last?” prompted the prosecutor, and Chandler’s attorney instinctively gathered his muscles to rise, hesitated, glanced at his client and shrugged. That was right, it was the seventeenth. Incautiously she went right into the room. She should have known better, she admitted. She should have called the plant police right away, but, well, they hadn’t had any trouble at the plant, you know, and﻿—well, she didn’t. She was a stupid woman, for all that she was rather good-looking, and insatiably curious. She had seen Peggy Flershem on the floor. “She was all *blood*. And her clothes were﻿—And she was, I mean her﻿—her body was﻿—” With relentless tact the prosecutor allowed her to stammer out her observation that the girl had clearly been raped. And she had seen Chandler laughing and breaking up the place, throwing racks of cultures through the windows, upsetting trays. Of course she had crossed herself and tried a quick exorcism but there was no visible effect; then Chandler had leaped at her. “He was *hateful*! He was just *foul*!” But as he began to attack her the plant police came, drawn by her screams.

Chandler’s attorney did not question.

Peggy Flershem’s deposition was introduced without objection from the defense. But she had little to say anyway, having been dazed at first and unconscious later. The plant police testified to having arrested Chandler; a doctor described in chaste medical words the derangements Chandler had worked on Peggy Flershem’s virgin anatomy. There was no question from Chandler’s lawyer﻿—and, for that matter, nothing to question. Chandler did not hope to pretend that he had not ravished and nearly killed one girl, then done his best to repeat the process on another. Sitting there as the doctor testified, Chandler was able to tally every break and bruise against the memory of what his own body had done. He had been a spectator then, too, as remote from the event as he was now; but that was why they had him on trial. That was what they did not believe.

At twelve-thirty the prosecution rested its case, Judge Ellithorp looking very pleased. He recessed the court for one hour for lunch, and the guards took Chandler back to the detention cell in the basement of the school.

Two Swiss cheese sandwiches and a wax-paper carton of chocolate milk were on the desk. They were Chandler’s lunch. As they had been standing, the sandwiches were crusty and the milk lukewarm. He ate them anyway. He knew what the judge looked pleased about. At one-thirty Chandler’s lawyer would put him on the stand, and no one would pay very much attention to what he had to say, and the jury would be out at most twenty minutes, and the verdict would be guilty. The judge was pleased because he would be able to pronounce sentence no later than four o’clock, no matter what. They had formed the habit of holding the executions at sundown. As, at that time of year, sundown was after seven, it would all go very well﻿—for everyone but Chandler. For Chandler it would be the end.

===Chinese===

---

控方很快就陈述完了案情。波特太太（Mrs. Porter）作证说，她在麦克尔维兄弟公司（McKelvey Bros.）——那家抗生素厂——上班，被告也在那里干活。是的，就是他。她是被培养室里传出的动静引过去的，时间是上——让我想想——“是去年六月十七日吗？”检察官提示道，钱德勒的律师本能地绷紧肌肉要站起来，又迟疑了一下，瞥了一眼自己的当事人，耸了耸肩。没错，就是十七日。她一时大意，径直走进了那间屋子。她本该更谨慎些的，她承认。她本该立刻叫厂警的，可是，唉，厂里一直没出过乱子，您知道，而且——唉，她没叫。她是个蠢女人，尽管模样还算标致，而且好奇心永无餍足。她看见佩吉·弗勒舍姆躺在地上。“她浑身都是*血*。她的衣服——她那个，我是说她的——她的身子——”检察官以毫不容情的圆滑，任由她结结巴巴地道出她的所见：那姑娘显然遭到了强奸。她还看见钱德勒在大笑，在砸场子，把一架架培养物从窗户扔出去，掀翻托盘。她当然画了十字，试着行了个匆匆的驱邪礼，可是看不出丝毫效力；接着钱德勒就朝她扑了过去。“他*可恨*极了！他就是个*污物*！”不过他刚开始袭击她，厂警就被她的尖叫声引来了。

钱德勒的律师没有发问。

佩吉·弗勒舍姆的书面证词被提交法庭，辩方没有异议。不过她本来也没什么可说的，起初她神志昏乱，后来便全然失去了知觉。厂警们作证说逮捕了钱德勒；一位医生用洁净无瑕的医学术语，描述了钱德勒对佩吉·弗勒舍姆处女之身造成的种种创损。钱德勒的律师没有发问——况且，也没有什么可问的。钱德勒并不指望能赖掉这一点：他强暴了一个姑娘，险些要了她的命，随后又竭力想对另一个如法炮制。医生作证的时候，钱德勒坐在那里，能把每一处破损与淤伤同他自己那具身体所干之事的记忆一一对上号。那时他也只是个旁观者，与那件事的疏离，和此刻一般无二；可这正是他们审判他的缘由。这正是他们不肯相信的地方。

十二点三十分，控方陈述完毕，埃利索普法官看上去非常满意。他宣布休庭一小时用午饭，看守们把钱德勒押回了学校地下室的拘留室。

桌上放着两块瑞士奶酪三明治和一纸盒巧克力奶。那是钱德勒的午饭。因为一直摆在那里，三明治的边角已经发硬，奶也温吞了。他还是把它们吃了下去。他知道法官在满意什么。一点三十分，钱德勒的律师会让他上证人席，没有谁会太留意他说些什么，陪审团至多退席二十分钟，裁决就会是有罪。法官满意的是：无论如何，他最迟四点就能宣判。他们已经养成了在日落时分行刑的习惯。那个时节，日落要到七点以后，所以一切都会进行得很顺当——对除钱德勒以外的所有人而言。对钱德勒，那将是终局。

## II / 二

===Original===

The odd thing about Chandler’s dilemma was not merely that he was innocent﻿—in a way, that is﻿—but that many who were guilty (in a way; as guilty as he himself, at any rate) were free and honored citizens. Chandler himself was a widower because his own wife had been murdered. He had seen the murderer leaving the scene of the crime, and the man he had seen was in the courtroom today, watching Chandler’s own trial. Of the six hundred or so in the court, at least fifty were known to have taken part in one or more provable acts of murder, rape, arson, theft, sodomy, vandalism, assault and battery or a dozen other offenses indictable under the laws of the state. Of course, that could be said of almost any community in the world in those years; Chandler’s was not unique. What had put Chandler in the dock was not what his body had been seen to do, but the place in which it had been seen to do it. For everybody knew that medicine and agriculture were never molested by the demons.

Chandler’s own lawyer had pointed that out to him the day before the trial. “If it was anywhere but at the McKelvey plant, all right, but there’s never been any trouble there. You know that. The trouble with you laymen is you think of lawyers in terms of Perry Mason, right? Rabbit out of the hat stuff. Well, I can’t do that. I can only present your case, whatever it is, the best way possible. And the best thing I can do for your case right now is tell you you haven’t got one.” At that time the lawyer was still trying to be fair. He was even casting around for some thought he could use to convince himself that his client was innocent, though he had frankly admitted as soon as he introduced himself that he didn’t have much hope there.

Chandler protested that he didn’t have to commit rape. He’d been a widower for a year, but﻿—

“Wait a minute,” said the lawyer. “Listen. You can’t make an ordinary claim of possession stick, but what about good old-fashioned insanity?” Chandler looked puzzled, so the lawyer explained. Wasn’t it possible that Chandler was﻿—consciously, subconsciously, unconsciously, call it what you will﻿—trying to get revenge for what had happened to his own wife?

No, said Chandler, certainly not! But then he had to stop and think. After all, he had never been possessed before; in fact, he had always retained a certain skepticism about “possession”﻿—it seemed like such a convenient way for anyone to do any illicit thing he chose﻿—until the moment when he looked up to see Peggy Flershem walking into the culture room with a tray of agar disks, and was astonished to find himself striking her with the wrench in his hand and ripping at her absurdly floral-printed slacks. Maybe his case was different. Maybe it wasn’t the sort of possession that struck at random; maybe he was just off his rocker.

===Chinese===

钱德勒这桩难局的离奇之处，不仅在于他是无辜的——在某种意义上无辜，也就是说——还在于许多有罪之人（也是在某种意义上；无论如何，与他本人同样地有罪）却是自由而受尊敬的公民。钱德勒本人是个鳏夫，因为他自己的妻子被人谋杀了。他亲眼看见凶手离开犯罪现场，而他看见的那个人，今天就在法庭上，旁观着钱德勒自己的审判。法庭里那六百来人当中，至少有五十个已知参与过一件或多件可加证明的谋杀、强奸、纵火、盗窃、鸡奸、毁坏公物、殴打伤害，或是其他十几种依本州法律可予起诉的罪行。当然，在那些年月，这话对世上几乎任何一个社区都成立；钱德勒这个镇并不特殊。把钱德勒推上被告席的，不是人们看见他这具身体干了什么，而是人们看见它在哪里干的。因为人人都知道，医药和农业从不受恶魔的骚扰。

早在审判前一天，钱德勒自己的律师就向他点明了这一点。“要是这事发生在麦克尔维工厂以外的任何地方，都好说，可那儿从来没出过乱子。这你是知道的。你们这些外行的毛病，就是把律师都想成佩里·梅森（Perry Mason），对吧？变戏法似的从帽子里掏出只兔子。可惜我变不出。我只能把你的案子——不管它是什么案子——以尽可能好的方式摆出来。而眼下我能为你的案子做的最好的事，就是告诉你：你根本就没有案子。”那时这位律师还在力求公允。他甚至在四处搜罗什么念头，好让自己相信当事人是无辜的——尽管初次见面他就坦率承认，在这方面他不抱多大指望。

钱德勒分辩说，他犯不着去强奸。他做鳏夫已经一年了，可是——

“等等，”律师说，“听着。寻常的附身申辩你站不住，可要是来一手老式的、正经的精神失常呢？”钱德勒露出困惑的神色，于是律师解释道：有没有可能，钱德勒——有意识地、下意识地、无意识地，随你怎么叫——是在为他自己妻子的遭遇复仇？

不，钱德勒说，当然不是！可他随即又不得不停下来想一想。毕竟，他从前从未被附过身；事实上，他对“附身”一说始终存着几分怀疑——它似乎太方便了，谁都可以借此去做自己选中的任何非法勾当——直到那一刻：他抬起头，看见佩吉·弗勒舍姆端着一托盘琼脂培养皿走进培养室，随即惊愕地发现，自己正用手中的扳手击打她，正在撕扯她那条花哨得荒唐的印花便裤。也许他这案子不一样。也许这不是那种随意乱附的附身；也许他只是脑子出了毛病。

===Original===

Margot, his wife, had been cut up cruelly. He had seen his friend, Jack Souther, leaving his home hurriedly as he approached; and although he had thought that the stains on his clothes looked queerly like blood, nothing in that prepared him for what he found in the rumpus room. It had taken him some time to identify the spread-out dissection on the floor with his wife Margot.﻿ ﻿… “No,” he told his lawyer, “I was shaken up, of course. The worst time was the next night, when there was a knock on the door and I opened it and it was Jack. He’d come to apologize. I﻿—fell apart; but I got over it. I tell you I was possessed, that’s all.”

“And I tell you that defense will put you right in front of a firing squad,” said his lawyer. “And *that’s* all.”

---

Five or six others had been executed for hoaxing; Chandler was familiar with the ritual. He even understood it, in a way. The world had gone to pot in the previous two years. The real enemy was out of reach; when any citizen might run wild and, when caught, relapse into his own self, terrified and sick, there was a need to strike back. But the enemy was invisible. The hoaxers were only whipping boys﻿—but they were the only targets vengeance had.

The real enemy had struck the entire world in a single night. One day the people of the world went about their business in the gloomy knowledge that they were likely to make mistakes but with, at least, the comfort that the mistakes would be their own. The next day had no such comfort. The next day anyone, anywhere, was likely to find himself seized, possessed, working evil or whimsy without intention and helplessly.

Chandler stood up, kicked the balled-up wax paper from his sandwiches across the floor and swore violently.

He was beginning to wake from the shock that had gripped him. “Damn fool,” he said to himself. He had no particular reason. Like the world, he needed a whipping boy too, if only himself. “Damn fool, you know they’re going to shoot you!”

He stretched and twisted his body violently, alone in the middle of the room, in silence. He *had* to wake up. He *had* to start thinking. In a quarter of an hour or less the court would reconvene, and from then it was only a steady, quick slide to the grave.

===Chinese===

玛戈，他的妻子，被残忍地肢解了。他走近自家时，看见他的朋友杰克·萨瑟（Jack Souther）正匆匆离开他的家；虽然他当时就觉得那人衣服上的污渍异样地像血，但那仍丝毫没能让他对娱乐室里的景象有半分准备。他花了些时间，才把地板上那摊开来的解剖物认作自己的妻子玛戈。……“不，”他对律师说，“我自然是垮了一阵子。最难熬的是第二天夜里，有人敲门，我开了门，是杰克。他是来道歉的。我——彻底垮了；不过我挺过来了。我告诉你，我当时是被附身的，仅此而已。”

“那我告诉你，这套答辩会把你径直送到行刑队面前。”他的律师说，“*就*这么多了。”

---

先前已有五六个人因行骗罪被处决；这套程式钱德勒是熟悉的。在某种意义上，他甚至能理解它。过去两年里，世界已经烂透了。真正的敌人够不着；任何一个公民都可能狂性大发，而一旦被抓住，又缩回他自身，吓得半死、恶心欲呕——人们需要还手。可敌人是无形的。骗子们不过是代人受过的替罪羊——但他们已是复仇仅有的靶子。

真正的敌人在一夜之间袭击了整个世界。头一天，世上的人们还在阴郁地各安其业，自知难免犯错，却至少还有一点安慰：那些过错将是他们自己的。第二天便再无这种安慰。第二天，任何地方的任何人都可能发现自己被攫住、被附身，无意而无可奈何地行起恶来，或是胡闹起来。

钱德勒站起来，把三明治揉成团的蜡纸一脚踢过地面，狠狠地咒骂了一声。

他正从攫住他的震惊中醒过来。“该死的蠢货。”他对自己说。他并没有什么特别的缘由。跟这个世界一样，他也需要一个替罪羊，哪怕就是他自己。“该死的蠢货，你知道他们要枪毙你的！”

他独自一人在房间中央，无声地剧烈伸展、扭动身体。他*必须*醒过来。他*必须*开始思考。一刻钟或更短的时间之后，法庭就要重新开庭，从那时起，便只剩一场平稳而迅速的滑坠，直落坟墓。

===Original===

It was better to do anything than to do nothing. He examined the windows of his improvised cell. They were above his head and barred; standing on the table, he could see feet walking outside, in the paved play-yard of the school. He discarded the thought of escaping that way; there was no one to smuggle him a file, and there was no time. He studied the door to the hall. It was not impossible that when the guard opened it he could jump him, knock him out, run﻿ ﻿… run where? The room had been a storage place for athletic equipment at the end of a hall; the hall led only to the stairs and the stairs emerged into the courtroom. It was quite likely, he thought, that the hall had another flight of stairs somewhere farther along, or through another room. What had he spent his taxes on these years, if not for schools designed with more than one exit in case of fire? But as he had not thought to mark an escape route when he was brought in, it did him no good.

The guard, however, had a gun. Chandler lifted up an edge of the table and tried to shake one of the legs. They did not shake; that part of his taxes had been well enough spent, he thought wryly. The chair? Could he smash the chair to get a club, which would give him a weapon to get the guard’s gun?﻿ ﻿…

Before he reached the chair the door opened and his lawyer came in.

“Sorry I’m late,” he said briskly. “Well. As your attorney I have to tell you they’ve presented a damaging case. As I see it﻿—”

“What case?” Chandler demanded. “I never denied the acts. What else did they prove?”

“Oh, God!” said his lawyer, not quite loudly enough to be insulting. “Do we have to go over that again? Your claim of possession would make a defense if it had happened anywhere else. We know that these cases exist, but we also know that they follow a pattern. Some areas seem to be immune﻿—medical establishments, pharmaceutical plants among them. So they proved that all this happened in a pharmaceutical plant. I advise you to plead guilty.”

===Chinese===

干点什么总比什么都不干强。他察看这间临时牢房的窗户。窗户高过他的头顶，装着铁栏；站到桌上，能看见外面有脚在走动，在学校铺了砖面的操场上。他从那个方向逃跑的念头打消了；没有人能给他偷带锉刀，也没有时间。他研究通往走廊的那扇门。等看守开门的时候扑上去、把他打昏、然后跑——倒也不是不可能……跑去哪里呢？这房间原先是走廊尽头存放体育器材的地方；走廊只通向楼梯，而楼梯口就开在法庭里。他很可以设想，走廊更远的某处还有另一道楼梯，或者穿过另一个房间。这些年的税钱要是没花在“为防火设计多个出口的学校”上，又花到哪儿去了？可是他被押进来时没想着记下一条逃跑路线，这对他毫无用处。

不过，看守有枪。钱德勒掀起桌子的一角，试着晃一晃桌腿。桌腿纹丝不动；税钱的这一部分倒是花得不冤，他苦涩地想。那么椅子呢？能不能砸碎椅子，弄一根棍棒，好有个武器去夺看守的枪？……

他还没走到椅子跟前，门开了，他的律师走了进来。

“抱歉来迟了。”他轻快地说，“嗯。作为你的律师，我必须告诉你，他们提出了一个极具杀伤力的案子。依我看——”

“什么案子？”钱德勒质问，“我从来没有否认那些行为。他们还证明了什么？”

“哦，上帝！”他的律师说，音量控制得恰好不至于构成侮辱。“咱们还得再过一遍这个吗？你那套附身的说法，要是发生在别的任何地方，倒还构成一条辩护理由。我们知道这类案子确实存在，可我们也知道它们有个规律。某些区域似乎是免疫的——医疗卫生机构、制药厂都在其列。于是他们证明了这一切都发生在一家制药厂里。我劝你认罪。”

===Original===

Chandler sat down on the edge of the table, controlling himself very well, he thought. He only asked: “Would that do me any good at all?”

The lawyer reflected, gazing at the ceiling. “… No. I guess it wouldn’t.”

Chandler nodded. “So what else shall we talk about? Want to compare notes about where you were and I was the night the President went possessed?”

The lawyer was irritated. He kept his mouth shut for a moment until he thought he could keep from showing it. Outside a vendor was hawking amulets: “St. Ann beads! Witch knots! Fresh garlic, local grown, best in town!” The lawyer shook his head.

“All right,” he said, “it’s your life. We’ll do it your way. Anyway, time’s up; Sergeant Grantz will be banging on the door any minute.”

He zipped up his briefcase. Chandler did not move. “They don’t give us much time anyway,” the lawyer added, angry at Chandler and at hoaxers in general but not willing to say so. “Grantz is a stickler for promptness.”

Chandler found a crumb of cheese by his hand and absently ate it. The lawyer watched him and glanced at his watch. “Oh, hell,” he said, picked up his briefcase and kicked the base of the door. “Grantz! What’s the matter with you? You asleep out there?”

===Chinese===

钱德勒在桌沿上坐下，自觉得克制得很好。他只问了一句：“那对我能有一点好处吗？”

律师望着天花板，思忖片刻。“……不。我想没有。”

钱德勒点点头。“那我们还有什么可谈的？想不想对对笔记，看看总统被附身那晚你在哪儿、我在哪儿？”

律师被激恼了。他闭嘴憋了片刻，直到觉得自己能不露在脸上。外面有个小贩在叫卖护身符：“圣安娜念珠！巫结！新鲜大蒜，本地种植，全城最好！”律师摇了摇头。

“好吧，”他说，“命是你自己的。就按你的法子办。反正时间到了；格兰茨中士（Sergeant Grantz）随时会来砸门。”

他拉上了公文包的拉链。钱德勒一动不动。“反正他们给我们的时间也不多。”律师又补了一句，他对钱德勒、对骗子们这一类人都憋着火，却不愿说破，“格兰茨是个分秒必究的人。”

钱德勒在手边发现一粒奶酪渣，心不在焉地吃了下去。律师看着他，瞥了一眼手表。“哦，见鬼。”他说着拿起公文包，一脚踢在门板上。“格兰茨！你怎么回事？在外面睡着了吗？”

===Original===

---

Chandler was sworn, gave his name, admitted the truth of everything the previous witnesses had said. The faces were still aimed at him, every one. He could not read them at all any more, could not tell if they were friendly or hating, there were too many and they all had eyes. The jurors sat on their funeral-parlor chairs like cadavers, embalmed and propped, the dead witnessing a wake for the living. Only the forewoman in the funny hat showed signs of life, looking alertly at Chandler, at the judge, at the man next to her, around the auditorium. Maybe it was a good sign. At least she did not have the frozen in concrete, guilty-as-hell look of the others.

His attorney asked him the question he had been waiting for: “Tell us, in your own words, what happened.” Chandler opened his mouth, and paused. Curiously, he had forgotten what he wanted to say. He had rehearsed this moment again and again; but all that came out was:

“I didn’t do it. I mean, I did the acts, but I was possessed. That’s all. Others have done worse, under the same circumstances, and been let off. Just as Fisher was acquitted for murdering the Learnards, as Draper got off after what he did to the Cline boy. As Jack Souther over there was let off after he murdered my own wife. They should be. They couldn’t help themselves. Whatever this thing is that takes control, I know it can’t be fought. My God, you can’t even *try* to fight it!”

He was not getting through. The faces had not changed. The forewoman of the jury was now searching systematically through her pocketbook, taking each item out and examining it, putting it back and taking out another. But between times she looked at him and at least her expression wasn’t hostile. He said, addressing her:

“That’s all there is to it. It wasn’t me running my body. It was someone else. I swear it before all of you, and before God.”

===Chinese===

---

钱德勒宣誓、报上姓名、承认此前各位证人所说句句属实。那些面孔依然一张张都对准着他。他全然读不出它们的含义了，分不清是友善还是憎恨——太多了，而且每一张都有眼睛。陪审员们坐在灵堂用的椅子上，像一具具尸体，经防腐处理后被撑立起来，死者为生者守灵。只有那位戴滑稽帽子的陪审团团长显出生命的迹象，机警地看看钱德勒，看看法官，看看邻座的男人，环顾整个礼堂。也许这是个好兆头。至少她没有别的人那种混凝土般凝固、罪孽深重的神情。

他的律师问出了他一直等着的问题：“告诉我们，用你自己的话，当时发生了什么。”钱德勒张开嘴，又停住了。奇怪的是，他忘了自己想说什么。这一刻他已经排练过一遍又一遍；可说出口的只有：

“不是我干的。我是说，行为是我干的，可我当时被附身了。就是这么回事。别人在同样的情形下干了更糟的事，都被放过了。就像费希尔（Fisher）谋杀利纳德一家获判无罪，就像德雷珀（Draper）对克莱因家那个男孩干下那档子事后安然脱身。就像那边那个杰克·萨瑟，谋杀了我自己的妻子之后被放过一样。他们本该如此。他们自己也没办法。不管这个夺人控制权的东西是什么，我知道它是没法对抗的。我的天，你连*试一试*对抗都做不到！”

他的话没有传进任何人心里。那些面孔毫无变化。陪审团团长此刻正在她的手提包里系统地搜寻，把每样东西掏出来端详，放回去，再掏出另一样。不过间隙里她仍望向他，至少她的表情不是怀有敌意的。他说道，冲着她说：

“事情的全部就是如此。操纵我身体的不是我。是别的什么人。我在你们全体面前发誓，在上帝面前发誓。”

===Original===

The prosecutor did not bother to question him.

Chandler went back to his seat and sat down and watched the next twenty minutes go by in the wink of an eye, rapid, rapid, they were in a hurry to shoot him. He could hardly believe that Judge Ellithorp could speak so fast, the jurymen rise and file out at a gallop, zip, whisk, and they were back again. Too fast! he cried silently, time had gone into high gear; but he knew that it was only his imagination. The twenty minutes had been a full twelve hundred seconds. And then time, as if to make amends, came to a stop, abrupt, brakes-on. The judge asked the jury for their verdict and it was an eternity before the forewoman arose.

She was beginning to look rather disheveled. Beaming at Chandler﻿—*surely* the woman was rather odd, it couldn’t be just his imagination﻿—she fumbled in her pocketbook for the slip of paper with the verdict. But she wore an expression of suppressed laughter.

“I *knew* I had it,” she cried triumphantly and waved the slip above her head. “Now, let’s see.” She held it before her eyes and squinted. “Oh, yes. Judge, we the jury, and so forth and so on﻿—”

She paused to wink at Judge Ellithorp. An uncertain worried murmur welled up in the auditorium. “All that junk, Judge,” she explained, “anyway, we unanimously﻿—but *unanimously*, love!﻿—find this son of a bitch innocent. Why,” she giggled, “we think he ought to get a medal, you know? I tell you what you do, love, you go right over and give him a big wet kiss and say you’re sorry.” She kept on talking, but no one heard. The murmur became a mass scream.

“Stop, stop her!” bawled the judge, dropping his glasses. “Bailiff!”

The scream became a word, in many voices chorused: *Possessed!* And beyond doubt the woman was. The men around her hurled themselves away, as from leprosy among them, and then washed back like a lynch mob. She was giggling as they fell on her. “Got a cigarette? No cigarettes in this lousy bag﻿—oh.” She screamed as they touched her, went limp and screamed again.

It was a different note this time, pure hysteria: “I couldn’t *stop*. Oh, *God*.”

---

===Chinese===

检察官懒得对他发问。

钱德勒回到座位坐下，眼看着接下来的二十分钟眨眼间就过去了，飞快，飞快，它们急着要枪毙他。他几乎不敢相信埃利索普法官能说得这么快，陪审员们能像疾驰一样起立鱼贯而出，嗖，唰，他们又回来了。太快了！他无声地喊道，时间挂上了高速挡；可他知道那只是他的错觉。那二十分钟足足是一千二百秒。随后时间仿佛要作出补偿，猛地刹住，戛然而止。法官向陪审团征询裁决，陪审团团长起身之前，已隔了一个永恒。

她的样子开始显得相当凌乱。她朝钱德勒眉开眼笑——这女人八成是有点古怪，总不能全怪他的错觉——在手提包里摸索那张写着裁决的纸条。可她脸上带着一种强忍笑意的表情。

“我*就知道*带在身上。”她得意地喊道，把纸条举过头顶挥了挥。“来，让我瞧瞧。”她把纸条举到眼前，眯起眼睛。“哦，对了。法官先生，我们陪审团，如此如此，这般这般——”

她顿了顿，朝埃利索普法官挤了挤眼。礼堂里涌起一阵迟疑不安的低语。“那些套话就免了吧，法官。”她解释道，“反正呢，我们全体一致——可是*全体一致*哟，亲爱的！——裁定这个狗娘养的无罪。要不，”她咯咯笑着说，“我们觉得他还该得一枚勋章，知道吗？我告诉你该怎么办，亲爱的，你这就走过去，给他一个湿乎乎的大大的吻，说声对不起。”她还要说下去，可没有人在听了。低语汇成了一片尖叫。

“拦住，拦住她！”法官吼道，眼镜都跌落了。“法警！”

尖叫变成了一个词，众口齐声合唱：*被附身了！*而毫无疑问，这女人确实被附身了。她周围的男人们猛地闪开，仿佛人群里发现了麻风病人，随即又像私刑暴民一样涌了回去。他们扑向她时，她还在咯咯地笑。“有烟吗？这破包里一支烟都没有——哦。”他们碰到她时她尖叫起来，身子一软，又尖叫起来。

这回是另一种调子了，纯粹的歇斯底里：“我*停不下来*。哦，*上帝*。”

---

===Original===

Chandler caught his lawyer by the arm and jerked him away from staring at the scene. All of a sudden he was alive again. “You, damn it. Listen! The jury acquitted me, right?”

The lawyer was startled. “Don’t be ridiculous. It’s a clear case of﻿—”

“Be a lawyer, man! You live on technicalities, don’t you? Make this one work for me!”

The attorney gave him a queer, thoughtful look, hesitated, shrugged and got to his feet. He had to shout to be heard. “Your honor! I take it my client is free to go.”

He made almost as much of a stir as the sobbing woman, but he outshouted the storm. “The jury’s verdict is on record. Granted there was an *apparent* case of possession. Nevertheless﻿—”

Judge Ellithorp yelled back: “No nonsense, you! Listen to me, young man﻿—”

===Chinese===

钱德勒抓住律师的胳膊，把他从盯着的现场猛地拽开。突然之间，他又活了过来。“你，见鬼。听着！陪审团判我无罪了，对吧？”

律师吓了一跳。“别犯傻。这是一桩明明白白的——”

“拿出律师的样子来，伙计！你就是靠程序上的条条框框吃饭的，不是吗？让这一条为我所用！”

律师古怪而若有所思地看了他一眼，迟疑了一下，耸耸肩，站起身。他得喊出来才能让人听见。“法官大人！我是否可以认为，我的当事人已获自由，可以离开了。”

他引起的骚动几乎不亚于那个啼哭的女人，但他压过了风暴般的喧哗。“陪审团的裁决已经记录在案。姑且承认存在一桩*表面上的*附身事例。然而——”

埃利索普法官回吼道：“少胡闹，你！听我说，年轻人——”

===Original===

The lawyer snapped, “Permission to approach the bench.”

“Granted.”

Chandler sat unable to move, watching the brief, stormy conference. It was painful to be coming back to life. It was agony to hope. At least, he thought detachedly, his lawyer was fighting for him; the prosecutor’s face was a thundercloud.

The lawyer came back, with the expression of a man who has won a victory he did not expect, and did not want. “Your last chance, Chandler. Change your plea to guilty.”

“But﻿—”

“Don’t push your luck, boy! The judge has agreed to accept a plea. They’ll throw you out of town, of course. But you’ll be alive.” Chandler hesitated. “Make up your mind! The best I can do otherwise is a mistrial, and that means you’ll get convicted by another jury next week.”

Chandler said, testing his luck: “You’re sure they’ll keep their end of the bargain?”

===Chinese===

律师厉声道：“请求走近法官席。”

“准许。”

钱德勒坐在那里动弹不得，望着那场短促而激烈的法官席会谈。重新活过来是痛苦的。怀抱希望是煎熬。至少，他冷然想道，他的律师在为他争；检察官的脸已经阴云密布。

律师回来了，带着一副打赢了一场他既不曾料到、也不想要的胜仗的神情。“你最后的机会，钱德勒。把答辩改成有罪。”

“可是——”

“别得寸进尺，小子！法官已经同意接受一项认罪答辩。他们会把你逐出本镇，这自然免不了。但你能活着。”钱德勒犹豫着。“快拿主意！不然我最多争取到无效审判，那意味着下星期你会被另一个陪审团定罪。”

钱德勒说，仍在试探运气：“你肯定他们会守约？”

===Original===

The lawyer shook his head, his expression that of a man who smells something unpleasant. “Your honor! I ask you to discharge the jury. My client wishes to change his plea.”

… In the school’s chemistry lab, an hour later, Chandler discovered that the lawyer had left out one little detail. Outside there was a sound of motors idling, the police car that would dump him at the town’s limits; inside was a thin, hollow hiss. It was the sound of a Bunsen burner, and in its blue flame a crudely shaped iron changed slowly from cherry to orange to glowing straw. It had the shape of a letter *h*.

*H* for “hoaxer.” The mark they were about to put on his forehead would be with him wherever he went and as long as he lived, which would probably not be long. *H* for “hoaxer,” so that a glance would show that he had been convicted of the worst offense of all.

No one spoke to him as the sheriff’s man took the iron out of the fire, but three husky policemen held his arms while he screamed.

===Chinese===

律师摇了摇头，神情像闻到了什么臭味。“法官大人！请求解散陪审团。我的当事人愿意更改答辩。”

……一小时后，在学校的化学实验室里，钱德勒发现律师漏掉了一个小小的细节。外面是引擎怠速的声音，那辆要把他丢到镇界的警车；里面是一声细而空的嘶嘶。那是本生灯的声音，在它的蓝色火焰里，一块粗制成形的烙铁正缓缓从樱桃红变成橙色，再变成灼亮的麦秆色。它是一个字母*h*的形状。

*H*，代表“骗子”。他们就要烙在他额头上的这个印记，将伴随他走遍任何地方、活到命尽头——而那大概不会太长。*H*，代表“骗子”，让人一眼就看出，他被定的是万罪之首。

治安官的手下把烙铁从火中取出时，没有一个人对他说话，只有三名魁梧的警察架住他的胳膊，任他尖叫。

## III / 三

===Original===

The pain was still burning when Chandler awoke the next day. He wished he had a bandage, but he didn't, and that was that.

He was in a freight car—had hopped it on the run at the yards, daring to sneak back into town long enough for that. He could not hope to hitchhike, with that mark on him. Anyway, hitchhiking was an invitation to trouble.

The railroads were safer—far safer than either cars or air transport, notoriously a lightning-rod attracting possession. Chandler was surprised when the train came crashing to a stop, each freight car smashing against the couplings of the one ahead, the engine jolting forward and stopping again.

Then there was silence. It endured.

Chandler, who had been slowly waking after a night of very little sleep, sat up against the wall of the boxcar and wondered what was wrong.

It seemed remiss to start a day without signing the Cross or hearing a few exorcismal verses. It seemed to be midmorning, time for work to be beginning at the plant. The lab men would be streaming in, their amulets examined at the door. The chaplains would be wandering about, ready to pray a possessing spirit out. Chandler, who kept an open mind, had considerable doubt of the effectiveness of all the amulets and spells—certainly they had not kept him from a brutal rape—but he felt uneasy without them. … The train still was not moving. In the silence he could hear the distant huffing of the engine.

===Chinese===

第二天钱德勒醒来时，疼痛仍在灼烧。他很想有条绷带，可他没有，事情也就只能这样了。

他躺在一节货车里——是在货场跑动着扒上去的，为这一下他斗胆溜回镇里，待了那么一小会儿。额上带着那个印记，搭便车是想也别想了。再说，搭便车本身就是招灾的请帖。

铁路要安全些——比汽车和航空运输都安全得多，后两者是出了名的招引附身的避雷针。列车哐当一声急停时，钱德勒吃了一惊：每节货车都朝前一节的挂钩上撞过去，机车向前一颠，又停住了。

随后便是寂静。寂静久久不散。

睡了一夜少得可怜的觉、正慢慢醒转的钱德勒靠着棚车壁坐起来，纳闷出了什么事。

一天开始却不画个十字、不听上几句驱邪经文，似乎总欠着点什么。看时间已是上午过半，正是厂里开工的时辰。实验室的人们正鱼贯而入，护身符要在门口挨个查验。驻厂牧师们会四处走动，随时准备把附身的邪灵祷告出去。钱德勒这人头脑开放，对满场护身符和咒语的效力素来疑心不小——它们当然没能拦住他去干那场野蛮的强奸——可没有它们，他心里又不踏实。……列车仍旧一动不动。寂静里，他听得见远处机车粗重的喷气声。

===Original===

He went to the door, supporting himself with one hand on the wooden wall, and looked out.

The tracks followed the roll of a river, their bed a few feet higher than an empty three-lane highway, which in turn was a dozen feet above the water. As he looked out the engine brayed twice. The train jolted uncertainly, then stopped again.

Then there was a very long time when nothing happened at all.

From Chandler's car he could not see the engine. He was on the convex of the curve, and the other door of the car was sealed. He did not need to see it to know that something was wrong. There should have been a brakeman running with a flare to ward off other trains; but there was not. There should have been a station, or at least a water tank, to account for the stop in the first place. There was not. Something had gone wrong, and Chandler knew what it was. Not the details, but the central fact that lay behind this and behind almost everything that went wrong these days.

The engineer was possessed. It had to be that.

Yet it was odd, he thought, as odd as his own trouble. He had chosen this car with care. It contained eight refrigerator cars full of pharmaceuticals, and if anything was known about the laws governing possession, as his lawyer had told him, it was that such things were almost never interfered with.

===Chinese===

他一手撑着木质车壁，挪到车门边，朝外望去。

铁轨顺着一条河的蜿蜒伸展，路基比一条空荡荡的三车道公路高出几英尺，公路又比水面高出十来英尺。他朝外张望的当儿，机车嘶吼了两声。列车犹疑地一晃，随即又停住了。

接着是好长一段时间，什么动静也没有。

从钱德勒所在的车厢，他望不见机车。他这边是弯道外凸的一侧，车厢另一头的门又封着。可不必看见他也知道出了事。本该有个制动员举着信号焰火奔跑，拦阻别的列车；却没有。本该有一座车站，至少也该有个水塔，好解释这趟停车；却没有。出岔子了，而钱德勒知道岔在哪里。不是细节，而是藏在这一切背后、也藏在近来几乎每一件出岔子的事情背后的那个核心事实。

机车司机被附身了。准是这个。

然而这很古怪，他想，古怪得就像他自己的麻烦一样。他挑选这列车是费过心思的。车上装着八节满载药品的冷藏车厢，而关于支配附身的法则，如果说有什么是人所共知的——正如他的律师告诉他的——那就是：这类东西几乎从不受到搅扰。

===Original===

Chandler jumped down to the roadbed, slipped on the crushed rock and almost fell. He had forgotten the wound on his forehead. He clutched the sill of the car door, where an ankh and fleur-de-lis had been chalked to ward off demons, until the sudden rush of blood subsided and the pain began to relent. After a moment he walked gingerly to the end of the car, slipped between the cars, dodged the couplers and climbed the ladder to its roof.

It was a warm, bright, silent day. Nothing moved. From his height he could see the Diesel at the front of the train and the caboose at its rear. No people. The train was halted a quarter-mile from where the tracks swooped across the river on a suspension bridge. Away from the river, the side of the tracks that had been hidden from him before, was an uneven rock cut and, above it, the slope of a mountain.

By looking carefully he could spot the signs of a number of homes within half a mile or so—the corner of a roof, a glassed-in porch built to command a river view, a twenty-foot television antenna poking through the trees. There was also the curve of a higher road along which the homes were strung.

Chandler took thought. He was alive and free, two gifts more gracious than he had had any right to expect. However, he would need food and he would need at least some sort of bandage for his forehead. He had a wool cap, stolen from the high school, which would hide the mark, though what it would do to the burn on his skin was something else again.

Chandler climbed down the ladder. With considerable pain he gentled the cap over the great raw H on his forehead and began to climb the mountain.

===Chinese===

钱德勒跳上路基，踩在碎石上打了个滑，险些摔倒。他忘了自己额头上的伤。他一把抓住车厢门槛——那儿用粉笔画着一个安卡十字和一朵鸢尾花，驱邪用的——直到蓦地涌出的血渐渐止住，疼痛开始缓和。过了一会儿，他小心翼翼地挪到车厢端头，钻进车厢之间，避开挂钩，顺着爬梯上了车顶。

那天温暖、明亮、寂静。什么都不动弹。站在这个高度，他看得见列车前端的柴油机车，也看得见列车尾部的守车。没有人影。列车停在离铁轨俯冲过河的那座吊桥四分之一英里的地方。背离河的一侧——先前他望不见的那一边——是一道高低不平的岩石路堑，堑的上方是一座山的斜坡。

细细察看，他能辨认出半英里上下有几户人家的迹象——一角屋顶，一座专为眺望河景加盖的玻璃暖廊，一根从树梢里戳出来的二十英尺电视天线。还有一条更高处的路蜿蜒绕行，那些人家便沿线错落分布。

钱德勒打起了算盘。他活着，而且自由，这两样恩赐，比他有资格指望的还多。可他需要吃的，额头至少也得弄点什么包一包。他有一顶羊毛帽，从中学里偷来的，能把印记遮住；至于它压在那块烧伤的皮肤上会怎么样，那就是另一回事了。

钱德勒爬下梯子。忍着不小的痛，他把帽子轻轻罩上额头那个血肉模糊的大 H，开始爬山。

---

===Original===

He knocked on the first door he came to, a great old three-story house with well tended gardens.

There was a wait. The air smelled warmly of honeysuckle and mown grass, with wild onions chopped down by the blades of the mower. It was pleasant, or would have been in happier times. He knocked again, peremptorily, and the door was opened at once. Evidently someone had been right inside, listening.

A man stared at him. "Stranger, what do you want?" He was short, plump, with an extremely thick and unkempt beard. It did not appear to have been grown for its own sake, for where the facial hair could not be coaxed to grow his skin had the gross pits of old acne.

Chandler said glibly: "Good morning. I'm working my way east. I need something to eat and I'm willing to work for it."

The man withdrew, leaving the upper half of the Dutch door open. As it looked in on only a vestibule it did not tell Chandler much. There was one curious thing—a lath and cardboard sign, shaped like an arc of a rainbow, lettered:

> Welcome to Orphalese

===Chinese===

他敲了敲碰上的头一扇门：一栋气派的旧三层楼房，园圃侍弄得很好。

敲门之后是一阵等待。空气里暖融融地漾着忍冬和刈过的青草香，草里还杂着被割草机刀片斩断的野葱气味。很宜人——或者说，在更快乐的年月里本该宜人。他又敲了一遍，不由分说，门立时开了。显然有人就在门后，一直听着。

一个男人瞪眼打量他。"生人，你要什么？"他矮而胖，蓄着一把浓得出奇、从不打理的大胡子。那胡子不像专意为着长它才留的：凡胡须哄不出来的地方，皮肤上便尽是旧粉刺留下的粗大麻坑。

钱德勒油嘴滑舌地说："早上好。我一路做工往东去。我需要点吃的，愿意干活来换。"

那人退了进去，留下荷兰门的上半扇敞着。从那半扇望进去只有一条门厅，钱德勒看不出多少名堂。只有一样东西古怪——一块板条加纸板做的招牌，弯成一道彩虹的弧形，上写着：

> 欢迎来到奥法利斯（Orphalese）

===Original===

He puzzled over it and dismissed it. The entrance room, apart from the sign, had a knickknack shelf of Japanese carved ivory and an old-fashioned umbrella rack, but that added nothing to his knowledge. He had already guessed that the owners of this home were well off. Also it had been recently painted; so they were not demoralized, as so much of the world had been demoralized, by the coming of the possessors. Even the elaborate sculpturing of its hedges had been maintained.

The man came back and with him was a girl of fifteen or so. She was tall, slim and rather homely, with a large jaw and an oval face. "Guy, he's not much to look at," she said to the pockmarked man. "Meggie, shall I let him in?" he asked. "Guy, you might as well," she shrugged, staring at Chandler with interest but not sympathy.

"Stranger, come along," said the man named Guy, and led him through a short hall into an enormous living room, a room two stories high with a ten-foot fireplace.

Chandler's first thought was that he had stumbled in upon a wake. The room was neatly laid out in rows of folding chairs, more than half of them occupied. He entered from the side, but all the occupants of the chairs were looking toward him. He returned their stares; he had had a good deal of practice lately in looking back at staring faces, he reflected. "Stranger, go on," said the man who had let him in, nudging him, "and meet the people of Orphalese."

Chandler hardly heard him. He had not expected anything like this. It was a meeting, a Daumier caricature of a Thursday Afternoon Literary Circle, old men with faces like moons, young women with faces like hags. They were strained, haggard and fearful, and a surprising number of them showed some sort of physical defect, a bandaged leg, an arm in a sling or merely the marks of pain on the features. "Stranger, go in," repeated the man, and it was only then that Chandler noticed the man was holding a pistol, pointed at his head.

===Chinese===

他对着招牌琢磨了一阵，随后撇在一边。门厅里除了这块招牌，还有一个陈列日本牙雕小件的小摆设架和一座老式伞架，但这添不了他的见识。他早猜到这家主人手头宽裕。房子又新近粉刷过；可见附身者降临之后，他们并没有像世上那么多人那样颓丧潦倒。连树篱上那些精巧的剪扎造型也一直有人照管。

那人回来了，身后跟着一个十五岁上下的姑娘。她高挑、清瘦，长相相当平平，下颚宽大，一张椭圆脸。"盖伊（Guy），他瞧着可不怎么样，"她对那麻脸男人说。"梅吉（Meggie），那我放他进来？"他问。"盖伊，放呗，"她耸耸肩，盯着钱德勒看，眼里有兴趣，却没有同情。

"生人，跟我来。"名叫盖伊的男人说，领他穿过短短一道过道，走进一间大得吓人的客厅——层高贯通两楼，壁炉宽十英尺。

钱德勒的头一个念头是：他误闯进了一场守灵。屋里齐齐整整排着一行行折叠椅，坐了满满一多半。他从侧面进来，可椅子上的所有人都在朝他望。他也回望他们；他想，近来他回望盯人脸孔的功夫可是练了不少。"生人，往里走，"放他进来的男人用胳膊肘捣了他一下，"去见见奥法利斯的民众。"

钱德勒几乎没听进去。他没料到会是这般光景。这是一场集会，一幅杜米埃笔下的"星期四下午文学社"漫画：老头们一张张月亮脸，年轻女人们一张张老巫婆脸。他们绷紧、憔悴、惶惶然，其中露出某种身体残损的多得惊人——一条缠着绷带的腿，一只吊在悬带里的胳膊，或者仅仅是眉眼间刻着的痛楚。"生人，往里去，"那人又说了一遍。直到这时，钱德勒才注意到那人手里握着一把手枪，正指着他的脑袋。

---

===Original===

Chandler sat in the rear of the room, watching. There must be thousands of little colonies like this, he reflected; with the breakdown of long-distance communication the world had been atomized. There was a real fear, well justified, of living in large groups, for they too were lightning rods for possession. The world was stumbling along, but it was lame in all its members; a planetary lobotomy had stolen from it its wisdom and plan. If, he reflected dryly, it had ever had any.

But of course things were better in the old days. The world had seemed on the brink of blowing itself up, but at least it was by its own hand. Then came Christmas.

It had happened at Christmas, and the first sign was on nationwide television. The old President, balding, grave and plump, was making a special address to the nation, urging good will to men and, please, artificial trees because of the fire danger in the event of H-bomb raids; in the middle of a sentence twenty million viewers had seen him stop, look dazedly around and say, in a breathless mumble, what sounded like: "Disht dvornyet ilgt." He had then picked up the Bible on the desk before him and thrown it at the television camera.

The last the televiewers had seen was the fluttering pages of the Book, growing larger as it crashed against the lens, then a flicker and a blinding shot of the studio lights as the cameraman jumped away and the instrument swiveled to stare mindlessly upward. Twenty minutes later the President was dead, as his Secretary of Health and Welfare, hurrying with him back to the White House, calmly took a hand grenade from a Marine guard at the gate and blew the President's party to fragments.

For the President's seizure was only the first and most conspicuous. "Disht dvornyet ilgt." C.I.A. specialists were playing the tapes of the broadcast feverishly, electronically cleaning the mumble and stir from the studio away from the words to try to learn, first, the language and second what the devil it meant; but the President who ordered it was dead before the first reel spun, and his successor was not quite sworn in when it became his time to die. The ceremony was interrupted for an emergency call from the War Room, where a very nearly hysterical four-star general was trying to explain why he had ordered the immediate firing of every live missile in his command against Washington, DC.

Over five hundred missiles were involved. In most of the sites the order was disobeyed, but in six of them, unfortunately, unquestioning discipline won out, thus ending not only the swearing in, the general's weeping explanation, the spinning of tapes, but also some two million lives in the District of Columbia, Maryland, Virginia and (through malfunctioning relays on two missiles) Pennsylvania and Vermont. But it was only the beginning.

===Chinese===

钱德勒坐在屋子后排，看着。世上这样的小聚落必定有成千上万，他想；远程通讯一垮，世界便被打散成了原子。人们怕住在大的人群里，这惧怕实实在在，也大有道理，因为大人群同样是招引附身的避雷针。世界跌跌撞撞地往前蹭，可它浑身上下没有一处不瘸；一场行星级的前脑叶白质切除术，把它的智慧和它的规划偷走了。倘若——他干巴巴地想——它本来还有过的话。

当然，从前要好得多。那时节世界看着已经到了把自己炸上天去的边缘，但至少那是出自它自己的手。接着，圣诞节来了。

那件事就出在圣诞节，头一个征兆上了全国电视。那位年迈的总统，头顶渐秃、仪态庄重、体态丰腴，正在向全国发表特别广播，劝人对人和善，并且——拜托——请用人工圣诞树，以防氢弹空袭时闹火灾；一句话说到半截，两千万观众眼看着他停住，晕晕乎乎地四下张望，然后气喘吁吁地咕哝了一句什么，听上去像是："迪什特·德沃尔涅特·伊尔格特。"接着他抓起面前桌上那本《圣经》，朝电视摄像机掷去。

电视观众看到的最后画面，是那部圣典扑扇着的书页，撞向镜头时越来越大；然后画面一闪，演播室的灯光刺目地扫过——摄像师跳开了，机器呆头呆脑地转上去，直愣愣仰视天顶。二十分钟后总统死了：他的卫生教育福利部长正陪他赶回白宫，在大门口不慌不忙地从一个海军陆战队卫兵手里接过一颗手榴弹，把总统一行人炸成了碎块。

因为总统的发作只是头一例，也是最显眼的一例。"迪什特·德沃尔涅特·伊尔格特。"中央情报局（C.I.A.）的专家们发疯般地回放广播录音，用电子手段把演播室里的咕哝与响动从字句上滤除，想先辨出那是什么语言，再辨出它究竟是个什么鬼意思；可下这道令的总统，第一盘带子还没转起来就死了，他的继任者宣誓就职还没礼成，就轮到了他自己的死期。仪式被作战室的一通紧急来电打断，那边一位近乎歇斯底里的四星上将，正在竭力解释他为什么下令把麾下每一枚实弹导弹立即射向华盛顿特区。

涉案的导弹有五百多枚。大多数阵地上这道命令被拒不执行，可不幸的是，有六处阵地上，不问情由的服从纪律占了上风，于是就此告终的不只是那场宣誓、那位将军涕泪交流的解释、磁带的转动，还有哥伦比亚特区、马里兰州、弗吉尼亚州（外加因两枚导弹继电器失灵而殃及的宾夕法尼亚州和佛蒙特州）境内约两百万条性命。然而，这仅仅是开场。

===Original===

These were the first cases of possession seen by the world in some five hundred years, since the great casting out of devils of the Middle Ages. A thousand more occurred in the next few days, a hundred in the next hours. The timetable was made up out of scattered reports in the wire-service newsrooms, while they still had facilities for spot coverage in any part of the world. (That lasted almost a week.) They identified 237 cases of possession by noon of the next day. Disregarding the dubious items—the Yankee pitcher who leaped from the Manhattan bridge (he had Bright's disease), the warden of San Quentin who seated himself in the gas chamber and, literally, kicked the bucket (did he know the Grand Jury was subpoenaing his books?)—disregarding these, the chronology of major cases that evening was:

8:27 p.m. EST: President has attack on television.

8:28 p.m. EST: Prime Minister of England orders bombing raid against Israel, alleging secret plot (order not carried out).

8:28 p.m. EST: Captain of S.S.N. Ethan Allen, surfaced near Montauk Point, orders crash dive and course change, proceeding submerged at flank speed to New York Harbor.

9:10 p.m. EST: Eastern Airlines six-engine jet makes wheels-up landing on roof of Pentagon, breaking some 1500 windows but causing no other major damage (except to the people aboard the jet); record of this incident fragmentary because entire site charred black in fusion attack two hours later.

9:23 p.m. EST: Rosalie Pan, musical-comedy star, jumps off stage, runs up center aisle and vanishes in cab, wearing beaded bra, G-string and $2500 headdress. Her movements are traced to Newark airport where she boards T.W.A. jetliner, which is never seen again.

===Chinese===

这是世人约五百年来头一回见到的附身，自从中世纪那场大规模驱魔以来。接下来几天里又发生了一千例，随后几个钟头里又有一百例。这张时序表，是通讯社新闻编辑室趁各地还保有现场报道手段的时候，用零散的报道拼凑出来的。（这光景持续了将近一个星期。）到第二天正午，他们已确认了237例附身。撇开那些可疑的项目不谈——那个从曼哈顿大桥上跳下去的洋基队投手（他患的是布赖特氏病），那个坐进毒气室、不折不扣地"踢了桶"（字面意思）一命呜呼的圣昆廷监狱长（他知不知道大陪审团正在传讯他的账簿？）——撇开这些不谈，当晚各要案的时序如下：

东部标准时间晚8:27：总统在电视直播中发作。

东部标准时间晚8:28：英国首相下令空袭以色列，声称破获秘密阴谋（命令未获执行）。

东部标准时间晚8:28："伊桑·艾伦"号核潜艇（S.S.N. Ethan Allen）在蒙托克角附近浮航，艇长下令紧急下潜、变更航向，以全速潜航驶往纽约港。

东部标准时间晚9:10：东方航空一架六发动机喷气客机收轮机腹迫降五角大楼楼顶，击碎约1500扇窗，此外未造成其他重大损失（机上人员除外）；本事件记录残缺，因为两小时后整个地点在聚变攻击中被烧成焦黑。

东部标准时间晚9:23：音乐喜剧明星罗莎莉·潘（Rosalie Pan）跃下舞台，沿中央过道奔出，钻进一辆出租汽车，不见了踪影，身上只穿串珠乳罩和丁字裤，头戴一顶值2500美元的头饰。她的行迹被追到纽瓦克机场，她登上一架环球航空（T.W.A.）的喷气客机，从此再无人见过。

===Original===

9:50 p.m. EST: Entire SAC fleet of 1200 jet bombers takes off for rendezvous over Newfoundland, where 72% are compelled to ditch as tankers fail to keep refueling rendezvous. (Orders committing the aircraft originate with SAC commander, found to be a suicide.)

10:14 p.m. EST: Submarine fusion explosion destroys 40% of New York City. Analysis of fallout indicates U.S. Navy Polaris missiles were detonated underwater in bay; by elimination it is deduced that the submarine was the Ethan Allen.

10:50 p.m. EST: President's party assassinated by Secretary of Health, Education and Welfare; Secretary then dies on bayonet of Marine guard who furnished the grenade.

10:55 p.m. EST: Satellite stations observe great nuclear explosions in China and Tibet.

11:03 p.m. EST: Heavily loaded munitions barges exploded near North Sea dikes of Holland; dikes breached, 1800 square miles of reclaimed land flooded out. …

And so on. The incidents were countless. But before long, before even the C.I.A. had finished the first playthrough of the tapes, before their successors in the task identified Disht dvornyet ilgt as a Ukrainian dialect rendering of, My God, it works!—before all this, one fact was already apparent. There were many incidents scattered around the world, but not one of them took place in Russia itself.

===Chinese===

东部标准时间晚9:50：战略空军司令部（SAC）全部1200架喷气轰炸机起飞，赴纽芬兰上空会合；因加油机未能准时赴会，72%被迫弃机落水。（出动这批飞机的命令出自战略空军司令部司令本人；事后查明他已自尽。）

东部标准时间晚10:14：一次潜艇聚变爆炸毁掉了纽约市的40%。沉降物分析表明，是美国海军的"北极星"导弹在海湾水下被引爆；经排除法推定，肇事潜艇就是"伊桑·艾伦"号。

东部标准时间晚10:50：总统一行遭卫生教育福利部长刺杀；部长随后丧命于一名海军陆战队卫兵的刺刀之下——手榴弹正是此人递的。

东部标准时间晚10:55：卫星观测站观测到中国和西藏境内的大规模核爆炸。

东部标准时间晚11:03：满载弹药的驳船在荷兰北海海堤附近爆炸；堤防决口，1800平方英里围垦田没于洪水。……

诸如此类。乱事多得数不清。但没过多久——甚至早在中央情报局把录音带放完第一遍之前，早在接手这项任务的后继者辨明"迪什特·德沃尔涅特·伊尔格特"是乌克兰方言里"我的天，当真灵验了！"的一种说法之前——在这一切之前，有一个事实已昭然若揭。乱子星散世界各地，却没有一件出在俄国本土。

===Original===

Warsaw was ablaze, China pockmarked with blasts, East Berlin demolished along with its western sector, in eight rounds fired from a U.S. Army nuclear cannon. But the U.S.S.R. had not suffered at all, as far as could be told by the prying eyes in orbit; and that fact was reason enough for it to suffer very greatly very soon.

Within minutes of this discovery what remained of the military strength of the Western world was roaring through airless space toward the most likely targets of the East.

One unscathed missile base in Alaska completed a full shoot, seven missiles with fusion warheads. The three American bases that survived at all in the Mediterranean fired what they had. Even Britain, which had already watched the fire-tails of the American missiles departing on suicide missions, managed to resurrect its own two prototype Blue Streaks from their racks, where they had moldered since the cancellation of the British missile program. One of these museum-pieces destroyed itself in launching, but the other chugged painfully across the sky, the tortoise following the flight of the hares. It arrived a full half-hour after the newer, hotter missiles. It might as well not have bothered. There was not much left to destroy.

It was fortunate for the Communists that most of the Western arsenal had already spent itself in suicide. What was left wiped out Moscow, Leningrad and nine other cities. It was even fortunate for the whole world, for this was the Apocalypse they had dreaded, every possible nuclear weapon committed. But the circumstances were such—hasty orders, often at once recalled; confusion; panic—that most were unfused, many others merely tore great craters in the quickly healing surface of the sea. The fallout was locally murderous but quite spotty.

And the conventional forces invading Russia found nothing to fight. The Russians were as confused as they. There were not many survivors of the very top brass, and no one seemed to know just what had happened.

Was the Secretary of the C.P., U.S.S.R. behind that terrible brief agony? As he was dead before it was over, there was no way to tell. More than a quarter of a billion lives went into mushroom-shaped clouds, and nearly half of them were Russian, Latvian, Tatar and Kalmuck. The Peace Commission squabbled for a month, until the breakdown of communications cut them off from their governments and each other; and in that way, for a time, there was peace.

===Chinese===

华沙一片火海，中国被炸得满身麻斑，东柏林连同它西面的西区一道被夷为平地——动用的是美军核火炮，打了八发。可苏联分毫未损，至少从轨道里窥探的眼睛看不出它吃了什么亏；而这个事实本身，就足够让它很快受一场大得多的罪。

这一发现作出后不过几分钟，西方世界残存的军事力量便呼啸着冲过没有空气的太空，扑向东方最可能的目标。

阿拉斯加一座毫发无伤的导弹基地打完了一整轮齐射——七枚聚变弹头导弹。地中海三处总算幸存的美军基地也把家底倾囊打出。就连英国——它已经眼睁睁看着美国导弹拖着火尾出发去送死——也设法把自己那两枚原型"蓝光"（Blue Streak）导弹从发射架上请了出来；自英国导弹计划取消以来，它们一直在架上发霉。这两件博物馆古董，一枚在发射时自我了断，另一枚吭哧吭哧、痛苦万分地爬过天空，恰似龟兔赛跑里追赶飞兔的那只龟。它比那些更新、更热的导弹足足晚了半小时才到。它来不来本无所谓。可留给摧毁的东西已经不多了。

对共产党人来说，所幸西方武库的大半已在赴死之中自行耗尽。剩下的那些抹掉了莫斯科、列宁格勒和另外九座城市。这对整个世界甚至是件幸事，因为他们所惧怕的世界末日已然降临，每一件可能动用的核武器都被押上了台。但情形如此——命令下得仓促，往往一下达就收回；混乱；恐慌——以致大多数导弹没有引爆，其余的许多不过是在迅速愈合的海面上凿出几个巨坑。放射性沉降在个别地方足以致命，分布却极其零散。

而攻入俄国的常规部队发现无仗可打。俄国人同他们一样茫然。最高统帅部里没剩下几个活口，似乎也没有谁确切知道究竟发生了什么。

那场可怕而短促的浩劫，幕后可是苏共总书记的手笔？浩劫尚未结束他已毙命，无从查证。超过两亿五千万条性命进了蘑菇状烟云，其中近一半是俄罗斯人、拉脱维亚人、鞑靼人和卡尔梅克人。和平委员会争吵了一个月，直到通讯崩溃，把他们同各自的政府、也彼此隔断；就这样，有那么一段时候，天下出现了和平。

===Original===

This was the sort of peace that was left, thought Chandler looking around at the queer faces and queerer surroundings, the peace of medieval baronies, cut off from the world, untouched where the rain of fallout had passed by but hardly civilized any more. Even his own home town, trying to take his life in a form of law, reduced at last to torture and exile to cast him out, was not the civilization he had grown up in but something new and ugly.

There was a great deal of talk he did not understand because he could not quite hear it, though they looked at him. Then Guy, with the gun, led him up to the front of the room. They had constructed an improvised platform out of plywood panels resting on squat, heavy boxes that looked like empty ammunition crates. On the dais was a dentist's chair, bolted to the plywood; and in the chair, strapped in, baby spotlights on steel-tube frames glaring on her, was a girl. She looked at Chandler with regretting eyes but did not speak.

"Stranger, get up there," said Guy, prodding him from behind, and Chandler took a plain wooden chair next to the girl.

"People of Orphalese," cried the teenage cutie named Meggie, "we have two more brands to save from the imps!"

The men and women in the audience cackled or shrilled "Save them! Save them!" They all had a look of invisible uniforms, Chandler saw, like baseball players in the lobby of a hotel or soldiers in a diner outside the gate of their post; they were all of a type. Their type was something strange. Some were tall, some short; there were old, fat, lean and young around them; but they all wore about them a look of glowing excitement, muted by an aura of suffering and pain. They wore, in a word, the look of bigots.

The bound girl was not one of them. She might have been twenty years old or as much as thirty. She might have been pretty. It was hard to tell; she wore no makeup, her hair strung raggedly to her neck, and her face was drawn into a tight, lean line. It was her eyes that were alive. She saw Chandler and she was sorry for him. And he saw, as he turned to look at her, that she was manacled to the dentist's chair.

===Chinese===

留下的就是这么一种和平了——钱德勒环顾着四周那些古怪的面孔和更加古怪的环境，心想。这是中世纪男爵领地的和平：与世隔绝，放射性尘雨没扫过的地方倒还完好，却再也算不得文明。就连他自己的家乡——起先想以法律的形式取他性命、末了只剩下烙刑加流放来把他逐出门墙——也不是他长大的那个文明，而是某种新生的、丑恶的东西。

屋里说着的许多话他听不懂，因为他听不真切，尽管人们都朝他看。随后，盖伊提着枪，把他领到屋子前部。他们用几块胶合板拼板搭了座临时台子，底下垫着几只矮墩墩的沉重木箱，看着像空弹药箱。台上是一把牙医椅，用螺栓固定在胶合板上；椅子里捆着一个姑娘，身上扎着皮带，钢管架上的小聚光灯刺目地照着她。她望着钱德勒，目光里含着歉意，却没有开口。

"生人，上去。"盖伊从背后捅了他一下说，钱德勒便在姑娘旁边的一把光板木椅上坐下。

"奥法利斯的民众，"那个名叫梅吉的俏丽少女喊道，"我们又有两名烙印者要从小鬼手里救出来啦！"

听众里的男男女女或是咯咯怪笑，或是尖声嚷："救他们！救他们！"钱德勒看得分明，他们个个穿着一身看不见的制服，就像聚在旅馆大堂里的棒球队员，或是驻营门外小饭馆里的士兵；他们全属一类。这一类人可有点蹊跷。有的高，有的矮；老的、胖的、瘦的、年轻的，他们当中都有；可人人身上都笼着一层灼热的亢奋，又被一层苦难与疼痛的氤氲压得黯淡。一句话，他们挂着一副偏执狂信者的面相。

被绑着的姑娘不属于他们。她可能有二十岁，也可能三十。她本可能挺漂亮。很难说：她不施脂粉，头发乱蓬蓬地垂到颈项，脸绷成一道紧窄的线。活着的是她的眼睛。她看见了钱德勒，为他难过。而他转身看她的时候，看见她被铐在牙医椅上。

===Original===

"People of Orphalese," chanted Guy, standing behind Chandler with the muzzle of the gun against his neck, "the *meeting* of the Orphalese Self-Preservation *Society* will now come to *order*." There was an approving, hungry murmur from the audience.

"Well, people of *Orphalese*," Guy went on in his singsong, "the agenda for the day is first the salvation of we *Orphalese* on McGuire's *Mountain*."

("All saved, all of us saved," rolled a murmur from the congregation.) A lean, redheaded man bounded to the platform and fussed with the stand of spotlights, turning one of them full on Chandler.

"People of Orphalese, as we are *saved*, do I have your consent to *pass on* and proceed to the next order of *business*?"

("Consent, consent, consent," rolled the echo.)

===Chinese===

"奥法利斯的民众，"盖伊吟诵道。他站在钱德勒身后，枪口抵着他的后颈，"奥法利斯自卫*会*的*集会*现在*开会*。"听众里响起一阵赞许而饥渴的低语。

"那么，*奥法利斯*的民众，"盖伊用他那单调的吟唱腔接着说，"今天的议程，第一项是拯救麦圭尔山（McGuire's Mountain）上的我们*奥法利斯人*。"

（"全得救了，我们全得救了，"会众中滚过一阵低语。）一个精瘦的红发汉子蹿上台，手忙脚乱地摆弄那架聚光灯，把其中一盏笔直打在钱德勒身上。

"奥法利斯的民众，我们既已*得救*，可否承诸位*恩准*，*往下进行*，着手下一项*议程*？"

（"恩准，恩准，恩准，"回声滚过。）

===Original===

"And then the *second* item of business is to *welcome* and bring to grace these two newly *found* and adopted *souls*."

The congregation shouted variously: "Bring them to grace! Save them from the imps! Keep Orphalese from the taint of the beast!"

Evidently Guy was satisfied. He nodded and became more chatty. "Okay, people of Orphalese, let's get down to it. We got two new ones, like I say. Their spirits have gone wandering on the wind, or anyway one of them has, and you all know the et cetera. They have committed a wrong unto others and therefore unto themselves. Herself, I mean. Course, the other one could have a flame spirit in him too." He stared severely at Chandler. "Boys, keep an eye on him, why don't you?" he said to two men in the front row, surrendering his gun. "Meggie, you tell about the female one."

===Chinese===

"接着，*第二*项议程，是*欢迎*这两位新*寻得*并收养的*灵魂*，引他们*入于*恩典。"

会众七嘴八舌地喊："把他们引到恩典里去！从小鬼手里救下他们！别让畜生的污秽沾染奥法利斯！"

显然盖伊满意了。他点点头，言谈也随意起来。"好啦，奥法利斯的民众，咱们开始办正事。就像我说的，咱们得了两个新人。他们的灵魂已随风游荡去了——至少其中一个如此——那一套你们全都晓得。他们对他人行了恶，因而也就是对自己行了恶。我是说她。当然，另一个身上说不定也有火焰精灵。"他狠狠盯住钱德勒。"弟兄们，替我盯着他点儿，好不好？"他对前排的两个人说，把枪交了出去。"梅吉，女的那位由你来讲。"

===Original===

"People of Orph'lese, well, I was walking down the cut and I heard this car coming. Well, I was pretty surprised, you know. I had to figure what to do. You all know what the trouble is with cars."

"The imps!" cried a woman of forty with a face like a catfish.

The girl nodded. "Most prob'ly. Well, I—I mean, people of Orph'lese, well, I was by the switchback where we keep the chevvy-freeze hid, so I just waited till I saw it slowing down for the curve—me out of sight, you know—and I rolled the chevvy-freeze out nice and it caught the wheels. Right over!" she cried gleefully. "Off the shoulder, people of Orph'lese, and into the ditch and over, and I didn't give it a chance to burn. I cut the switch and I had her! I put a knife into her back, just a little, about a quarter of an inch, maybe. Her pain was the breakin' of the shell that enclosed her understanding, like it says. I figured she was all right then because she yelled but I brought her along that way. Then Guy took care of her until we got the synod. Oh," she remembered, "and her tongue staggered a little without purpose while he was putting it on, didn't it, Guy?" The bearded man nodded, grinning, and lifted up the girl's foot. Incredulously, Chandler saw that it was bound tight with a three-foot length of barbed wire, wound and twisted like a tourniquet, the blood black and congealed around it. He lifted his shocked eyes to meet the girl's. She only looked at him, with pity and understanding.

===Chinese===

"奥法利斯乡亲们，是这样，我正顺着路堑往下走，听见一辆汽车开过来。啊，我可着实吃了一惊，你们知道的。我得合计合计怎么办。汽车那毛病出在哪儿，大伙儿都知道。"

"小鬼！"一个四十来岁、长着鲶鱼脸的女人喊道。

姑娘点点头。"多半是。是这样，我——我是说，奥法利斯乡亲们，我那时正待在回头弯那儿，咱们藏'冻僵的雪佛兰'的地方，我就等着，等到瞅见那车为转弯减了速——我藏在看不见的地方，你们知道——我就把那辆'冻僵的雪佛兰'顺顺当当推出去，正好卡住车轮。翻了个底朝天！"她乐得直喊。"冲下路肩，奥法利斯乡亲们，栽进沟里，翻了个个儿，我没让它烧起来。我拧断了点火开关，她就到手了！我把刀捅进她后背，就一点点，约莫四分之一英寸深吧。她的疼破开了包住她悟性的那层壳，经上是这么说的。我寻思她那时已经没事了，因为她喊出了声，我就一路那样带着她走。然后盖伊照应她，一直照应到咱们开评议会。哦，"她想起来，"他给她缠那玩意儿的时候，她的舌头有点不着边际地乱打转，是不是，盖伊？"蓄胡子的男人点点头，咧嘴笑了，托起被绑姑娘的脚。钱德勒难以置信地看到，那只脚被一段三英尺长的铁蒺藜捆得死紧，缠了一圈又一圈，绞得像条止血带，四周的血又黑又凝。他惊骇地抬起眼睛，去迎那姑娘的目光。她只是望着他，眼中有怜悯，有体谅。

===Original===

Guy patted the foot and let it go. "I didn't have any more C-clamps, people of Orphalese," he apologized, "but it looks all right at that. Well, let's see. We got to make up our minds about these two, I guess—no, wait!" He held up his hand as a murmur began. "First thing is, we ought to read a verse or two."

He opened a purple-bound volume at random, stared at a page for a moment, moving his lips, and then read:

"Some of you say, 'It is the north wind who has woven the clothes we wear.'
"And I say, Ay, it was the north wind, but shame was his loom, and the softening of the sinews was his thread.
"And when his work was done he laughed in the forest."

===Chinese===

盖伊拍了拍那只脚，松开手。"我手里再没有C形夹钳了，奥法利斯的民众，"他致歉道，"不过看上去倒也不碍事。好，想想看。这两位，咱们得拿个主意，我看——不，慢着！"低语声刚起，他抬手止住。"头一件事，咱们得先读上一两节经文。"

他随手翻开一本紫皮册子，盯着书页瞅了一会儿，嘴唇翕动，然后念道：

"你们中间有人说：'织我们衣裳的，是北风。'
"而我说：是啊，正是北风，但羞耻是他的织机，使筋骨软化的丝线，是他的纱。
"而当他的活计做成，他便在林中放声大笑。"

===Original===

Gently he closed the book, looking thoughtfully at the wall at the back of the room. He scratched his head. "Well, people of Orphalese," he said slowly "they're laughing in the forest all right, I guarantee, but we've got one here that may be honest in the flesh, probably is, though she was a thief in the spirit. Right? Well, do we take her in or reject her, O people of Orphalese?"

The audience muttered to itself and then began to call out: "Accept! Oh, bring in the brand! Accept and drive out the imp!"

"Fine," said the teenager, rubbing her hands and looking at the bearded man. "Guy, let her go." He began to release her from the chair. "You, girl stranger, what's your name?"

The girl said faintly, "Ellen Braisted."

" '*Meggie*, my name is Ellen Braisted,' " corrected the teenager. "Always say the name of the person you're talkin' to in Orph'lese, that way we know it's you talkin', not a flame spirit or wanderer. Okay, go sit down." Ellen limped wordlessly down into the audience. "Oh, and people of Orph'lese," said Meggie, "the car's still there if we need it for anything. It didn't burn. Guy, you go on with this other fellow."

Guy stroked his beard and assessed Chandler, looking him over carefully. "Okay," he said. "People of Orphalese, the *third* order of business is to *welcome* or reject this *other* brand saved from the imps, as may be your *pleasure*." Chandler sat up straighter now that all of them were looking at him again; but it wasn't quite his turn, at that, because there was an interruption. Guy never finished. From the valley, far below, there was a sudden mighty thunder, rolling among the mountains. The windows blew in with a crystalline crash.

===Chinese===

他轻轻合上书，若有所思地望着屋子后墙，挠了挠头皮。"喏，奥法利斯的民众，"他慢吞吞地说，"林子里保管在笑，这我担保。可咱们这儿有一个，论肉身也许是诚实的——多半是——尽管论灵魂她是个贼。对不对？那么，收下她，还是拒斥她，哦，奥法利斯的民众？"

观众嘀嘀咕咕了一阵，接着喊起来："接纳！哦，把烙印者带进来！接纳她，把小鬼逐出去！"

"好，"少女搓着手掌，望向蓄胡子的男人。"盖伊，放她下来。"他开始把她从椅子里松开。"你，生人姑娘，叫什么名字？"

姑娘有气无力地说："艾伦·布雷斯特德（Ellen Braisted）。"

"'梅吉，我叫艾伦·布雷斯特德，'"少女纠正道。"在奥法利斯，跟谁说话都得先叫出对方的名字，这样我们才知道是你在说话，不是火焰精灵，也不是流浪者。好啦，坐下吧。"艾伦一言不发，一瘸一拐地走下观众席。"哦，还有，奥法利斯乡亲们，"梅吉说，"那辆车还停在那儿，咱们要有什么用得着的。它没烧。盖伊，这位另来的伙计由你接着办。"

盖伊捻着胡须，把钱德勒上下细细打量了一遍，掂量着他。"好，"他说。"奥法利斯的民众，*第三*项议程，是*欢迎*还是拒斥这名从小鬼手里救下的*另*一名烙印者，悉凭诸位*尊意*。"众人又都望着他，钱德勒把腰背挺得更直；可这还不全然轮到他，因为来了个岔子。盖伊终究没把话说完。山谷深处，远在下方，骤然滚过一阵巨大的雷霆，在群山之间隆隆回荡。窗玻璃伴着一阵水晶相击般的脆响，向屋内炸开。

---

===Original===

The room erupted into confusion, the audience leaping from their seats, running to the broad windows, Guy and the teenage girl seizing rifles, everyone in motion at once.

Chandler straightened, then sat down again. The redheaded man guarding him was looking away. It would be quite possible to grab his gun, run, get away from these maniacs. Yet he had nowhere to go. They might be crazy, but they seemed to have organization.

They seemed, in fact, to have worked out, on whatever crazed foundation of philosophy, some practical methods for coping with possession. He decided to stay, wait and see.

And at once he found himself leaping for the gun.

No. Chandler didn't find himself attacking the redheaded man. He found his *body* doing it; Chandler had nothing to do with it. It was the helpless compulsion he had felt before, that had nearly cost him his life; his body active and urgent and his mind completely cut off from it. He felt his own muscles move in ways he had not planned, observed himself leap forward, felt his own fist strike at the back of the redheaded man's ear. The man went spinning, the gun went flying, Chandler's body leaped after it, with Chandler a prisoner in his own brain, watching, horrified and helpless. And he had the gun!

===Chinese===

屋里霎时炸成一锅乱：听众从座位上蹦起来，奔向宽大的窗户；盖伊和那少女抓起步枪；人人同时在动弹。

钱德勒挺直身子，随即又坐下。看守他的红发汉子正望着别处。夺下他的枪、跑掉、逃出这群疯子，本来易如反掌。可他无处可去。他们或许是疯子，但他们似乎有组织。

说实在的，他们似乎已经在不知怎样一种疯癫的哲学地基之上，摸索出了几套对付附身的实用办法。他决定留下，等着瞧。

而紧接着，他发现自己朝那支枪扑了过去。

不。钱德勒并没有发现自己去攻击那红发汉子——他发现干这事的是他的*身体*；钱德勒与此毫无干系。又是那种他领教过的无助胁迫，那次几乎要了他的命：身体主动而急切，心智被彻底隔绝在外。他感觉自己的肌肉以他未曾打算的方式动作，眼看着自己扑上前去，感觉自己的拳头砸向红发汉子的耳后。那人旋转着栽倒，枪飞了出去，钱德勒的身体扑上去接——钱德勒成了自己脑壳里的囚徒，旁观着，惊骇而无助。而枪，到了手里！

===Original===

He caught it in the hand that was his own hand, though someone else was moving it; he raised it and half-turned. He was suddenly conscious of a fusillade of gunfire from the roof, and a scattered echo of guns all round the outside of the house. Part of him was surprised, another alien part was not. He started to shoot the teenaged girl in the back of the head, silently shouting *No!*

His fingers never pulled the trigger.

He caught a second's glimpse of someone just beside him, whirled and saw the girl, Ellen Braisted, limping swiftly toward him with her barbed-wire amulet loose and catching at her feet. In her hands was an axe-handle club caught up from somewhere. She struck at Chandler's head, with a face like an eagle's, impersonal and determined. The blow caught him and dazed him, and from behind someone else struck him with something else. He went down.

He heard shouts and firing, but he was stunned. He felt himself dragged and dropped. He saw a cloudy, misty girl's face hanging over him; it receded and returned. Then a frightful blistering pain in his hand startled him back into full consciousness.

It was the girl, Ellen, still there, leaning over him and, oddly, weeping. And the pain in his hand was the burning flame of a kitchen match. Ellen was doing it, his wrist in one hand, a burning match held to it with the other.

===Chinese===

他用那只本属于他自己的手接住枪，虽然挪动它的是别人；他举枪半转过身。他蓦然意识到屋顶上炸开了排枪，屋子四周零零落落地回响着枪声。他有一部分吃惊，另一个陌生的部分却不吃惊。他开始朝那少女的后脑开枪，同时无声地嘶喊：*不！*

他的手指终究没有扣动扳机。

他瞥见紧挨在身边有个人，只有一瞬；猛一转身，看见那姑娘艾伦·布雷斯特德正飞快地一瘸一拐朝他奔来，她那件铁蒺藜护身符松脱了，绊缠着她的双脚。她手里是一根不知从哪儿抄来的斧柄木棒。她朝钱德勒的头上打去，一张鹰隼似的脸，不含个人意气，只有决绝。这一击命中，打得他发懵，背后又有什么人用别的什么打了他一下。他倒了下去。

他听得见喊叫与枪声，但他已被打得发懵。他觉出自己被拖拽，又被撂下。他看见一张云雾似的少女面孔悬在自己上方；隐去，又回来。随后，手上那阵骇人的、燎起水泡的剧痛把他惊回到全然的知觉之中。

是那姑娘艾伦，还在那儿，俯身看着他，而且奇怪的是，在哭。他手上那疼，是一根点燃的厨房火柴的灼焰。是艾伦干的：她一只手攥住他的手腕，另一只手把燃烧的火柴抵在上面。

## IV / 四

===Original===

Chandler yelled hoarsely, jerking his hand away.

She dropped the match and jumped up, stepping on the flame and watching him. She had a butcher knife that had been caught between her elbow and her body while she burned him. Now she put her hand on the knife, waiting. “Does it hurt?” she demanded tautly.

Chandler howled, with incredulity and rage: “God damn it, yes! What did you expect?”

“I expected it to hurt,” she agreed. She watched him for a moment more and then, for the first time since he had seen her, she smiled. It was a small smile, but a beginning. A fusillade of shots from outside wiped it away at once. “Sorry,” she said. “I had to do that. Please trust me.”

===Chinese===

钱德勒嘶声叫喊，猛地把手抽了回来。

她丢掉火柴，跳起身来，一脚踩灭火苗，眼睛盯着他。烧伤他的时候，她那把屠刀一直夹在臂肘和身体之间，这时她的手又搭上了刀柄，等待着。“疼吗？”她紧绷着嗓子问。

钱德勒吼了起来，满腔的难以置信和怒火：“该死，当然疼！你还指望怎样？”

“我本来就指望它疼。”她表示同意。她又盯了他片刻，随后，自他见到她以来头一回，她笑了。那只是浅浅一笑，却是个开端。外面一阵排枪立刻把这笑意扫得干干净净。“对不起，”她说，“我不得不那么做。请相信我。”

===Original===

“*Why* did you have to burn my hand?”

“House rules,” she said. “Keeps the flame-spirits out, you know. They can’t stand pain.” She took her hand off the knife warily, “it still hurts, doesn’t it?”

“It still does, yes,” nodded Chandler bitterly, and she lost interest in him and got up, looking about the room. Three of the Orphalese were dead, or seemed to be from the casual poses in which they lay draped across a chair on the floor. Some of the others might have been freshly wounded, though it was hard to tell the casualties from the others in view of the Orphalese custom of self-inflicted pain. There was still firing going on outside and overhead, and a shooting-gallery smell of burnt powder in the air. The girl, Ellen Braisted, limped back with the butcher knife held carelessly in one hand. She was followed by the teenager, who wore a smile of triumph﻿—and, Chandler noticed for the first time, a sort of tourniquet of barbed-wire on her left forearm, the flesh puffy red around it “Whopped ’em,” she said with glee, and pointed a .22 rifle at Chandler.

===Chinese===

“你*为什么*非要烧我的手？”

“本家规矩。”她说，“能挡住火焰精灵，你知道的。它们受不了疼。”她警惕地把手从刀上移开，“现在还疼，对吧？”

“还疼，可不。”钱德勒苦涩地点头说。她对他失去了兴趣，站起身环视房间。三个奥法利斯人（Orphalese）死了——至少从他们瘫软地摊在地板上一把椅子上的散漫姿势看，像是死了。另有一些可能是新挂了彩，不过奥法利斯人有自我施加疼痛的习俗，很难把伤员同旁人区分开来。外面和头顶上仍枪声不断，空气里有一股打靶场似的火药焦味。那姑娘，艾伦·布雷斯特德（Ellen Braisted），一手随随便便提着那把屠刀，一瘸一拐地走了回来。跟在她身后的是那个少女，脸上挂着得胜的笑——钱德勒还头一回注意到，她左小臂上缠着一圈铁蒺藜网似的止血带，四周的皮肉红肿凸起。“收拾了他们。”她兴高采烈地说，把一支.22步枪指向钱德勒。

===Original===

Ellen Braisted said, “Oh, he﻿—*Meggie*, I mean, he’s all right.” She pointed at his burned palm. Meg approached him with competent care, the rifle resting on her good right forearm and aimed at him as she examined his burn. She pursed her lips and looked at his face. “All right, Ellen, I guess he’s clean. But you want to burn ’em deeper’n that. Never pays to go easy, just means we’ll have to do something else to ’im tomorrow.”

“The hell you will,” thought Chandler, and all but said it; but reason stopped him. In Rome he would have to do Roman deeds. Besides, maybe their ideas worked. Besides, he had until tomorrow to make up his mind about what he wanted to do.

“Ellen, show him around,” ordered the teenager. “I got no time myself. Shoosh! Almost got us that time, Ellen. Got to be more careful, cause the white-handed aren’t clean, you know.” She strutted away, the rifle at trail. She seemed to be enjoying herself very much.

===Chinese===

艾伦·布雷斯特德说：“哦，他——*梅吉*（Meggie），我是说，他没事。”她指了指他烧伤的手掌。梅格带着利落的谨慎朝他走来，步枪架在她完好的右小臂上，一边检查他的烧伤，一边用枪口对着他。她噘起嘴唇，端详他的脸。“行了吧，艾伦，我看他是干净的。不过你该烧得再深一点。手软从来不划算，无非意味着我们明天还得再给他来点别的。”

“你休想。”钱德勒想，这话几乎说出了口；但理智拦住了他。身在罗马，就得行罗马人的事。再说，也许他们这一套真管用。再说，到明天之前，他还来得及想清楚自己究竟想做什么。

“艾伦，带他四处看看。”少女命令道，“我自己没空。嘘！那一下差点儿打着咱们，艾伦。得当心点儿，因为白手上头也不干净，你是知道的。”她提着枪口朝下的步枪，神气活现地走开了。她看上去玩得开心极了。

===Original===

---

The name of the girl in the barbed-wire bracelet was Ellen Braisted. She came from Lehigh County, Pennsylvania, and Chandler’s first wonder was what she was doing nearly three thousand miles from home.

Nobody liked to travel much these days. One place was as bad as another, except that in the place where you were known you could perhaps count on friends and as a stranger you were probable fair game anywhere else. Of course, there was one likely reason for travel.

She didn’t like to talk about it, that was clear, but that was the reason. She had been possessed. When the teenager trapped her car the day before she had been the tool of another’s will. She had had a dozen sub-machine guns in the trunk and she had meant to deliver them to a party of hunters in a valley just south of McGuire’s Mountain. Chandler said, with some effort, “I must have been﻿—”

“*Ellen*, I must have been,” she corrected.

“Ellen, I must have been possessed too, just now. When I grabbed the gun.”

===Chinese===

---

那个腕上戴着铁蒺藜环的姑娘，名叫艾伦·布雷斯特德。她来自宾夕法尼亚州的利哈伊县（Lehigh County），钱德勒最初的惊奇是：她跑到离家将近三千英里的地方来干什么。

如今没人喜欢远行。一个地方和另一个地方一样糟，只不过在熟人之地你或许还能指望朋友，而在别处，一个外乡人多半就是现成的猎物。当然，出门远行倒也有一个说得通的理由。

她不愿谈这个，这一点很清楚，可那正是缘由。她曾被附身。前一天少女拦下她汽车的时候，她还是他人意志的工具。她后备箱里装着十二支冲锋枪，本打算送给麦圭尔山（McGuire’s Mountain）正南面一条山谷里的一伙猎人。钱德勒费了点劲才说出口：“我想必是——”

“*艾伦*，我想必是被附身了。”她纠正道。

“艾伦，我刚才想必也被附身了。就在我抢枪的时候。”

===Original===

“Of course. First time?”

He shook his head. For some reason the brand on his forehead began to throb.

“Well, then you know. Look out here, now.”

They were at the great pier windows that looked out over the valley. Down below was the river, an arc of the railroad tracks, the wooded mountainside he had scaled. “Over there, Chandler.” She was pointing to the railroad bridge.

Wispy gray smoke drifted off southward toward the stream. The freight train Chandler had ridden on had been stopped, all that time, in the middle of the bridge. The explosion that blew out their windows had occurred when another train plowed into it﻿—evidently at high speed. It seemed that one of the trains had carried some sort of chemicals. The bridge was a twisted mess.

===Chinese===

“当然。头一回？”

他摇了摇头。不知怎的，他额头上的烙印开始突突地跳。

“那你就知道了。现在，往这儿看。”

他们站在巨大的凸窗前，窗外俯瞰着整条山谷。下面是河流、铁路路轨的一道弧线，还有他攀爬上来的那面林木覆盖的山坡。“那边，钱德勒。”她指着铁路桥说。

一缕缕灰白的烟正向南面的溪流方向飘去。钱德勒搭的那列货车，原来一直都停在桥中央。把他们的窗户震塌的那场爆炸，是另一列火车撞上它时发生的——显然是以极高的速度。看来两列火车中有一列载着某种化学品。桥已经拧成一团废铁。

===Original===

“A diversion, Chandler,” said Ellen Braisted. “They wanted us looking that way. Then they attacked from up the mountain.”

“Who?”

Ellen looked surprised. “The men that crashed the trains﻿ ﻿… if they *are* men. The ones who possessed me﻿—and you﻿—and the hunters. They don’t like these Orphalese, I think. Maybe they’re a little afraid of them. I think the Orphalese have a pretty good idea of how to fight them.”

Chandler felt a sudden flash of sensation along his nerves. For a moment he thought he had been possessed again, and then he knew it for what it was. It was hope. “Ellen, I never thought of fighting them. I thought that was given up two years ago.”

===Chinese===

“那是调虎离山，钱德勒。”艾伦·布雷斯特德说，“他们要我们往那边看。然后他们从山上头攻了下来。”

“谁？”

艾伦露出惊讶的神色。“撞火车的那伙人……如果他们还算得上是*人*的话。就是附过我的身——还有你的身——还有那些猎人的那些东西。我看它们不喜欢这些奥法利斯人。也许还有点怕他们。我觉得，奥法利斯人对怎么对付它们，已经摸出了相当不错的一套门道。”

钱德勒感到一阵突如其来的震颤沿着神经窜过。有一瞬间他以为自己又被附身了，随后他认出了它是什么。那是希望。“艾伦，我从来没想过跟它们斗。我以为这事两年前大家就放弃了。”

===Original===

“So maybe you agree with me? Maybe you think it’s worth while sticking with the Orphalese?”

Chandler allowed himself the contemplation of what hope meant. To find someone in this world who had a *plan*! Whatever the plan was. Even if it was a bad plan. He didn’t think specifically of himself, or the brand on his forehead or the memory of the body of his wife. What he thought of was the prospect of thwarting﻿—not even defeating, merely hampering or annoying was enough!﻿—the imps, the “flame creatures,” the pythons, devils, incubi or demons who had destroyed a world he had thought very fair.

“If they’ll have me,” he said, “I’ll stick with them, all right! Where do I go to join?”

===Chinese===

“这么说，也许你同意我的看法？也许你觉得跟着奥法利斯人是值得的？”

钱德勒让自己细细体味了一下希望意味着什么。在这世上居然还能找到一个有*计划*的人！不管那计划是什么。哪怕是个糟糕的计划也罢。他想到的并不是他自己，不是额上的烙印，也不是对妻子遗体的记忆。他想的是挫败那些小鬼、那些“火焰生灵”、那些蟒、魔鬼、淫魔或恶魔的前景——甚至说不上挫败，仅仅是碍它们的事、惹它们厌，也就够了！正是它们，毁掉了一个他曾认为十分美好的世界。

“只要他们肯收我，”他说，“我就跟他们干下去，没问题！我上哪儿入伙？”

===Original===

---

It was not hard to join at all. Meg chattily informed him that he was already practically a member. “Chandler, we got to watch everybody strange, you know. See why, don’t you? Might have a flame spirit in ’em, no fault of theirs, but look how they could mess us up. But now we know you don’t, so﻿—What do you mean, how do we know? Cause you *did* have one when you busted loose in there. Can’t have two at a time, you know. Think we couldn’t tell the difference?”

The interrupted meeting was resumed after the place had been tidied up and the dead buried. There had been four of the hunters, and even without their sub-machine guns they had succeeded in killing eight Orphalese. But it was not all loss to the Orphalese, because two of the hunters were still alive, though wounded, and under the rules of this chessboard the captured enemy became a friend.

Guy had suffered a broken jaw in the scuffle and another man presided, a fat youth who favored a bandaged leg. He limped to his feet, grimacing and patting his leg. “O Orphalese and brothers,” he said, “we have lost friends, but we have won a test. Praise the Prophet, we will be spared to win again, and to drive the imps of fire out of our world. Meggie, you going to tie these folks up?” The girl proudly ordered one of the hunters into the spotlighted dentist’s chair, another into a wing chair that was hastily moved onto the platform. The men were bleeding and hurt, but they had clearly been abandoned by their possessors. They watched with puzzlement and fear.

“Walter, they’re okay now,” Meg reported as others finished tying up the hunters. “Oh, wait a minute.” She advanced on Chandler. “Chandler, I’m sorry. You sit down there, hear?”
