===Chinese===

---

入伙根本不难。梅格叽叽喳喳地告诉他，他实际上已经算是会员了。“钱德勒，对外来的人我们都要盯紧点，你知道的。明白为什么吧？他们身上说不定揣着个火焰精灵，这不怪他们本人，可你瞧瞧那能把我们搅成什么样。不过现在我们知道你没有，所以——什么意思，我们怎么知道的？因为你在里面发作那阵子，身上*确实*有一个。一个人身上同时搁不下两个，这你知道。以为我们分不出来吗？”

被打断的集会在屋子收拾停当、死者掩埋之后重新开始。猎人一共有四个，就算没了他们的冲锋枪，他们也干掉了八个奥法利斯人。但对奥法利斯人来说这并不全是损失，因为猎人里还有两个活着，虽然带了伤，而按这块棋盘上的规则，俘获的敌人就成了朋友。

混战中盖伊（Guy）下巴被打断了，改由另一个人主持——一个胖乎乎的年轻人，一条缠着绷带的腿偏着使。他龇牙咧嘴地撑起身来，拍了拍那条腿。“哦，奥法利斯人，众弟兄，”他说，“我们失去了朋友，但我们赢下了一场考验。赞美先知，我们必蒙眷顾，再战再胜，把火魔从我们的世界里驱逐出去。梅吉，你来把这几位绑上，好不好？”姑娘神气活现地吩咐把一个猎人按进牙医椅——那椅子正罩在聚光灯下——另一个按进一把匆忙搬上台去的高背扶手椅。那两个人流着血，带着伤，但显然已被他们身上的附主抛弃了。他们带着困惑和恐惧望着这一切。

“沃尔特（Walter），他们现在没问题了。”梅格汇报说，其他人也正好把猎人捆完。“哦，等一下。”她朝钱德勒逼近一步。“钱德勒，对不住。你也坐那边去，听见没？”

===Original===

Chandler suffered himself to be bound to a camp chair on the platform and Walter took a drink of wine and opened the ornate book that was before him on the rostrum.

“Meg, thanks. Guy, I hope I do this as good as you do. Let me read you a little. Let’s see.” He put on his steel-rimmed glasses and read:

“Much in you is still man, and much in you is not yet man, but a shapeless pygmy that walks asleep in the mist searching for its own awakening.”

He closed the book, looked with satisfaction at Guy and said: “Do you understand that, new friends? They are the words of the Prophet, who men call Kahlil Gibran. For the benefit of the new folks I ought to say that he died this fleshly life quite a good number of years ago, but his vision was unclouded. Like we say, we are the sinews that batter the flame spirits but he is our soul.” There was an antiphonal murmur from the audience and Walter flipped the pages again rapidly, obviously looking for a familiar passage. “People of Orphalese, here we are now. This’s what he says. What is this that has torn our world apart? The Prophet says: ‘It is life in quest of life, in bodies that fear the grave.’ Now, honestly, nothing could be clearer than that, people of Orphalese and friends! We got something taking possession of us, see? What is it? Well, he says here, people of Orphalese and friends, ‘It is a flame spirit in you ever gathering more of itself.’ Now, what the heck! Nobody can blame *us* for what a flame spirit *in* us does! So the first thing we got to learn, friends﻿—and people of Orphalese﻿—is, we aren’t to blame. And the second thing is, we *are* to blame!”

===Chinese===

钱德勒由着人把他绑在台上的一张折叠椅上。沃尔特喝了一口酒，翻开讲坛上摆在他面前的那本装帧华丽的书。

“梅格，谢了。盖伊，但愿我念得有你那么好。让我给你们念上一小段。我找找。”他戴上钢框眼镜，念道：

“你们身上有许多仍是人，还有许多尚不是人，只是一个在雾中沉睡行走、寻觅着自身觉醒的无形侏儒。”

他合上书，满意地望了盖伊一眼，说：“你们听懂了吗，新朋友们？这是先知的话，世人称他哈利勒·纪伯伦（Kahlil Gibran）。为照顾新来的各位，我应该说明一下，他脱离这具肉身的生活已经有相当年头了，但他的异象毫未蒙尘。就像我们常说的：我们是捶打火焰精灵的筋骨，而他，是我们的灵魂。”听众中响起一阵应答轮诵般的低语。沃尔特又飞快地翻动书页，显然在找一段熟地方。“奥法利斯的民众，这不，找到了。先知是这样说的。是什么把我们的世界撕成了碎片？先知说：‘那是在惧怕坟墓的躯壳中，追寻生命的生命。’说实在的，再没有比这更明白的了，奥法利斯的民众和朋友们！我们身上有个东西附上来，明白吗？是什么？瞧，他在这里说了，奥法利斯的民众和朋友们：‘它是你们体内的火焰精灵，不断聚拢更多的自身。’嘿，这算什么话！体内有个火焰精灵在作祟，这种事可不能怪到*我们*头上！所以我们首先要学会的，朋友们——还有奥法利斯的民众——就是：我们没有罪。而第二件事是：我们*有*罪！”

===Original===

He turned and grinned at Chandler kindly, while the chorus of responses came from the room, “Like here,” he said, “people of Orphalese, the Prophet says *everybody* is guilty. ‘The murdered is not unaccountable for his own murder, and the robbed is not blameless in being robbed. The righteous is not innocent of the deeds of the wicked, and the white-handed is not clean in the doings of the felon.’ You see what he’s getting at? We all got to take the responsibility for *everything*﻿—and that means we got to suffer﻿—but we don’t have to worry about any special things we did when some flame spirit or wanderer, like, took us over.

“But we do have to suffer, people of Orphalese.” His expression became grim. “Our beloved founder, Guy, who’s sitting there doing a little extra suffering now, was favored enough to understand these things in the very beginning, when he himself was seized by these imps. And it is all in this book! Like it says, ‘Your pain is self-chosen. It is the bitter potion by which the physician within you heals your sick self.’ Ponder on that, people of Orphalese﻿—and friends. No, I mean really ponder,” he explained, glancing at the bound “friends” on the platform. “We always do that for a minute. Ada there will play us some music so we can ponder.”

===Chinese===

他转过身，和蔼地朝钱德勒咧嘴一笑，屋里响起众口一声的应和。“就像这里，”他说，“奥法利斯的民众，先知说*人人*都有罪。‘被杀者对于自己被杀，不能全然无责；被劫者对于自己被劫，不能毫无过错。正直的人对恶人的行为并非无辜，白手的人对罪人的作为也并非干净。’你们品出他的意思了吧？我们所有人都得为*一切*承担责任——那就意味着我们都得受苦——但那些在火焰精灵或者流浪者之类占住我们身体时干下的种种事情，我们就不必再挂心了。

“可是我们确实得受苦，奥法利斯的民众。”他的神情严峻起来。“我们敬爱的创建人盖伊——这会儿正坐在那儿额外多受一份苦呢——蒙受了足够的恩眷，早在一开始、他自己被这些小鬼攫住的时候，就参透了这些道理。而这一切全都写在这本书里！就像书上说的：‘你们的痛苦多是自择的。那是你们体内的医者用来医治你们病己的苦药。’细细思量吧，奥法利斯的民众——还有朋友们。不，我是说要真地思量，”他解释道，瞥了一眼台上那几位被捆着的“朋友”，“我们总要这样默想一分钟。那边的埃达（Ada）会给我们奏点乐，好让大家思量。”

===Original===

---

Chandler shifted uncomfortably, while an old woman crippled by arthritis began fumbling a tune out of an electric organ. The burn Ellen Braisted had given him was beginning to hurt badly. If only these people were not such obvious *nuts*, he thought, he would feel a lot better about casting his lot in with them. But maybe it took lunatics to do the job. Sane people hadn’t accomplished much.

And anyway he had very little choice.﻿ ﻿…

“Ada, that’s enough,” ordered the fat youth. “Meg, come on up here. People of Orphalese, now you can listen again while Meg explains to the new folks how all this got started, seeing Guy’s in no condition to do it.”

===Chinese===

---

钱德勒不自在地挪动着身子，一位被关节炎弄成残废的老妇人开始在一架电风琴上磕磕绊绊地摸出一支曲子。艾伦·布雷斯特德给他的烧伤开始剧烈地疼起来。要是这帮人不这么明显地是一伙*疯子*就好了，他想，那他把自己的命运押给他们时，心里会踏实得多。可也许，干成这事偏偏就得靠疯子。正常人可没干成过什么。

再说，他本来也没有多少选择的余地。……

“埃达，够了。”胖青年吩咐道，“梅格，到这儿来。奥法利斯的民众，现在你们可以接着听了，让梅格给新来的各位讲讲这一切是怎么开始的，因为盖伊这副样子讲不了。”

===Original===

The teenager marched up to the platform and took the parade-rest position learned in some high-school debating society﻿—in the days when there were debating societies and high schools. “Ladies and gentlemen, well, let’s start at the beginning. Guy tells this better’n I do, of course, but I guess I remember it all pretty well too. I ought to. I was in on it and all.” She grimaced and said, “Well, anyway, ladies and gentlemen﻿—people of Orph’lese﻿—the way Guy organized this Orphalese self-protection society was, like Walter says, he was possessed. The only difference between Guy and you and me was that he knew what to do about it, because he read the book, you see. Not that that helped him at first, when he was took over. He was really seized. Yes, people of Orph’lese, he was taken and while his whole soul and brain and body was under the influence of some foul wanderer fiend from hell he did things that, ladies and gentlemen of Orph’lese, I wouldn’t want to tell you. He was a harp in the hand of the mighty, as it says. Couldn’t help it, not however much he tried. Only while he was doing﻿—the things﻿—he happened to catch his hand in a gas flame and, well, you can see it was pretty bad.” With a deprecatory smile Guy held up a twisted hand. “And, do you know, he was free of his imp right then and there! Now, Guy is a scientist, people of Orph’lese, he worked for the telephone company, and he not only had that training in the company school but he had read the book, you see, and he put two and two together. Oh, and he’s my uncle, of course. I’m proud of him. I’ve always loved him, and even when he﻿—when he was not one with himself, you know, when he was doing those terrible things to me, I knew it wasn’t Uncle Guy that was doing them, but something else. I didn’t know what, though. And when he told me he had figured out the Basic Rule, I went along with him every bit. I knew Guy wasn’t wrong, and what he said was from Scripture. Imps fear pain! So we got to love it. That one I know by heart, all right: ‘Could you keep your heart from wonder at the daily miracles of your life, your pain would not seem less wondrous than your joy.’ That’s what it says, right? So that’s why we got to hurt ourselves, people of Orph’lese﻿—and new brothers﻿—because the wanderers don’t like it when we hurt and they leave us alone. Simple’s that.

“Well﻿—” the girl’s face stiffened momentarily﻿—“I knew *I* wasn’t going to be seized. So Guy and I got Else, that’s the other girl he’d been doing things to, and we knew she wasn’t going to be taken either. Not if the imps feared pain like Guy said, because,” she said solemnly, “I want to tell you Guy hurt us pretty bad.

===Chinese===

少女迈步上台，摆出一个从某所高中辩论学会学来的稍息立姿——那还是有辩论学会、还有高中可上的年月。“女士们，先生们，呃，咱们从头讲起。这故事盖伊讲得比我好，这个自然，不过我想我也记得挺全的。也该记得。我可是从头到尾都在局的。”她做了个鬼脸，说：“总而言之呢，女士们先生们——奥法利斯的乡亲们——盖伊把这个奥法利斯自卫会组织起来的经过呢，就像沃尔特说的，是因为他被附身了。盖伊跟你和我的唯一不同，是他知道该怎么办，因为他读过那本书，你们明白吧。不过起初这也帮不了他，在他被夺去的时候。他是实打实地被攫住了。是的，奥法利斯的乡亲们，他被夺走了，而当他整个灵魂、头脑和身体都听命于地狱里某个下流流浪恶鬼的时候，他干下了那些事——奥法利斯的女士们先生们——那些事我都不想告诉你们。就像书上说的，他是强者掌中的竖琴。由不得自己，怎么使劲也由不得自己。只是就在他干着——那些事——的时候，他的手碰巧探进了煤气火苗里，喏，你们瞧得出，伤得不轻。”盖伊带着自谦的微笑，举起一只扭曲变形的手。“还有，你们知道吗？他当场就把身上那只小鬼甩脱了！要知道，盖伊是个科学家，奥法利斯的乡亲们，他在电话公司干过，他不光受过公司办学校的那套训练，还读过那本书，你们明白吧，于是他前后一串连，就想通了。哦，对了，他是我叔叔，这个自然。我为他骄傲。我一向爱他。就连在他——在他跟自己不是一个人的时候，你知道吧，在他对我干那些可怕事情的时候，我也知道，干那些事的不是盖伊叔叔，是别的什么东西。虽然我那会儿还不知道是什么。等他告诉我他悟出了基本法则，我就一点一滴全都跟了他。我知道盖伊不会错，他讲的都出自圣典。小鬼怕疼！所以咱们就得爱疼。这一段我倒背如流，真的：‘只要你们的心不对你们生命中的日日奇迹失去惊叹，你们的痛苦便不会显得比你们的欢乐少了奇妙。’书上就是这么说的，对不对？所以咱们才得弄疼自己，奥法利斯的乡亲们——还有新弟兄们——因为流浪者见不得咱们疼，它们就绕开咱们。就这么简单。

“这个——”姑娘的脸一时僵住了——“我原以为*我*是不会被攫走的。于是盖伊和我找来了埃尔斯（Else），就是他也对她干过那些事的另一个姑娘，我们知道，她同样不会被夺走。只要小鬼真像盖伊说的那样怕疼，就不会——因为，”她郑重其事地说，“我要告诉各位，盖伊把咱们弄得可真疼。”

===Original===

“And then we came out here, and found this place, and ever since then we’ve been adding brothers and sisters. It’s been slow, of course, because not many people come this way any more, and we’ve had to kill a lot. Yes, we have. Sometimes the possessed just can’t be saved, but﻿—”

Abruptly her face changed.

Suddenly alert, her face years older, she glanced around the room. Then she relaxed.﻿ ﻿…

And screamed.

===Chinese===

“后来我们就到了这儿，找到了这个地方，打那以后就一直不断地添弟兄、添姐妹。当然，进度慢，因为如今没多少人往这边来了，我们也只好杀了不少。是的，杀了。有时候，被附身的人实在救不回来，可是——”

她的脸骤然一变。

她突然警觉起来，那张脸一下子老了几年，眼睛扫视整个房间。随后她松弛了下来。……

随即尖叫起来。

===Original===

---

Guy leaped up. Hoarsely, his voice almost inarticulate as he tried to talk with his broken jaw, he cried, “Wha﻿ ﻿… Wha’s﻿ ﻿… *matter*, Meg?

“Uncle Guy!” she wailed. She plunged off the platform and flung herself into his arms, crying hysterically.

“*Wha?*”

She sobbed, “I could feel it! They *took* me. Guy, you promised me they couldn’t!”

He shook his head, dazed, staring at her as though she were indeed possessed﻿—still possessed, and telling him some fearful great lie to destroy his hopes. He seemed unable to comprehend what she had said. One of the hunters bellowed in stark fear: “For God’s sake, untie us! Give us a chance, anyway!” Chandler yelled agreement. In one split second everyone in the room had been transmuted by terror into something less than human. No one seemed capable of any action. Slowly the plump youth who had presided moved over to the hunter bound in the dentist’s chair and began to fumble blindly at the knots. Ellen Braisted dropped her head into her hands and began to shake.

===Chinese===

---

盖伊一跃而起。他嘶哑地喊，下巴断了，话几乎说不成句：“什……什么……*怎么*了，梅格？”

“盖伊叔叔！”她恸哭道。她从台上扑下来，一头扎进他怀里，歇斯底里地哭喊着。

“*什么？*”

她抽泣着说：“我能觉出来！它们*夺*了我。盖伊，你答应过我，说它们不能的！”

他摇着头，茫然地瞪着她，仿佛她真的被附了身——此刻仍被附在身上，正编着一个可怕的大谎来毁掉他的希望。他似乎无法理解她说的话。一个猎人发出极度恐惧的吼叫：“看在上帝分上，给我们解开！好歹给我们一个机会！”钱德勒喊出了同样的要求。一眨眼的工夫，屋里每一个人都被恐惧蜕变成了某种低于人的东西。似乎谁也没能力做出任何动作。主持集会的那个胖青年慢慢挪到绑在牙医椅上的猎人跟前，开始两眼无神地摸索绳结。艾伦·布雷斯特德把头埋进双手，开始浑身发抖。

===Original===

The cruelty of the moment was that they had all tasted hope. Chandler writhed wildly against his ropes, his mind racing out of control. The world had become a hell for everyone, but a bearable hell until the promise of a chance to end it gave them a full sight of what their lives had been. Now that that was dashed they were far worse off than before.

Walter finished with the hunter and lethargically began to pick at Chandler’s bonds. His face was slack and unseeing.

Then it, too, changed.

The plump youth stood up sharply, glanced about, and walked off the platform.

===Chinese===

这一刻的残忍在于：他们都尝过希望的滋味了。钱德勒疯狂地扭动挣扎着绳索，思绪脱缰般狂奔。世界早已对人人成了地狱，但那是个可以忍受的地狱——直到终结它的机会的许诺，让他们把自己一直过的是什么样的日子看了个通通透透。如今这许诺破灭了，他们的处境比从前还要糟得多。

沃尔特解完了猎人，没精打采地开始挑钱德勒的绳结。他面孔松弛，目光散乱。

接着，他身上也起了那种变化。

胖青年猛地站起身，扫视四周，走下了台。

===Original===

Ellen Braisted raised her face from her hands and, her eyes streaming, quietly stood up and followed. The old lady with the arthritis about-faced and limped with them. Chandler stared, puzzled, and then comprehended.

They were marching toward the corner of the room where the rifles were stacked. “Possessed!” Chandler bellowed, the words tasting of acid as they ripped out of his throat. “Stop them! You﻿—Guy﻿—look!” He flailed wildly at his loosened bonds, lunged, tottered and toppled, chair and all, crashingly off the platform.

The three possessed ones did not need to hurry. They had all the time in the world. They were already reaching out for the rifles when Chandler shouted. Economically they turned, raising the butts to their shoulders, and began to fire at the Orphalese. It was a queerly frightening sight to see the arthritic organist, with a face like a relaxed executioner, take quick aim at Guy and, with a thirty-thirty shell, blow his throat out. Three shots, and the nearest three of the congregation were dead. Three more, and others went down, while the remainder turned and tried to run. It was like a slaughter of vermin. They never had a chance.

When every Orphalese except themselves was down on the floor, dead, wounded or, like Chandler, overlooked, the arthritic lady took careful aim at Ellen Braisted and the plump youth and shot them neatly in the temples. They didn’t try to prevent her. With expressions that seemed almost impatient they presented their profiles to her aim.

===Chinese===

艾伦·布雷斯特德从双手间抬起脸，泪流不止，悄无声息地站起来跟了上去。那位患关节炎的老妇人也原地转身，一瘸一拐地随他们而去。钱德勒先是茫然瞪着，随即明白了。

他们正朝屋子那头步枪架立的角落走去。“被附身了！”钱德勒吼道，这几个字撕出喉咙时带着酸味。“拦住他们！你——盖伊——看啊！”他疯狂地甩动已松的绳索，猛一挣，踉跄着连人带椅轰然翻下台去。

三个被附身的人用不着着急。他们有的是时间。钱德勒喊出声的时候，他们的手已经伸向步枪。他们干净利落地转过身，枪托抵肩，开始朝奥法利斯人射击。看着那位患关节炎的风琴手——一张松弛得像刽子手的脸——迅速瞄准盖伊，用一发.30-30子弹打爆他的喉咙，是种怪诞而骇人的景象。三枪，会众中离得最近的三个倒地死了。又是三枪，更多人倒了下去，其余的人转身想逃。那简直是对害兽的一场宰杀。他们从头到尾没有过一丝机会。

等到除他们仨以外的每一个奥法利斯人都倒在地上——死了的、伤了的，或是像钱德勒这样被略过的——患关节炎的老妇人便仔细瞄准艾伦·布雷斯特德和胖青年，干净利落地各射中他们的太阳穴。他们没有试图阻止她。他们把侧脸迎向她的枪口，神情里几乎是迫不及待。

===Original===

Then the arthritic lady glanced leisurely about, fired into the stomach of a wounded man who was trying to rise, reloaded her rifle for insurance and began to search the bodies of the nearest dead. She was looking for matches. When she found them, she tugged weakly at the upholstery on a couch, swore and began methodically to rip and crumple pages out of Kahlil Gibran. When she had a heap of loose papers piled against the dais she pitched the remainder of the book out of the window, knelt and ignited the crumpled heap.

She stood watching the fire, her expression angry and impatient, tapping her foot.

The crumpled pages burned briskly. Before they died the wooden dais was beginning to catch. Laboriously the old lady toted folding chairs to pile on the blaze until it was roaring handsomely.

She watched it for several minutes, until it was a great orange pillar of fire sweeping to the ceiling, until the drapes on the wall behind were burning and the platform was a holocaust, until the noise of crackling flame and the beginning of plaster falling from the high ceiling proved that there was no likelihood of the fire going out and, indeed, no way to put it out without a complete fire department arriving on the scene at once.

The old lady’s expression cleared. She nodded to herself. She then put the muzzle of the rifle in her mouth and, with her thumb, pulled the trigger that blew the top of her head off. The body fell into the flames, but it was by then already dead.

===Chinese===

随后患关节炎的老妇人不慌不忙地环顾四周，朝一个正想撑起来的伤员的肚子里开了一枪，又给步枪压上子弹以防万一，然后开始搜查离得最近的尸体的口袋。她在找火柴。找到之后，她有气无力地扯了扯沙发上的软垫面，骂了一句，开始有条不紊地从纪伯伦的书里撕下书页、揉成团。等松散的纸页在讲坛边堆成一小堆，她把书的残部扔出窗外，跪下来点燃了那团纸。

她站在那里看着火，神情又恼又急，一只脚点着地。

揉皱的纸页烧得很旺。不等它们熄灭，木讲坛已经开始着火。老妇人吃力地把折叠椅一把把搬来堆上火，直到火烧得轰轰烈烈、颇为壮观。

她看了它好几分钟，直到它化作一根直扫天花板的巨大橙色火柱，直到后面墙上的帷幔烧起来、整座台子变成一片火海，直到噼啪的火焰声和高顶棚上灰泥开始坠落的动静证明：这火绝无自己熄灭的可能，而且，除非一支建制齐全的消防队立刻赶到现场，也绝无扑灭的办法。

老妇人的神情豁然开朗。她对自己点了点头。然后她把枪口塞进嘴里，用拇指扣动了扳机，轰掉了自己的天灵盖。躯体栽进火焰里，但那时人早已死了。

===Original===

---

Chandler had not been shot, but he was very near to roasting. Walter had released one hand and, while the possessed woman’s attention was elsewhere, Chandler had worked on the other knots.

When he saw her commit suicide he redoubled his efforts. It was incredible to him that his life had been saved, and he knew that if he escaped the flames he still had nothing to live for﻿—that blasted brief hope had broken his spirit﻿—but his fingers had a will of their own.

He lay there, struggling, while great black clouds of smoke, orange painted from the flames, gathered under the high ceiling, while the thunder of falling lumps of plaster sounded like a child heaving volumes of the Encyclopedia Britannica down a flight of stairs, while the heat and shortage of oxygen made him breathe in violent spasms. Then he cried out sharply and stumbled to his feet. It was only a matter of moments before he was out of the house, but it was very nearly not time enough.

Behind him was a great, sustained crash. He thought it must have been the furniture on the upper floor toppling through the burned-out ceiling of the hall. He turned and looked.

===Chinese===

---

钱德勒没有挨枪子，却差一点被烤熟。沃尔特先前已经放开了他一只手；趁被附身的女人注意力在别处，钱德勒一直在磨另外几个绳结。

看见她自杀，他加倍拼命地解。对他来说，自己这条命居然得救了，这是难以置信的；他也知道，就算逃出火焰，他依然没有任何活头——那该死的短暂希望已经击碎了他的精神——可他的手指头自有一股自己的意志。

他躺在那里挣扎，一大团一大团的黑烟——被火焰染成橙色——在高顶棚下聚集；灰泥大块坠落的轰响，听着像个孩子把一册册《大英百科全书》搬起来砸下一道楼梯；高温和缺氧逼得他一阵阵剧烈痉挛地喘息。随后他厉声叫出一嗓子，跌跌撞撞地爬了起来。冲出那栋房子不过是片刻间的事，可那片刻差一点就不够了。

身后传来一声巨大而绵长的轰塌。他想，准是楼上的家具砸穿了烧塌的厅堂顶棚。他回过头去看。

===Original===

It was dark, and now every window on the side of the house facing him was lighted. It was as though some mad householder had decided to equip his rooms only with orange lights, orange lights that flickered and moved. For a second Chandler thought there were still living people in the rooms﻿—shapes moved and cavorted at the windows, as though they were gathering up possessions or waving wildly for help. But it was only the drapes, aflame, tossed about in the fierce heat.

Chandler sighed and turned away.

Pain was not a sure defense after all. Evidently it was only an annoyance to the possessors﻿ ﻿… whoever, or whatever, they might be. As soon as they had become suspicious they had exerted themselves and destroyed the Orphalese. He listened and looked about, but no one else moved. He had not expected anyone. He had been sure that he was the only survivor.

He began to walk down the hill toward the wrecked railway bridge, turning only when a roar told him that the roof of the house had fallen in. A tulip of flame a hundred feet tall rose above the standing walls, and above that a shower of floating red-orange sparks, heat-borne, drifting up and away and beginning to settle all over the mountainside. Many were still red when they landed, a few still flaming. It was a distinct risk that the trees would begin to burn, and then he would be in fresh danger. So great was his stupor that he did not even hurry.

===Chinese===

天黑了，此刻朝他这一面的每一扇窗都亮着。仿佛哪个疯宅主打定主意，给每个房间只装配橙色的灯——忽闪的、晃动的橙色灯。有一秒钟，钱德勒以为屋里还有活人——窗口有人影在动、在腾跃翻滚，像是在收拾细软，或在狂乱地挥手求救。可那只是窗帘，烧着的窗帘，在猛烈的酷热里翻卷飘荡。

钱德勒叹了口气，转过身去。

疼痛终究不是可靠的防御。对那些附主来说，它显然只是件恼人的小事……不管它们是谁，也不管它们是什么。它们一起了疑心，便发了力，把奥法利斯人斩尽杀绝。他听了听，望了望，再没有别人动弹。他本来也不指望有谁。他一直确信，自己是唯一的幸存者。

他开始下山，朝那座毁了铁路桥走去，直到一声轰鸣告诉他房子的屋顶塌了才回头。一朵百英尺高的郁金香状火焰在残壁之上绽放，火焰上方是一场红橙色火星的浮雨，乘着热气，飘升、四散，开始落遍整面山坡。许多火星落地时还红着，几颗还燃着火苗。树木很可能接着烧起来，那时他就会有新的危险。可他恍惚得太深，连脚步都不曾加快。

===Original===

By a plowed field he flung himself to the ground.

He could go no farther because he had nowhere to go. He had had two homes and he had been driven from both of them. He had had hope twice, and twice he had been damned.

He lay on his back, with the burning house mumbling and crackling in the distance, and stared up at the orange-lit tops of the trees and, past them, the stars. Over his left shoulder Deneb chased Vega across the sky; toward his feet something moved between the bright rosy dot that was Antares and another, the same brightness and hue﻿—Mars? He spent several moments wondering if Mars were in that part of the heavens. Then he looked again for the tiny moving point that had crossed the claws of the Scorpion, but it was gone. A satellite, maybe. Although there were few of them left that the naked eye could hope to see. And there would never be any more, because the sort of accumulated wealth of nations that threw rockets into the sky was forever spent.

It was probably an airplane, he thought drowsily, and drifted off to sleep without realizing how remote even that possibility had become.﻿ ﻿… He woke up to find that he was getting to his feet.

Once again an interloper tenanted his brain. He tried to interfere, for he could not help it, although he knew how useless it was, but his own neck muscles turned his head from side to side, his own eyes looked this way and that, his own hand reached down for a dead branch that lay on the ground, then hesitated and withdrew. His body stood motionless for a second, the lips moving, the larynx mumbling to itself. He could almost hear words. Chandler felt like a fly in amber, prisoned in his own brainbox. He was not surprised when his legs moved to carry him back toward the destroyed building, now a fakir’s bed of white-hot coals with brush fires spattered around it. He thought he knew why. It seemed very likely that what possessor had him was a sort of cleanup squad, tidying up the loose ends of the slaughter; he expected that his body’s errand was to destroy itself, and thus him, as all the Orphalese had been destroyed.

===Chinese===

走到一块犁过的田边，他一头扑倒在地。

他再也走不远了，因为他无处可去。他先后有过两个家，先后被逐出这两个家。他先后两次怀过希望，也先后两次被判定沉沦。

他仰面躺着，远处燃烧的房子喃喃地噼啪作响，他仰望着被火光映成橙色的树梢，以及树梢之上的群星。在他左肩上方，天津四正追赶着织女星横渡天穹；朝脚的方向，有个东西在移动，位置在心宿二——一颗明亮的玫瑰色光点——和另一颗同样亮、同样色调的星之间。是火星吗？他花了好一阵琢磨，火星当时是不是在天上的那个方位。接着他再去找那个刚刚横越天蝎螯钳的微小移动光点，它却不见了。也许是颗卫星。虽然如今肉眼还能指望看见的，已经没有几颗了。而且永远不会再有新的了——因为那种能把火箭抛上天的国家积攒的财富，已经永远花光了。

那多半是架飞机吧，他昏昏沉沉地想着，滑入了睡眠，没有意识到就连这种可能，如今都已变得多么渺茫。……他醒来时，发现自己正在站起身来。

又一次，一个擅入者住进了他的脑子。他试图插手——他忍不住不试，尽管他清楚这毫无用处——但他自己的颈肌把他的头从这边拧到那边，他自己的眼睛东看西看，他自己的手伸向地上一截枯枝，随即又迟疑着缩了回去。他的身体静止了一秒，嘴唇翕动，喉头自顾自地咕哝。他几乎能听出词句来。钱德勒觉得自己像琥珀里的苍蝇，囚在自己这具脑壳里。所以当他的双腿带着他走回那栋毁掉的建筑时——它如今是一床苦行僧的白热炭火，四下溅着灌木的小火——他并不惊讶。他自以为知道为什么。附在他身上的这个附主，多半是某种扫尾小队，来收拾这场屠杀的残局；他料想，他这具身体的差事就是毁灭它自己、连带毁灭他，正如所有奥法利斯人已经被毁灭那样。

## IX / 九

===Original===

Chandler had his empty glass halfway to his lips, automatically, before he realized there was nothing in it to brace him. He said hoarsely, “Yes, thanks. Do you come here often?” It was like the banal talk of a language guide, wildly inappropriate to what had been going on a moment before. He was shaken.

“Oh, I love it,” cooed Hsi, investigating the dishes before him. “All finished, I see. Too bad. Your friend doesn’t feel like he ate much, either.”

“I guess he wasn’t hungry,” Chandler managed.

“Well, I am.” Hsi cocked his head and smiled like a female impersonator. “I know! Are you doing anything special right now, love? I know you’ve eaten, but﻿—well, I’ve been a good girl and I guess I can eat a real meal, I mean not with somebody else’s teeth, and still keep the calories in line. Suppose I meet you down at the Beach? There’s a place there where the luau is divine. I can be there in half an hour.”

Chandler’s breathing was back to normal. Why not? “I’ll be delighted.”

“Luigi the Wharf Rat, that’s the name of it. They won’t let you in, though, unless you tell them you’re with me. It’s special.” Hsi’s eye closed in Rosalie Pan’s wink. “Half an hour,” Hsi said, and was again himself. He began to shake.

===Chinese===

钱德勒把空杯半举到唇边，完全是下意识的动作，随后才想起里面已经没有东西可以给自己壮胆了。他哑着嗓子说：“好的，谢谢。您常来这儿吗？”这活像语言手册里的陈腔滥调，跟方才发生的事全然不搭调。他吓坏了。

“哦，我爱极了这儿。”徐柔声说着，低头察看面前的菜碟。“都吃完了呀，我瞧见了。真可惜。你这位朋友看样子也没吃多少。”

“我猜他是没什么胃口。”钱德勒勉强答道。

“可我有。”徐偏着头一笑，笑得像反串的优伶。“有了！你这会儿有什么特别安排吗，亲爱的？我知道你吃过了，不过——是这样，我一直当了个乖姑娘，想来到可以正经吃顿饭了——我是说，不借别人的牙齿吃——而且还不至于超出卡路里。咱们到海滩那边碰头怎么样？有个地方，卢奥宴（luau）做得跟神仙享的一样。我半小时就能到。”

钱德勒的呼吸恢复了正常。有何不可？“我很乐意。”

“码头鼠路易吉（Luigi the Wharf Rat），店名就是这个。不过你要不说你是跟我一起的，他们不会放你进去。是特设的。”徐的一只眼睛合上，眨出罗莎莉·潘式的眼风。“半小时。”徐说，随后又变回了他自己。他开始发抖。

===Original===

The waiter brought him straight whiskey and, pretense abandoned, stood by while Hsi drank it. After a moment he said, “Scares you. But﻿—I guess we’re all right. She couldn’t have heard much. You’d better go, Chandler. I’ll talk to you again some other time.”

Chandler stood up. But he couldn’t leave Hsi like that. “Are you all right?”

Hsi almost managed control. “Oh﻿—I think so. Not the first time it’s come close, you know. Sooner or later it’ll come closer still, and that will be the end, but﻿—yes, I’m all right for now.”

Chandler tarried. “You were saying something about the Society of Slaves.”

“Damn it, go!” Hsi barked. “She’ll be waiting for you.﻿ … Sorry, I didn’t mean to shout. But go.” As Chandler turned, he said more quietly, “Come around to the store tomorrow. Maybe we can finish our talk then.”

===Chinese===

侍者给他端来纯威士忌，也不再作遮掩，站在一旁看徐喝了下去。过了一会儿他说：“吓着你了。不过——我看咱们没事。她听去的应该不多。你最好走吧，钱德勒。改天我再跟你谈。”

钱德勒站起身。可他不忍就这么把徐撂在那儿。“你没事吧？”

徐几乎把持住了自己。“哦——我想没事。你知道，险些露馅也不是头一回了。早晚还会更险，到那时就全完了，不过——是啊，眼下我还成。”

钱德勒还在磨蹭。“你刚才正说到奴隶协会来着。”

“见鬼，快走！”徐厉声道，“她要等你呢。……对不起，我不是有意嚷嚷。可你快走。”钱德勒转身时，他又放低了声音：“明天到店里来。兴许那时候咱们能把话讲完。”

===Original===

---

Luigi the Wharf Rat’s was not actually on the beach but on the bank of a body of water called the Ala Wai Canal. Across the water were the snowtopped hills. A maitre-de escorted Chandler personally to a table on a balcony, and there he waited. Rosalie’s “half-hour” was nearly two; but then he heard her calling him from across the room, in the voice which had reached a thousand second balconies, and he rose as she came near.

She said lightly, “Sorry. You ought to be flattered, though. It’s a twenty-minute drive﻿—and an hour and a half to put on my face, so you won’t be ashamed to be seen with me. Well, it’s good to be out in my own skin for a change. Let’s eat!”

The talk with Hsi had left a mark on Chandler that not even this girl’s pretty face could obscure. It was a pretty face, though, and she was obviously exerting herself to make him enjoy himself. He could not help responding to her mood.

===Chinese===

---

“码头鼠路易吉”其实并不在海边，而是在一片叫阿拉维运河（Ala Wai Canal）的水体岸上。水的那一边是雪顶的山峦。领班侍者亲自把钱德勒引到楼上一张临栏的桌子，他就在那儿等。罗莎莉的“半小时”几乎变成了两个钟头；随后他听见她隔着大厅喊他，用的正是那副曾灌满一千座剧院二楼后排的嗓子，她人还未到，他已起身。

她轻快地说：“抱歉啦。不过你该觉得受宠若惊才是。开车只要二十分钟——可往这张脸上涂抹花了一个半钟头，免得人家看见你跟我在一起替你害臊。唉，难得换回自己的皮囊出门，真好。咱们吃吧！”

与徐的那番谈话在钱德勒心上留下了印子，连这姑娘的漂亮脸蛋也盖不住。不过那确实是张漂亮脸蛋，而且她显然在卖力地哄他开心。他不免被她的情绪带动了。

===Original===

She talked of her life on the stage, the excitement of a performance, the entertainers she had known. Her conversation was one long name-drop, but it was not pretense: the world of the famous was the world she had lived in. It was not a world that Chandler had ever visited, but he recognized the names. Rosie had been married once to an English actor whose movies Chandler had made a point of watching on television. It was interesting, in a way, to know that the man snored and lived principally on vitamin pills. But it was a view of the man that Chandler had not sought.

The restaurant drew its clientele mostly from the execs, young ones or young-acting ones, like the girl. The coronets were all over. There had been a sign on the door: **Kapu, Walihini!** to mark it off limits to anyone not an exec or a collaborator. Still, Chandler thought, who on the island was not a collaborator? The only effective resistance a man could make would be to kill everyone within reach and then himself, thus depriving them of slaves﻿—and that was, after all, only what the execs themselves had done in other places often enough. It would inconvenience them only slightly. The next few planeloads or shiploads of possessed warm bodies from the mainland would be permitted to live, instead of being required to dash themselves to destruction, like the crew of the airplane that had carried Chandler. Thus the domestic stocks would be replenished.

An annoying feature of dining with Rosalie in the flesh, Chandler found, was that half a dozen times while they were talking he found himself taken, speaking words to Rosie that were not his own, usually in a language he did not understand. She took it as a matter of course. It was merely a friend, across the room or across the island, using Chandler as the casual convenience of a telephone. “Sorry,” she apologized blithely after it happened for the third time, and then stopped. “You don’t like that, love, do you?”

===Chinese===

她谈她的舞台生涯，谈演出时的兴奋劲儿，谈她结识过的演艺圈人士。她的谈话从头到尾都在抬名人，倒不是装点门面：名人的世界本来就是她生活着的世界。那不是钱德勒涉足过的世界，可那些名字他都认得。罗西曾嫁给一位英国演员，那人的电影，钱德勒在电视上是特意追着看的。从某种意义上说，得知此人睡觉打鼾、平日主要靠维生素丸过活，挺有意思。可这并不是钱德勒主动想了解的那一面。

这家餐馆的客人多半是执政者，年轻的或者装年轻的，像这姑娘一样。满眼都是头冠。门上曾挂过一块牌子：**卡普，瓦利希尼！（Kapu, Walihini！禁忌，外来人止步！）**标明非执政者及其合作者不得入内。话虽如此，钱德勒想，这岛上又有谁不是合作者呢？一个人能做出的唯一有效抵抗，就是杀光够得着的所有人、再杀了自己，从而剥夺他们的奴隶——而这说到底，也不过是执政者自己在别处干过许多回的事。只会给他们添一点小麻烦。大陆上再运来几飞机、几船被附身的温热躯体，获准活下去就是了，不必像载钱德勒来的那架飞机的机组一样被驱使着自毁。家用的库存便这样得到补充。

钱德勒发现，同本尊的罗莎莉共餐有一桩恼人之处：交谈中间，他有五六次发觉自己被占了，冲着罗西说出一些不是他自己的话，往往还是一种他听不懂的语言。她对此习以为常。那不过是哪个朋友——隔着大厅也罢，隔着整座岛也罢——把钱德勒当成随手可用的电话使唤罢了。“对不起。”第三回发生之后她快快活活地道了歉，随即停了下来。“你不喜欢这个，对吧，亲爱的？”

===Original===

“Can you blame me?” He stopped himself from saying more; he was astonished even so at his tone.

She said it for him. “I know. It takes away your manhood, I suppose. Please don’t let it do that to you, love. We’re not so bad. Even﻿—” She hesitated, and did not go on. “You know,” she said, “I came here the same way you did. Kidnaped off the stage of the Winter Garden. Of course, the difference was the one who kidnaped me was an old friend. Though I didn’t know it at the time and it scared me half to death.”

Chandler must have looked startled. She nodded. “You’ve been thinking of us as another race, haven’t you? Like the Neanderthals or﻿—well, worse than that, maybe.” She smiled. “We’re not. About half of us came from Russia in the first place, but the others are from all over. You’d be astonished, really.” She mentioned several names, world-famous scientists, musicians, writers. “Of course, not everybody can qualify for the club, love. Wouldn’t be exclusive otherwise. The chief rule is loyalty. I’m loyal,” she added gently after a moment, “and don’t you forget it. Have to be. Whoever becomes an exec has to be with us, all the way. There are tests. It has to be that way﻿—not only for our protection. For the world’s.”

Chandler was genuinely startled at that. Rosie nodded seriously. “If one exec should give away something he’s not supposed to it would upset the whole applecart. There are only a thousand of us, and I guess probably two billion of you, or nearly. The result would be complete destruction.”

===Chinese===

“能怪我吗？”他把后面的话咽了回去；尽管如此，自己的口气还是让他吃了一惊。

她替他把话说完了。“我知道。我猜，它夺走的是你的男子气概。别让它把你变成那样，亲爱的。我们没有那么坏。就连——”她迟疑了一下，没有说下去。“你知道，”她说，“我到这儿来的路数，跟你一模一样。是从冬园剧场（Winter Garden）的舞台上被绑走的。当然，不同的是，绑走我的那位是个老朋友。虽然我当时并不知道，还吓得半死。”

钱德勒想必露出了错愕的神色。她点点头。“你们一直把我们当成另一个种族，对不对？像尼安德特人那样——嗯，也许比那还糟。”她笑了。“我们不是。我们约莫一半人本来就是从俄国来的，其余的来自四面八方。你真会大吃一惊的。”她点了几个名字，全是世界闻名的科学家、音乐家、作家。“当然啦，不是人人都入得了这个俱乐部，亲爱的。不然就算不得高等会员制了。头一条规矩就是忠诚。我是忠诚的，”过了一会儿她温柔地补了一句，“你可给我记住了。必须如此。谁要当执政者，就必须跟我们一条道走到黑。是要考验的。非这样不可——不光是为了保护我们。也是为了这个世界。”

这话让钱德勒由衷地吃了一惊。罗西严肃地点头。“哪怕只有一个执政者说漏了不该说的嘴，就会把整盘计划打翻。我们总共只有一千人，而你们，我估摸着差不多有二十亿，或者接近这个数。结果就是彻底的毁灭。”

===Original===

Of the Executive Committee, Chandler thought she meant at first, but then he thought again. No. Of the world. For the thousand execs, outnumbered though they were two million to one, could not fail to triumph. The contest would not be in doubt. If the whole thousand execs at once began systematically to kill and destroy, instead of merely playing at it as the spirit moved them, they could all but end the human race overnight. A man could be made to slash his throat in a quarter of a minute. An exec, killing, killing, killing without pause, could destroy his own two million enemies in an eight-hour day.

And there were surer, faster ways. Chandler did not have to imagine them, he had seen them. The massacre of the Orphalese, the victims at the Monument﻿—they were only crumbs of destruction. What had happened to New York City showed what mass-production methods could do. No doubt there were bombs left, even if only chemical ones. Shoot, stab, crash, blow up; swallow poison, leap from window, slit throat. Every man a murderer, at the touch of a mind from Hawaii; and if no one else was near to murder, surely each man could find a victim in himself. In one ravaging day mankind would cease to exist as a major force. In a week the only survivors would be those in such faroff and hopelessly impotent places that they were not worth the trouble of tracking down.

===Chinese===

执行委员会的彻底毁灭——钱德勒起先以为她指的是这个，随即又想了想。不。是世界的。因为这一千名执政者，尽管以一比两百万居于劣势，却不可能不获胜。这场较量毫无悬念。假如一千名执政者一齐动手，有系统地进行杀戮和破坏，而不是兴之所至地玩上一玩，他们几乎可以一夜之间终结人类。让一个人割开自己的喉咙，用不了一分钟的四分之一。一名执政者杀、杀、杀个不停，干满八个小时的一个班，就能消灭他那两百万个敌人。

而且还有更稳、更快的法子。这些钱德勒不必凭空想象，他都亲眼见过。奥法利斯人（the Orphalese）的大屠杀，纪念碑下的那些牺牲品——那些不过是毁灭的碎屑。纽约市的遭遇显示了批量生产式的手法能办到什么。毫无疑问炸弹还有剩的，哪怕只剩化学炸弹。开枪、捅刀、坠机、爆破；吞毒、跳窗、割喉。人人皆凶手，只消夏威夷的一个心智轻轻一触；要是近旁再没有别人可杀，每个人总归还能在自己身上找出一个受害者。浩劫一日之间，人类作为一个主要势力便不复存在。不出一个星期，仅存的幸存者只会是那些身处如此遥远、如此无望地无能为力之地、以至于不值得费事追索的人。

===Original===

---

“You hate us, don’t you?”

Chandler paused and tried to find an answer. Rosie was not either belligerent or mocking. She was only sympathetically trying to reach his point of view. He shook his head silently.

“Not meaning ‘no’﻿—meaning ‘no comment’? Well, I don’t blame you, love. But do you see that we’re not altogether a bad thing? It’s bad that there should be so much violence. In a way. Hasn’t there always been violence? And what were the alternatives? Until we came along the world was getting ready to kill itself anyway.”

“There’s a difference,” Chandler mumbled. He was thinking of his wife. He and Margot had loved each other as married couples do﻿—without any very great, searing compulsion; but with affection, with habit and with sporadic passion. Chandler had not given much thought to the whole, though he was aware of the parts, during the last years of his marriage. It was only after Margot’s murder that he had come to know that the sum of those parts was a quite irreplaceable love.

===Chinese===

---

“你恨我们，是不是？”

钱德勒顿了顿，想找出一个回答。罗西既没有挑衅，也没有讥诮。她只是怀着善意，想够到他的立场。他默默摇了摇头。

“意思是‘不是’——还是‘无可奉告’？唉，我不怪你，亲爱的。可你看不看得出来，我们并不全然是件坏事？暴力这么多，确实糟糕。从某种意义上说。可暴力不是从来就有吗？再说，除此之外还有别的路吗？我们没来的时候，这个世界反正也正准备自杀来着。”

“总有个分别。”钱德勒含糊道。他想起的是自己的妻子。他和玛戈像天下夫妻那样相亲相爱——没有那种巨大灼人、不由自主的激情；却有感情，有习惯，有间或发作的热烈。婚后最后那几年，钱德勒对整体并没有多想，尽管各个局部他都有数。直到玛戈被杀之后他才明白：那些局部加在一起，是一种无可替代的爱。

===Original===

But Rosie was shaking her head. “The difference is all on our side. Suppose Koitska’s boss had never discovered the coronets. At any moment one country might have got nervous and touched off the whole thing﻿—not carefully, the way we did it, with most of the really dirty missiles fused safe and others landing where they were supposed to go. I mean, touched off a *war*. The end, love. The bloody *finis*. The ones that were killed at once would have been the lucky ones. No, love,” she said, in dead earnest, “we aren’t the worst things that ever happened to the world. Once the﻿—well, the *bad* part﻿—is over, people will understand what we really are.”

“And what’s that, exactly?”

She hesitated, smiled and said modestly, “We’re gods.”

It took Chandler’s breath away﻿—not because it was untrue, but because it had never occurred to him that gods were aware of their deity.

===Chinese===

可罗西摇着头。“分别全在我们这一边。假设科伊茨卡的上司从来没发明头冠呢。任何一个国家都可能随时沉不住气，把整个火药桶点着——可不像我们干得那样小心：真正脏的那批导弹大多保险了引信，其余的也都落在该落的地方。我是说，点着一场*战争*。完结，亲爱的。血淋淋的*终曲*。当场死掉的那些，倒算是走运的。不，亲爱的，”她一本正经地说，“我们并不是这个世界遭遇过的最坏的东西。等那一段——嗯，*坏*的那一段——过去，人们会明白我们真正是什么。”

“那究竟是什么呢？”

她迟疑了一下，微微一笑，谦逊地说：“我们是神。”

钱德勒一时屏住了呼吸——不是因为这话不实，而是因为他从没想过：神居然知道自己是神。

===Original===

“We’re gods, love, with the privilege of electing mortals to the club. Don’t judge us by anything that has gone before. Don’t judge us by anything. We are a New Thing. We don’t have to conform to precedent because we upset all precedents. From now on, to the end of time, the rules will grow from us.”

She patted her lips briskly with a napkin and said, “Would you like to see something? Let’s take a little walk.”

She took him by the hand and led him across the room, out to a sundeck on the other side of the restaurant. They were looking down on what had once been a garden. There were people in it; Chandler was conscious of sounds coming from them, and he was able to see that there were dozens of them, perhaps a hundred, and that they all seemed to be wearing suntans like his own.

“From Tripler?” he guessed.

“No, love. They pick out those clothes themselves. Stand there a minute.”

The girl in the coronet walked out to the rail of the sundeck, where pink and amber spotlights were playing on nothing. As she came into the colored lights there was a sigh from the people in the garden. A man walked forward with an armful of leis and deposited them on the ground below the rail.

They were *adoring* her.

Rosalie stood gravely for a moment, then nodded and returned to Chandler.

“They began doing that about a year ago,” she whispered to him, as a murmur of disappointment came up from the crowd. “Their own idea. We didn’t know what they wanted at first, but they weren’t doing any harm. You see, love,” she said softly, “we can make them do anything we like. But we don’t make them do that.”

===Chinese===

“我们是神，亲爱的，还享有把凡人选举进俱乐部的特权。别拿以往的一切来评判我们。别拿任何东西来评判我们。我们是一件新事物。我们不必遵从先例，因为我们推翻了一切先例。从今往后，直到时间尽头，规则都将从我们身上生长出来。”

她拿餐巾轻快地按了按嘴唇，说：“想看点东西吗？咱们去散个小步。”

她牵起他的手，领他穿过大厅，走到餐馆另一侧的日光露台上。他们俯瞰着一片曾经是花园的地方。里面有人；钱德勒听得见他们发出的声响，也看清了他们有几十个，兴许上百，而且个个身上似乎都穿着跟他那身相仿的黄褐色衣裳。

“从特里普勒来的？”他猜道。

“不是，亲爱的。那些衣裳是他们自己挑的。在那儿站一会儿。”

戴头冠的姑娘走到露台栏杆前，粉红和琥珀色的聚光灯正空空地打在那里。她一走进彩色灯光，花园里的人群便发出一声叹息。一个男人抱着一捧夏威夷花环走上前，把它们安放在栏杆下的地面上。

他们在*崇拜*她。

罗莎莉肃立了片刻，然后颔首，回到钱德勒身边。

“他们大约一年前开始这么干的。”人群里升起一阵失望的低语，她凑在他耳边悄声说，“是他们自己的主意。起初我们不知道他们要干什么，不过他们也没干什么坏事。你明白吗，亲爱的，”她柔声说，“我们能叫他们做我们喜欢的任何事。可我们没叫他们做这个。”

===Original===

---

Hours later, Chandler was not sure just how, they were in a light plane flying high over the Pacific, clear out of sight of land. The moon was gold above them, the ocean black beneath.

Chandler stared down as the girl circled the plane, slipping lower toward the water, silent and perplexed. But he was not afraid. He was almost content. Rosie was good company﻿—gay, cheerful﻿—and she had treasures to share. It had been an impulse of hers, a long drive in her sports car and a quick, comfortable flight over the ocean to cap the evening. It had been a pleasant impulse. He reflected gravely that he could understand now how generations of country maidens had been dazzled and despoiled. A touch of luxury was a great seducer.

The coronet on the girl’s body could catch his body at any moment. She had only to think herself into his mind, and her will, flashed to a relay station like the one he was building for Koitska, at loose in infinity, could sweep into him and make him a puppet. If she chose, he would open that door beside him and step out into a thousand feet of air and a meal for the sharks.

But he did not think she would do it. He did not think anyone would, really, though with his own eyes he had seen some anyones do things as bad as that and sickeningly worse. There was no corrupt whim of the most diseased mind in history that some torpid exec had not visited on a helpless man, woman or child in the past years. Even as they flew here, Chandler knew, the gross bodies that lay in luxury in the island’s villas were surging restlessly around the world; and death and horror remained where they had passed. It was a paradox too great to be reconciled, this girl and this vileness. He could not forget it, but he could not feel it in his glands. She was pretty. She was gay. He began to think thoughts that had left him alone for a long time.

The dark bulk of the island showed ahead and they were sinking toward a landing.

===Chinese===

---

几个钟头后——具体怎么来的，钱德勒说不清——他们已经置身一架轻型飞机上，高高飞在太平洋上空，陆地渺然无踪。头顶的月亮是金色的，脚下的海洋是黑色的。

姑娘驾着飞机盘旋，向海面徐徐溜降，钱德勒俯视下方，沉默而困惑。但他并不害怕。他几乎是怡然的。罗西是个好伴儿——快活、爽朗——而且她有宝贝可以分享。这是她一时兴起：开着她的跑车兜上一大圈，再往海洋上空来一趟轻快舒适的飞行，给这个晚上收个尾。这一时兴起来得叫人愉快。他严肃地想道：他如今算是明白了，一代一代的乡下姑娘当年是怎么被炫惑、又被糟蹋的。奢华的滋味，是了不得的诱奸者。

姑娘身上那顶头冠随时可以把他的身体抓住。她只消把自己想进他的头脑，她的意志——闪送到一座像他正替科伊茨卡建造的那样的中继站，在无穷空间里纵横无碍——就能涌进他体内，把他变成傀儡。只要她愿意，他就会打开身旁那扇门，一步跨进一千英尺的空中，变成鲨鱼的一餐。

但他不认为她会这么做。他其实也不认为有谁会这么做，尽管他亲眼见过一些“什么人”干过跟那一样糟、而且糟得叫人作呕的事。历史上最病态的头脑里没有一种腐坏的邪念，是过去这几年里没有被某个慵懒的执政者施到某个无助的男人、女人或孩子身上的。就在他们飞往这里的当口，钱德勒知道，那些瘫在岛上别墅里穷奢极侈的臃肿躯体，正躁动不安地涌向世界各地；他们所过之处，留下的只有死亡与恐怖。这个姑娘与这种邪恶，是一个大到无法调和的悖论。他忘不掉它，却又无法在自己的脏腑里感觉到它。她漂亮。她快活。他开始想一些很久没来纠缠他的念头了。

岛屿黑黢黢的巨影在前面显现，他们正朝一处降落场沉下去。

===Original===

The girl landed skillfully on a runway that sprang into light as she approached﻿—electronic wizardry, or the coronet and some tethered serf at a switch? It didn’t matter. Nothing mattered very greatly at that moment to Chandler.

“Thank you, love,” she said, laughing. “I liked that. It’s all very well to use someone else’s body for this sort of thing, but every now and then I want to keep my own in practice.”

She linked arms with him as they left the plane. “When I was first given the coronet here,” she reminisced, amusement in her voice, “I got the habit real bad. I spent six awful months﻿—really, six months in bed! And by myself at that. Oh, I was all over the world, and skin-diving on the Barrier Reef and skiing in Norway and﻿—well,” she said, squeezing his arm, “never *mind* what all. And then one day I got on the scales, just out of habit. Do you know what I *weighed*?” She closed her eyes in mock horror, but they were smiling when she opened them again. “I won’t do that again, love. Of course, a lot of us do let ourselves go. Even Koitska. Especially Koitska. And some of the women﻿—But just between us, the ones who do really didn’t have much to keep in shape in the first place.”

She led the way into a villa that smelled of jasmine and gardenias, snapped her fingers and subdued lights came on. “Like it? Oh, we’ve nothing but the best. What would you like to drink?”

She fixed them both tall, cold glasses and vetoed Chandler’s choice of a sprawling wicker chair to sit on. “Over here, love.” She patted the couch beside her. She drew up her legs, leaning against him, very soft, warm and fragrant, and said dreamily, “Let me see. What’s nice? What do you like in music, love?”

===Chinese===

姑娘熟练地降落在一道跑道上，跑道在她临近时骤然亮起灯火——是电子魔法，还是头冠外加哪个被拴住的农奴守着开关？无所谓。那一刻，没有什么事让钱德勒觉得很要紧。

“谢谢你，亲爱的。”她笑着说，“我很尽兴。这种事用别人的身子固然无妨，可我时不时也得让自个儿的身子练练手。”

下飞机时她挽住他的手臂。“我刚在这儿得到头冠那阵子，”她忆旧起来，声音里带着笑意，“这毛病犯得可凶了。我足足赖了六个月——真的，六个月没下床！还是自个儿一个人待着。哦，我可满世界都跑遍了，在大堡礁潜水，在挪威滑雪，还有——唉，”她捏了捏他的手臂说，“别的就*不提*啦。后来有一天，纯粹出于习惯，我往体重秤上一站。你知道我*称出*多重吗？”她夸张地闭眼作惊恐状，再睁开时眼里全是笑。“我再不那么干啦，亲爱的。当然，我们里有不少人确实放任自流。连科伊茨卡都是。尤其是科伊茨卡。还有些女人——不过这话只咱俩说，那些放任的，本来也没多少身段好保持的。”

她领路走进一栋别墅，满屋茉莉和栀子的香气，她一打响指，柔和的灯便亮了。“喜欢吗？哦，我们这儿用的全是最最好的。你想喝点什么？”

她给他们俩各调了一大杯冰凉的酒，又否决了钱德勒相中的那把可以瘫坐的藤椅。“到这边来，亲爱的。”她拍拍身边的沙发。她蜷起腿，偎着他，那样柔软、温热、芬芳，梦悠悠地说：“让我想想。来点什么好呢？你喜欢什么音乐，亲爱的？”

===Original===

“Oh … anything.”

“No, no! You’re supposed to say, ‘Why, the original-cast album from *Hi There*.’ Or anything else I starred in.” She shook her head reprovingly, and the points of her coronet caught golden reflections from the lights. “But since you’re obviously a man of low taste I’ll have to do the whole bit myself.” She touched switches at a remote-control set by her end of the couch, and in a moment dreamy strings began to come from tri-aural speakers hidden around the room. It was not *Hi There*. “That’s better,” she said drowsily, and in a moment, “Wasn’t it nice in the plane?”

“It was fine,” Chandler said. Gently﻿—but firmly﻿—he sat up and reached automatically into his pocket.

The girl sighed and straightened. “Cigarette? They’re on the table beside you. Hope you like the brand. They only keep one big factory going, not to count those terrible Russian things that’re all air and no smoke.” She touched his forehead with cool fingers. “You never told me about that, love.”

It was like an electric shock﻿—the touch of her fingers and the touch of reality at once. Chandler said stiffly, “My brand. But I thought you were there at the trial.”

“Oh, only now and then. I missed all the naughty parts﻿—though, to tell the truth, that’s why I was hanging around. I do like to hear a little naughtiness now and then … but all I heard was that stupid lawyer and that stupid judge. Made me mad.” She giggled. “Lucky for you. I was so irritated I decided to spoil their fun too.”

===Chinese===

“哦……随便什么都行。”

“不对，不对！你该说的是：‘哎呀，当然是《嗨，你好》（*Hi There*）的原版卡司专辑啦。’要不就是我主演过的随便哪部。”她摇头轻责，头冠的尖角映着灯光，闪出金色的反光。“不过，既然你显然是个趣味不高的人，只好我亲自来全套了。”她按了按沙发这头遥控台上的开关，不一会儿，如梦的弦乐便从藏在房间四处的三耳扬声器里流淌出来。那不是《嗨，你好》。“这还差不多。”她困倦地说，过了片刻又说，“飞机上不是很美吗？”

“是很美。”钱德勒说。他轻轻地——却不失坚定地——坐直身子，下意识地去摸上衣口袋。

姑娘叹了口气，直起身来。“要烟？就在你手边的桌上。希望这牌子合你的口味。他们如今只养活着一家大烟厂——还没算上那种可怕的俄国货，全是空气，没有烟。”她用凉凉的手指碰了碰他的额头。“这个你可从没跟我说过，亲爱的。”

那像是一道电击——她的指尖和现实同时触了上来。钱德勒生硬地说：“是我抽的牌子。可我原以为审判的时候你在场来着。”

“哦，只偶尔去去。不雅的段落我全错过了——不过说老实话，我在那儿转悠，图的就是这个。我确实喜欢时不时听点儿不雅的……可我听到的净是那个蠢律师和那个蠢法官。把我气坏了。”她咯咯一笑。“算你走运。我火得不行，索性决定也别让他们玩痛快。”

===Original===

---

Chandler sat up and took a long pull at his drink. Curiously, it seemed to sober him. He said: “It’s nothing. I happened to rape and kill a young girl. Happens every day. Of course, it was one of your friends that was doing it for me, but I didn’t miss any of what was going on, I can give you a blow-by-blow description if you like. The people in the town where I lived, at that time, thought I was doing it on my own, though, and they didn’t approve. Hoaxing﻿—you know? They thought I was so perverse and cruel that I would do that sort of thing under my own power, instead of with some exec﻿—or, as they would have put it, being ignorant, some imp, or devil, or demon﻿—pulling the strings.”

He was shaking. He waited for what she had to say; but she only whispered, “I’m sorry, love,” and looked so contrite and honest that, as rapidly as it had come upon him, his anger passed.

He opened his mouth to say something to her. He didn’t get it said. She was sitting there, looking at him, alone and soft and inviting. He kissed her; and as she returned the kiss, he kissed her again, and again.

But less than an hour later he was in her Porsche, cold sober, raging, frustrated, miserable. He slammed it through the unfamiliar gears as he sped back to the city.

===Chinese===

---

钱德勒坐起身，长长灌了一大口酒。说来奇怪，这口酒倒像让他清醒了。他说：“没什么大不了的。我碰巧强奸并杀死了一个年轻姑娘。天天都有的事。当然，替我动手的是你们的一位朋友，可当时进行到哪一步我一步没落，你若想听，我可以给你逐招逐式地描述一遍。不过，我住的那镇上的人当时以为是我自己干的，他们不答应。行骗——懂吗？他们觉得我变态、残忍到了这个地步：宁可用自己的力气去干那种事，也不劳哪个执政者——或者照他们那种没见识的说法，哪个小鬼、魔鬼、恶魔——来牵线。”

他在发抖。他等着她的回答；可她只是低声说：“对不起，亲爱的。”那神情那样懊悔、那样诚恳，他的怒气竟像来时一样快地退了下去。

他张开嘴，想对她说点什么。没说出来。她坐在那儿望着他，孤单、柔软、动人。他吻了她；她回吻了他，他便又吻，一遍又一遍。

可不到一个钟头，他已经坐进她的保时捷，酒意全消，怒火中烧，欲求受挫，狼狈不堪。他一路上胡乱砸着陌生的排挡，朝城里狂奔。

===Original===

She had left him. They had kissed with increasing passion, his hands playing about her, her body surging toward him, and then, just then, she whispered, “No, love.” He held her tighter and without another word she opened her eyes and looked at him.

He knew what mind it was that caught him then. It was her mind. Stiffly, like wood, he released her, stood up, walked to the door and locked it behind him.

The lights in the villa went out. He stood there, boiling, looking into the shadows through the great, wide, empty window. He could see her lying there on the couch, and as he watched he saw her body toss and stir; and as surely as he had ever known anything before he knew that somewhere in the world some woman﻿—or some man!﻿—lay locked with a lover, violent in love, and was unable to tell the other that a third party had invaded their bed.

===Chinese===

她甩了他。他们吻得越来越炽热，他的双手在她身上游走，她的身体朝他涌动，而就在那时，恰恰在那时，她低语道：“不，亲爱的。”他把她搂得更紧，她二话没说，睁开眼睛，望着他。

他知道那时抓住他的是哪个心智。是她自己的。他僵硬得像块木头，松开她，站起身，走到门口，反手锁上了门。

别墅的灯熄了。他站在那儿，五内如沸，透过那扇巨大、宽阔、空空的窗望着暗影。他看得见她躺在沙发上，而且他眼看着她的身体翻腾、扭动；他确知这一点，就像他从前确知任何事一样：世界上的某个地方，某个女人——或者某个男人！——正与爱人交缠，爱得狂暴，却没法告诉对方，有个第三者闯进了他们的床笫。

===Original===

Chandler did not know it until he saw something glistening on his wrist, but he was weeping on the wild ride back to Honolulu in the car. Her car. Would there be trouble for his taking it? God, let there be trouble! He was in a mood for trouble. He was sick and wild with revulsion.

Worse than her use of him, a casual stimulant, an aphrodisiac touch, was that she thought what she did was right. Chandler thought of the worshipping dozens under the sundeck of the exec restaurant, and Rosalie’s gracious benediction as they made her their floral offerings. Blind, pathetic fools!

Not only the deluded men and women in the garden were worshippers trapped in a vile religion, he thought. It was worse. The gods and goddesses worshipped at their own divinity as well!

===Chinese===

直到腕上有什么东西闪着微光，钱德勒才知道，在开回檀香山的疯狂车程里，自己一直在流泪。开的是她的车。他把车开走会招来麻烦吗？上帝啊，让麻烦来吧！他正巴望着麻烦。他厌恶得发狂、发呕。

比她把他当随手的一剂兴奋剂、一贴催情的触摸来用更糟的是：她认为自己所做的是对的。钱德勒想起执政者餐馆露台下那几十个崇拜者，想起他们向她敬献花环时罗莎莉那雍容的祝福。瞎眼的、可怜的蠢货！

他想，困在邪秽宗教里的拜神者，还不只是花园里那些受了蛊惑的男男女女。更糟的是：连神明和女神们，自己也拜着自己的神性！

## V / 五

===Original===

Chandler’s body carried him rapidly toward the house. Now and then it paused and glanced about. It seemed to be weighing some shortcut in its errand; but always it resumed its climb.

Chandler could sympathize with it, in a way. He still felt every pain from burn, brand and wound; as they neared the embers of the building the heat it threw off intensified them all. He could not be a comfortable body to inhabit for long. He was almost sympathetic because his tenant could not find a convenient weapon with which to fulfill his purpose.

When it seemed they could get no closer without the skin of his face crackling and bursting into flame his body halted.

Chandler could feel his muscles gathering for what would be the final leap into the auto-da-fe. His feet took a short step﻿—and slipped. His body stumbled and recovered itself; his mouth swore thickly in a language he did not know.

Then his body hesitated, glanced at the ground, paused again and bent down. It had tripped on a book. It picked the book up, and Chandler saw that it was the Orphalese copy of Gibran’s *The Prophet*.

Chandler’s body stood poised for a moment, in an attitude of thought. Then it sat down, in the play of heat from the coals. It was a moment before Chandler realized he was free. He tested his legs; they worked; he got up, turned and began to walk away.

===Chinese===

钱德勒的身体载着他快步朝那栋房子走去。它时不时停下四下张望，像是在掂量要不要抄哪条近路；但每次又都继续向上爬。

从某种意义上说，钱德勒倒能体谅它。烧伤、烙伤和伤口的每一分疼痛，他仍然感同身受；离那栋房子的余烬越近，它腾起的热浪就越把这一切烘得加剧。长住的话，他实在不是一具舒适的躯壳。他几乎要心生怜悯了，因为他的那位住客一时找不到一件趁手的兵器来完成任务。

等到了再走近一步、脸上的皮肤就要噼啪爆裂着起火的地步，他的身体停了下来。

钱德勒能感到肌肉正在攒劲，准备完成那纵身投入火刑的最后一跃。他的脚向前迈了一小步——滑了一下。他的身体踉跄着稳住；他的嘴用一种他不懂的语言，浓重地骂了句粗话。

接着，他的身体迟疑起来，朝地面瞥了一眼，又顿了顿，弯下腰去。它被一本书绊了一下。它把书捡起来，钱德勒看清了：那是奥法利斯人那册纪伯伦（Gibran）的《先知》（*The Prophet*）。

钱德勒的身体定定地站了片刻，摆出一副思索的架势。然后它坐了下来，坐在炭火烘来的热浪里。过了一会儿钱德勒才意识到：自己自由了。他试了试腿；还听使唤；他站起身，转过身，朝来路走去。

===Original===

He had traveled no more than a few yards when he stumbled slightly, as though shifting gears, and felt the tenant in his mind again.

He continued to walk away from the building, down toward the road. Once his arm raised the book he still carried and his eyes glanced down, as if for reassurance that it was the same book. That was the only clue he was given as to what had happened and it was not much. It was as though his occupying power, whatever it was, had gone﻿—somewhere﻿—to think things over, perhaps to ask a question of an unimaginable companion, and then returned with an altered purpose. As time passed, Chandler began to receive additional clues, but he was in little shape to fit them together, for his body was near exhaustion.

He walked to the road, and waited, rigid, until a panel truck came bouncing along. He hailed it, his arms making a sign he did not understand, and when it stopped he addressed the driver in a language he did not speak. “*Shto*,” said the driver, a somber-faced Mexican in dungarees. “*Ja nie jestem Ruska. Czego pragniesh?*”

“*Czy ty jedziesz to* Los Angeles?” asked Chandler’s mouth.

===Chinese===

他走出去不过几码远，身子微微一个趔趄，像是换了个挡，那住客便又回到了他的头脑里。

他继续朝远离那栋房子的方向走，往下走向大路。有一回，他的手臂举起还拿在手里的书，眼睛朝下扫了扫，像是确认一下还是不是原来那本。这是他得到的关于所发生之事的唯一线索，而且算不上什么线索。仿佛他体内那股占据他的力量——不管它究竟是什么——曾到过什么地方，把事情想了一遍，也许还向一位难以想象的同伴问了个问题，然后带着一个改换过的目的回来了。随着时间的推移，钱德勒开始接到更多的线索，可他几乎已没有精力把它们拼到一起，因为他的身体已近乎虚脱。

他走到大路边，直挺挺地等着，直到一辆厢式货车颠簸着驶来。他拦住它，双臂打了一个他自己看不懂的手势；车停下后，他用一种自己不会说的语言对司机开了口。“*Shto*，”司机说，是个面色阴沉、穿粗布工装的墨西哥人。“*Ja nie jestem Ruska. Czego pragniesh?*”

“*Czy ty jedziesz to* Los Angeles？”钱德勒的嘴问道。

===Original===

“*Nyet. Acapulco.*”

Chandler’s voice argued, “*Wes na* Los Angeles.”

“*Nyet*.” The voices droned on. Chandler lost interest in the argument and was only relieved when it seemed somehow to be settled and he was herded into the back of the truck. The somber Mexican locked him in; he felt the truck begin to move; his tenant left him, and he was at once asleep.

He woke long enough to find himself standing in the mist of early dawn at a crossroads. In a few minutes another car came by, and his voice talked earnestly with the driver for a moment. Chandler got in, was released, slept again and woke to find himself free and abandoned, sprawled across the back seat of the car, which was parked in front of a building marked Los Angeles International Airport.

===Chinese===

“*Nyet. Acapulco.*”

钱德勒的嗓音争辩道：“*Wes na* Los Angeles。”

“*Nyet*。”两个嗓音嗡嗡地扯个不停。钱德勒对这场争执失了兴味，等到事情不知怎么总算有了结果、他也被塞进货车后厢时，他只觉得松了口气。那阴沉的墨西哥人把他锁在里面；他感到货车开动了；住客离开了他，他随即睡着了。

他醒来了一会儿，发现自己正站在破晓时分的薄雾里的一处十字路口。几分钟后又开来一辆汽车，他的嗓音同司机恳切地交谈了片刻。钱德勒上了车，被放开，又睡了过去；再醒来时，他自由了，也被丢下了，横躺在汽车后座上。车停在一栋楼前，楼上标着：洛杉矶国际机场（Los Angeles International Airport）。

===Original===

---

Chandler got out of the car and strolled around, stretching. He realized he was very hungry.

No one was in sight. The field showed clear signs of having been through the same sort of destruction that had visited every major communications facility in the world. Part of the building before him was smashed flat and showed signs of having been burned. He saw projecting aluminum members, twisted and scorched but still visibly aircraft parts. Apparently a transport had crashed into the building. Burned-out cars littered the parking lot and what had once been a green lawn. They seemed to have been bulldozed out of the way, but not an inch farther than was necessary to clear the approach roads.

To his right, as he stared out onto the field, was a strange-looking construction on three legs, several stories high. It did not seem to serve any useful purpose. Perhaps it had been a sort of luxury restaurant at one time, like the Space Needle from the old Seattle Fair, but now it too was burned out and glassless in its windows. The field itself was swept bare except for two or three parked planes in the bays, but he could see wrecked transports lining the approach strips. All in all, Los Angeles International Airport appeared to be serviceable, but only just.

He wondered where all the people were.

===Chinese===

---

钱德勒下了车，伸着懒腰四处溜达。他发觉自己饿得很了。

视野里一个人也没有。机场场地清清楚楚留着痕迹，显示它也经受了降临在全世界每一处主要通信设施上的同一种毁灭。他面前那栋楼有一部分被砸得坍平，还有烧过的痕迹。他看见几根铝制构件斜捅出来，扭曲熏黑，但仍看得出是飞机部件。显然是一架运输机撞进了楼里。烧空的汽车狼藉地散在停车场和曾经是绿草坪的地方。看得出它们是被推土机推到一边的，但一寸也没有多推——刚够把进场道路清出来为止。

他朝场内眺望时，右边立着一座三条腿的怪建筑，有好几层楼高。它似乎不承担任何实际用途。或许它当年是一家豪华餐馆，像老西雅图世博会（Seattle Fair）的太空针（Space Needle）那样，可它如今也烧空了，窗上没了玻璃。场地本身被扫荡一空，只有停机坪里还泊着两三架飞机，但沿进场跑道他看得见排成一列的运输机残骸。总而言之，洛杉矶国际机场看样子还能用——也只是勉强能用。

他纳闷人都到哪儿去了。

===Original===

Distant truck noises answered part of the question. An Army six by six came bumping across a bridge that led from the takeoff strips to this parking area of the airport. Five men got out next to one of the ships. They glanced at him but did not speak as they began loading crates of some sort of goods from the truck into the aircraft, a four-engine, sweptwing jet of what looked to Chandler like an obsolete model. Perhaps it was one of the early Boeings. There hadn’t been many of those in use at the time the troubles began, too big and fast for short hops, too slow to compete over long distances with the rockets. But, of course, with all the destruction, and with no new aircraft being built anywhere in the world any more, no doubt they were as good as could be found.

The truckmen did not seem to be possessed; they worked with the normal amount of grunting and swearing, pausing to wipe sweat away or to scratch an itch. They showed neither the intense malevolent concentration nor the wide-eyed idiot curiosity of those whose bodies were no longer their own. Chandler settled the woolen cap over the brand on his forehead, to avoid unpleasantness, and drifted over toward them.

They stopped work and regarded him. One of them said something to another, who nodded and walked toward Chandler. “What do you want?” he demanded warily.

“I don’t know. I was going to ask you the same question, I guess.”

The man scowled. “Didn’t your exec tell you what to do?”

===Chinese===

远处货车的声响回答了这个问题的一部分。一辆军用六轮大卡车（six-by-six）颠簸着驶过一座桥——桥从起飞跑道通往机场这片停车区。五个人在其中一架飞机旁下了车。他们朝他瞥了一眼，却没有说话，只顾把一箱箱什么货从卡车往飞机里装。那是一架四发后掠翼喷气机，在钱德勒看来是个过了时的型号。也许是早年的波音（Boeing）之一。出乱子的时候这种机型用着的已经不多：跑短途嫌太大太快，跑远程又太慢，竞争不过火箭。可当然啦，到处都毁了，世界上哪儿也不再造新飞机，它们无疑已是能找得到的最好的了。

这些开卡车装货的人不像是被附身的；他们干活时嘟囔和骂骂咧咧的量都正常，时不时停下来擦擦汗、挠挠痒。他们身上既没有那种咬牙切齿的歹毒专注，也没有那种身子已不归自己所有的人瞪圆了眼的白痴式好奇。钱德勒把毛线帽往下拉了拉，罩住额头上的烙印，免生事端，然后慢慢朝他们蹭了过去。

他们停下活计，打量着他。其中一个对另一个说了句什么，那人点点头，朝钱德勒走来。“你要干什么？”他警惕地喝问。

“不知道。我想，我本来正要拿同一个问题问你呢。”

那人皱起眉。“你的执政者没告诉你该干什么？”

===Original===

“My what?”

The man paused, scratched and shook his head. “Well, stay away from us. This is an important shipment, see? I guess you’re all right or you couldn’t’ve got past the guards, but I don’t want you messing us up. Got enough trouble already. I don’t know why,” he said in the tones of an old grievance, “we can’t get the execs to let us *know* when they’re going to bring somebody in. It wouldn’t hurt them! Now here we got to load and fuel this ship and, for all I know, you’ve got half a ton of junk around somewhere that you’re going to load onto it. How do I know how much fuel it’ll take? No weather, naturally. So if there’s headwinds it’ll take full tanks, but if there’s extra cargo I﻿—”

“The only cargo I brought with me that I can think of is a book,” said Chandler. “Weighs maybe a pound. You think I’m supposed to get on that plane?”

The man grunted noncommittally.

“All right, suit yourself. Listen, is there any place I can get something to eat?”

The man considered. “Well, I guess we can spare you a sandwich. But you wait here. I’ll bring it to you.”

He went back to the truck. A moment later one of the others brought Chandler two cold hamburgers wrapped in waxed paper, but would answer no questions.

===Chinese===

“我的什么？”

那人顿了顿，挠了挠，摇摇头。“唉，离我们远点。这可是一趟要紧的货，懂吗？我猜你没什么问题，不然你也过不了警卫那一关，可我不想让你搅了我们的活儿。麻烦已经够多了。我就不明白了，”他用翻旧账的口气说，“怎么就不能让执政者们提前*告诉*我们一声，他们要接谁进来。这又不会亏了他们！这不，现在我们得给这架飞机装货加油，天知道你是不是在哪儿还堆着半吨破烂，打算也装上去。我怎么知道该加多少油？气象，自然是没有的。要是顶风就得加满油箱，可要是多出了额外载荷，我——”

“我想得起来的、随身带来的唯一一件货，是一本书，”钱德勒说，“分量也许一磅。你觉得我是该上那架飞机的？”

那人不置可否地哼了一声。

“行吧，随你的便。听着，有什么地方能让我弄点吃的吗？”

那人盘算了一下。“嗯，我想我们可以匀你一个三明治。不过你在这儿等着。我给你送来。”

他回到卡车那边去了。过了一会儿，另一个人给钱德勒送来两个蜡纸包着的冷汉堡，但什么问题也不肯答。

===Original===

---

Chandler ate every crumb, sought and found a washroom in the wrecked building, came out again and sat in the sun, watching the loading crew. He had become quite a fatalist. It did not seem that it was intended he should die immediately, so he might as well live.

There were large gaps in his understanding, but it seemed clear to Chandler that these men, though not possessed, were in some way working for the possessors. It was a distasteful concept; but on second thought it had reassuring elements. It was evidence that whatever the “execs” were, they were very possibly human beings﻿—or, if not precisely human, at least shared the human trait of working by some sort of organized effort toward some sort of a goal. It was the first nonrandom phenomenon he had seen in connection with the possessors, barring the short-term tactical matters of mass slaughter and destruction. It made him feel﻿—what he tried at once to suppress, for he feared another destroying frustration﻿—a touch of hope.

The men finished their work but did not leave. Nor did they approach Chandler, but sat in the shade of their truck, waiting for something. He drowsed and was awakened by a distant sputter of a single-engined Aerocoupe that hopped across the building behind him, turned sharply and came down with a brisk little run in the parking bay itself.

From one side the pilot climbed down and from the other two men lifted, with great care, a wooden crate, small but apparently heavy. They stowed it in the jet while the pilot stood watching; then the pilot and one of the other men got into the crew compartment. Chandler could not be sure, but he had the impression that the truckman who entered the plane was no longer his own master. His movements seemed more sure and confident, but above all it was the mute, angry eyes with which his fellows regarded him that gave Chandler grounds for suspicion. He had no time to worry about that; for in the same breath he felt himself occupied once more.

===Chinese===

---

钱德勒连一点碎渣都吃了个精光，在残破的楼里找到一间盥洗室，出来后又坐到太阳底下，看着装货的伙计们干活。他已经相当认命了。看来没打算让他立刻就死，那他不活下去还等什么。

他的理解里还有大片大片的空白，但钱德勒看得清楚：这些人虽然没被附身，却在以某种方式为附身者们效力。这是个叫人厌恶的概念；可转念一想，其中又有叫人宽心的成分。它证明，不管“执政者”是什么，他们极可能是人——或者，就算不确切是人，至少也共有人类那副习性：会靠某种有组织的努力，朝某种目标去干。这是他看到的、与附身者有关的头一桩非随机的现象——不算大规模屠杀与毁灭那类短期战术事务。这让他觉出了——他立刻试图把它压下去，因为怕再遭一次毁人的失望——一丝希望。

伙计们干完了活，却没有走。他们也不来搭理钱德勒，只坐在卡车的荫凉里，等着什么。他打起了盹，又被一阵遥远的噗噗声惊醒——一架单引擎的“艾罗库普”（Aerocoupe）轻快地掠过他身后的楼顶，一个急转弯，顺着停车坪本身利索地小跑着落了下来。

飞行员从一侧爬下来，另一侧两个人小心翼翼地抬下一只木箱，不大，看分量却沉。他们把它安置进那架喷气机，飞行员站在一旁看着；随后飞行员和另一个人上了机组舱。钱德勒不敢肯定，但他有个印象：上飞机的那个卡车伙计，已不再是他自己的主人了。他的动作似乎更笃定、更自信，但最主要的，是伙计们望着他的那种无言而愤怒的眼神，让钱德勒起了疑心。他没工夫为此操心；因为就在同一口气息间，他感到自己又被占据了。

===Original===

He did not rise. His own voice said to him, “You. Votever you name, you fellow vit de book! You go get de book verever you pud it and get on dat ship dere, you see?” His eyes turned toward the waiting aircraft. “And don’t forget de book!”

He was released. “I won’t,” he said automatically, and then realized that there was no longer anyone there to hear his answer.

When he retrieved the Gibran volume from the car and approached the plane the loading crew said nothing. Evidently they knew what he was doing﻿—either because they too had been given instructions, or because they were used to such things. He paused at the wheeled stairs. “Listen,” he said, “can you at least tell me where I’m going?”

The four remaining men looked at him silently, with the same angry, worried expression he had seen on their faces before. They did not answer, but after a moment one of them raised his arm and pointed.

West. Out toward the Pacific. Out toward some ten million square miles of nearly empty sea.

===Chinese===

他没有起身。他自己的嗓音对他说：“你。不管你叫啥名字，拿书的那个家伙！你去把书从你撂的地方拿上，然后上那边的飞机去，明白？”他的眼睛转向那架等候的飞机。“还有，别忘了书！”

他被放开了。“忘不了。”他脱口应道，随即发觉身边早没有人在听他回答了。

他从汽车里取回那册纪伯伦，走近飞机，装货的伙计们一言不发。他们显然知道他在干什么——要么他们也接了指示，要么就是对这种事习以为常。他在轮式舷梯前停了停。“听着，”他说，“你们至少能告诉我，我要去的是哪儿吧？”

留下的那四个人默默地望着他，脸上还是他先前见过的那种又怒又忧的神情。他们没有回答，但过了一会儿，其中一个抬起手臂，指了指。

西边。朝着太平洋的方向。朝着大约一千万平方英里近乎空旷的大海。

===Original===

---

Long before they reached their destination Chandler had reasoned what it must be. He was correct: it was the islands of Hawaii.

Chandler knew that the pilot and his coopted partner were up forward, in the crew compartment, but the door was locked and he never saw them again. Apart from them he was the only living person on the plane.

The plane was lightly loaded with cargo of unidentifiable sorts. In the rear section, where once tourist-class passengers had eaten their complimentary tray meals and planned their vacations, the seats had been removed and a thin scatter of crates and boxes were strapped to the floor. In the luxury of the forward section Chandler sat, stared at the water and drowsed. He seemed to be always sleepy. Perhaps it was the consequence of his exertions; more likely it was a psychological phenomenon. He was beyond worry. He had reached that point in emotional fatigue when the sudden rattle of cannonfire or the enemy’s banzai charge can no longer flood the blood with adrenalin. The glands are dry. The emotions have been triggered too often. Battle fatigue takes men in many different ways, but in Chandler it was only apathy. He not only could not worry, he could not even rouse himself to feel hunger, although the pricking of habit made him get up and search the flight kitchen, unsuccessfully, for food.

===Chinese===

---

离目的地还很远，钱德勒就推想出它必然是哪儿了。他没猜错：是夏威夷群岛（Hawaii）。

钱德勒知道飞行员和他那位被收编的同伴在前头机组舱里，但舱门锁着，他再没见过他们。除了他们俩，整架飞机上活人就只有他一个。

飞机轻载着一批分不清名堂的货物。后舱——从前经济舱的旅客们在这里吃过免费托盘餐、盘算过各自的假期——座椅已被拆掉，稀稀落落几只板条箱和货箱用绑带固定在地板上。钱德勒坐在前舱的豪华座椅里，望着海水打盹。他好像总是犯困。也许是操劳过度的后果；更可能是种心理现象。他已经愁不动了。他的情绪疲劳已到了这个地步：炮声骤响也好，敌军万岁冲锋也罢，都再也激不起血液里的肾上腺素。腺体已经干涸。情绪被扣动得太多次了。战斗疲劳症在每个人身上表现各异，在钱德勒身上却只有冷漠。他不但愁不起来，连饿都懒得觉得饿——尽管习惯的针刺还让他起身去机上厨房里翻吃的，一无所获。

===Original===

He had no idea how much time had passed when the hiss of the jets changed key.

The horizon dipped below the wingtip and straightened again, and he beheld land. He never saw the airfield, only water, then beach, then water again, then a few buildings. Then there was a roar of jets, with their clamshells deflecting their thrust forward to brake their speed, and then the wheels were on the ground. As the plane stopped he felt himself once more possessed. It was no longer terrifying﻿—though Chandler was sure he was doomed.

Without knowing where he was going or why he picked up the ripped book, opened the cabin exit and stepped down onto the rolling steps that had immediately been brought into place. He was conscious of a horde of men swarming around the plane, stripping it of its cargo, and wondered briefly at the rush; but he could not stop to watch them, his legs carried him swiftly across a paved strip to where a police car was cruising.

Chandler cringed inside, instinctively, but his body did not falter as it stepped into the path of the car and raised its hand.

===Chinese===

等喷气机的嘶鸣变了调，他已不知道过去了多久。

地平线沉到翼尖之下，又摆平，他望见了陆地。他始终没看见机场，只看见水，然后是海滩，然后又是水，然后是几栋房子。接着喷气机轰鸣起来，反推的蛤壳门把推力偏折向前，刹住速度，然后轮子落了地。飞机一停，他感到自己又一次被附了身。这不再吓人了——尽管钱德勒确信自己是在劫难逃。

不知要去哪儿、也不知为何，他拿起那本破了的书，打开客舱出口，踏上一副立刻推拢过来的活动舷梯。他觉察到一群人围着飞机蜂拥，正把它卸个精光，还短暂地诧异于这份匆忙；可他停不下来看，他的双腿载着他迅速穿过一段铺过的场地，走向一辆正在巡行的警车。

钱德勒心底里本能地一缩，可他的身体毫不迟疑，一步跨进车道，抬起了手。

===Original===

The police car jammed on its brakes. The policeman at the wheel, Chandler thought inside himself, looked startled, but he also looked resigned. “To de South Gate, qvickly,” said Chandler’s lips, and he felt his legs carry him around to the door on the other side.

There was another policeman on the seat next to the driver. He leaped like a hare to get the door open and get out before Chandler’s body got there. He made it with nothing to spare. “Jack, you go on, I’ll tell Headquarters,” he said hurriedly. The driver nodded without speaking. His lips were white. He reached over Chandler to close the door and made a sharp U-turn.

As soon as the car was moving Chandler felt himself able to move his lips again.

“I,” he said. “I don’t know﻿—”

===Chinese===

警车猛地刹住。方向盘后那个警察，钱德勒在心里暗想，看上去吓了一跳，但也一脸听天由命。“去南门，快。”钱德勒的嘴唇说，他感到双腿载着他绕到了另一侧的车门。

驾驶座旁的座位上还有一名警察。他兔子似的蹦起来抢着开车门，要在钱德勒的身体赶到之前先跳出去。他刚好赶上，一分不多。“杰克（Jack），你走吧，我去报告总部。”他匆匆说。司机点点头，没说话。他的嘴唇发白。他隔着钱德勒探身关上车门，猛地掉了个头。

车一动起来，钱德勒就感到自己的嘴唇又能动了。

“我，”他说，“我不知道——”

===Original===

“Friend,” said the policeman, “kindly keep your mouth shut. ‘South Gate,’ the exec said, and South Gate is where I’m going.”

Chandler shrugged and looked out the window﻿ … just in time to see the jet that had brought him to the islands once more lumbering into life. It crept, wobbling its wingtips, over the ground, picked up speed, roared across taxi strips and over rough ground and at last piled up against an ungainly looking foreign airplane, a Russian jet by its markings, in a thunderous crash and ball of flame as its fuel exploded. No one got out.

It seemed that traffic to Hawaii was all one way.

===Chinese===

“朋友，”警察说，“劳驾闭嘴。‘南门，’执政者说了，南门就是我要去的地方。”

钱德勒耸耸肩，望向窗外……正赶上看见把他送到群岛来的那架喷气机再一次笨重地活了过来。它颤着翼尖在地面上爬行，渐渐提速，吼着碾过滑行道和坑洼的荒地，最后轰然撞上一架模样笨拙的外国飞机——看机徽是架俄国喷气机——炸成一团烈焰火球，燃油轰然引爆。没有一个人出来。

看来，通向夏威夷的航线是单行的。

## VI / 六

===Original===

They roared through downtown Honolulu with the siren blaring and cars scattering out of the way. At seventy miles an hour they raced down a road by the sea. Chandler caught a glimpse of a sign that said “Hilo,” but where or what “Hilo” might be he had no idea. Soon there were fewer cars; then there were none but their own.

The road was a surburban highway lined with housing development, shopping centers, palm groves and the occasional center of a small municipality, scattering helterskelter together. There was a road like this extending in every direction from every city in the United States, Chandler thought; but this one was somewhat altered. Something had been there before them. About a mile outside Honolulu’s outer fringe, life was cut off as with a knife. There were no people on foot, and the only cars were rusted wrecks lining the roads. The lawns were ragged stands of weeds in front of the ranch-type homes.

It was evidently not allowed to live here.

Chandler craned his neck. His curiosity was becoming almost unbearable. He opened his mouth, but, “I said, ‘Shut up.’ ” rumbled the cop without looking at him. There was a note in the policeman’s voice that impressed Chandler. He did not quite know what it was, but it made him obey. They drove for another fifteen minutes in silence, then drew up before a barricade across the road.

===Chinese===

警笛长鸣，车辆四散躲闪，他们呼啸着穿过檀香山市中心，又以七十英里的时速沿海边一条公路疾驰。钱德勒瞥见一块路牌，上面写着“希洛（Hilo）”，可“希洛”是哪里、是什么，他毫无概念。不久车渐渐稀少；再后来，路上就只剩他们这一辆了。

这是一条郊区公路，沿线挤着住宅开发区、购物中心、棕榈林，间或有一个小镇的中心，乱七八糟地撒作一处。美国的每座城市，往哪个方向都有这么一条伸出去的路，钱德勒想；可这一条有些不一样。有什么东西先他们一步到过这里。出了檀香山外围约一英里，生机就像被刀切断了一样。路上没有一个行人，仅有的汽车是一辆辆锈蚀的残骸，靠边停成一列。牧场式住宅前的草坪，成了一丛丛参差的荒草。

这里显然不许可人活下去。

钱德勒伸长脖子。他的好奇心已经几乎按捺不住。他张开嘴，可是“我说了，‘闭嘴。’”警察头也不回地低吼。警察的嗓音里有种什么东西让钱德勒为之一凛。他说不上那是什么，可它让他服从了。他们又默默开了十五分钟，在横贯路面的一道路障前停了下来。

===Original===

Chandler got out. The policeman slammed the door behind him, ripping rubber off his tires with the speed of his U-turn and acceleration back toward Honolulu. He did not look at Chandler.

Chandler stood staring off after him, in bright warm sunlight with a reek of hibiscus and rotting palms in his nostril. It was very quiet there, except for a soft scratchy sound of footsteps on gravel. As Chandler turned to face the man who was coming toward him, he realized he had learned one fact from the policeman after all. The cop was scared clear through.

Chandler said, “Hello,” to the man who was approaching.

He too wore a uniform, but not that of the Honolulu city police. It was like U.S. Army suntans, but without insignia. Behind him were half a dozen others in the same dress, smoking, chatting, leaning against whatever was handy. The barricades themselves were impressively thorough. Barbed wire ran down the beach and out into the ocean; on the other side of the road, barbed wire ran clear out of sight along the middle of a side road. The gate itself was bracketed with machine-gun emplacements.

===Chinese===

钱德勒下了车。警察在他身后砰地关上车门，一个掉头，轮胎撕扯着地面，加速朝檀香山开回去。他没有看钱德勒一眼。

钱德勒站在那里望着车走远，明亮的暖阳下，鼻端满是木槿花和腐棕榈的气味。那里非常安静，只有砂石路面上一种轻而涩的脚步声。当钱德勒转过身来面对朝他走来的人时，他意识到自己到底还是从那警察身上摸到了一个事实：那警察怕到了骨头里。

钱德勒对走近的人说了声：“你好。”

那人也穿制服，但不是檀香山市警察的制服。像美军的卡其夏装，只是没有任何徽记。他身后还有六七个同样装束的人，抽着烟，闲聊着，靠在顺手可及的什么东西上。路障本身修得彻底得吓人。带刺铁丝网沿着海滩一路拉进海里；路的另一侧，铁丝网沿着一条岔路的中央拉出去，一眼望不到头。大门两侧，机枪工事左右环卫。

===Original===

The guard waited until he was close to Chandler before speaking. “What do you want?” he asked without greeting. Chandler shrugged. “All right, just wait here,” said the guard, and began to walk away again.

“Wait a minute! What am I waiting for?” The guard shook his head without stopping or turning. He did not seem very interested, and he certainly was not helpful.

Chandler put down the copy of *The Prophet* which he had carried so far and sat on the ground, but again he had no long time to wait. One of the guards came toward him, with the purposeful movements Chandler had learned to recognize. Without speaking the guard dug into a pocket. Chandler jumped up instinctively, but it was only a set of car keys.

As Chandler took them the look in the guard’s eyes showed the quick release of tension that meant he was free again; and in that same moment Chandler’s own body was occupied once more.

He reached down and picked up the book. Quickly, but a little clumsily, his fingers selected a key, and his legs carried him toward a little French car parked just the other side of the barrier.

===Chinese===

卫兵等走到钱德勒跟前才开口。“你要干什么？”他招呼也不打地问。钱德勒耸了耸肩。“好吧，就在这儿等着。”卫兵说着，转身又要走。

“等一下！我等的是什么？”卫兵头也不回地摇了摇头，脚下不停。他似乎没什么兴趣，也绝不热心。

钱德勒放下那一路拎到这里的《先知》，在地上坐下，不过这回他也没等多久。一个卫兵朝他走来，带着钱德勒已经学会辨认的那种目标明确的步态。卫兵一言不发地把手伸进口袋。钱德勒本能地跳了起来，可掏出来的不过是一串车钥匙。

钱德勒接过钥匙时，卫兵眼中的神情倏然一松，那意味着他又自由了；而就在同一瞬间，钱德勒自己的身体再次被占据。

他俯身拾起那本书。他的手指很快、却略有些笨拙地挑出一把钥匙，他的双腿带着他朝路障那边刚停稳的一辆小小的法国车走去。

===Original===

---

Chandler was learning at last the skills of allowing his body to have its own way. He couldn’t help it in any event, so he was consciously disciplining himself to withdraw his attention from his muscles and senses. It involved queerly vertiginous problems. A hundred times a minute there was some unexpected body sway or movement of the hand, and his lagging, imprisoned mind would wrench at its unresponsive nerves to put out the elbow that would brace him or to catch itself with a step. He had learned to ignore these things. The mind that inhabited his body had ways not his own of maintaining balance and reaching an objective, but they were equally sure.

He watched his own hands shifting the gears of the car. It was a make he had never driven, with a clutchless drive he did not understand, but the mind in his brain evidently understood it well enough. They picked up speed in great, gasoline-wasting surges.

Chandler began to form a picture of that mind. It belonged to an older man, from the hesitancy of its walk, and a testy one, from the heedless crash of the gears as it shifted. It drove with careless slapdash speed. Chandler’s mind yelled and flinched in his brain as they rounded blind curves, where any casual other motorist would have been a catastrophe; but the hand on the wheel and the foot on the accelerator did not hesitate.

===Chinese===

---

钱德勒终于在学习听任自己身体自行其是的技艺了。反正他也无可奈何，于是便自觉地训练自己，把注意力从肌肉和感官上撤出来。这里面有些古怪得让人头晕的难题。每分钟总有上百回，身体会冷不丁晃一下，或手动一下，而他那迟钝的、被囚的头脑便去扳那些不听使唤的神经，想把撑住身体的胳膊肘伸出去，或想跨出一步稳住自己。他已经学会不去理会这些了。栖居在他身体里的那个头脑，自有它一套非他所有的保持平衡、抵达目标的法子，而那些法子同样稳妥。

他看着自己的双手换挡。那是他从未开过的一个牌子，装着一套他弄不懂的无离合器传动，可住在他脑子里的那个头脑显然对它了如指掌。他们一阵一阵地蹿起速来，大把大把地烧着汽油。

钱德勒开始在心里勾勒那个头脑的模样。从走路的迟疑看，它属于一个上了年纪的人；从换挡时无所顾忌的打齿声听，还是个火气暴躁的人。它开起车来毛手毛脚，一味求快。每逢车子拐过盲弯——那种地方，随便撞上哪个别的开车人都是一场浩劫——钱德勒的头脑就在脑壳里叫喊畏缩；可方向盘上的手和油门上的脚，从不迟疑。

===Original===

Beyond the South Gate the island of Oahu became abruptly wild.

There were beautiful homes, but there were also great, gap-toothed spaces where homes had once been and were no longer. It seemed that some monstrous Zoning Commissar had stalked through the island with an eraser, rubbing out the small homes, the cheap ones, the old ones; rubbing out the stores, rubbing out the factories. This whole section of the island had been turned into an exclusive residential park.

It was not uninhabited. Chandler thought he glimpsed a few people, though since the direction of his eyes was not his to control it was hard to be sure. And then the Renault turned into a lane, paved but narrow. Hardwood trees with some sort of blossoms, Chandler could not tell what, overhung it on both sides.

===Chinese===

过了南门，瓦胡岛蓦地荒野起来。

美丽的宅邸依然有，但也有大片大片豁牙似的空地，那里从前有过房屋，如今没有了。仿佛有一个狰狞的规划专员拿着橡皮在岛上巡行过，擦掉小房子、廉价房子、老房子；擦掉商店，擦掉工厂。岛的这一整片，被辟成了一座专属的住宅园林。

这里并非无人居住。钱德勒觉得自己瞥见了寥寥几个人影，不过他的目光朝哪儿看由不得他，很难说准。随后那辆雷诺（Renault）拐进了一条巷道，铺过路面，但很窄。两边高大的阔叶树枝叶交覆，开着什么花，钱德勒辨认不出。

===Original===

It meandered for a mile or so, turned and opened into a great vacant parking lot. The Renault stopped with a squeal of brakes in front of a door that was flanked by bronze plaques: *T.W.A. Flight Message Center*.

Chandler caught sight of a skeletal towering form overhead, like a radio transmitter antenna, as his body marched him inside, up a motionless escalator, along a hall and into a room.

His muscles relaxed.

He glanced around and, from a huge couch beside a desk, a huge soft body stirred and, gasping, sat up. It was a very fat old man, almost bald, wearing a coronet of silvery spikes.

He looked at Chandler without much interest. “Vot’s your name?” he wheezed. He had a heavy, ineradicable accent, like a Hapsburg or a Russian diplomat. Chandler recognized it readily. He had heard it often enough, from his own lips.

===Chinese===

巷道蜿蜒了一英里上下，一拐弯，豁然开朗，是一片空旷的大停车场。雷诺在一扇门前刹住，发出一声轮胎的尖叫，门两侧立着铜牌：环球航空航班电讯中心（T.W.A. Flight Message Center）。

他的身体押着他往里走，上一架停摆的自动扶梯，穿过一条走廊，进了一个房间；钱德勒瞥见头顶一座嶙峋的高塔，像无线电发射塔的天线。

他的肌肉松弛了下来。

他环顾四周，只见书桌旁一张巨大的躺椅上，一大团松软的身体动了动，喘着粗气坐起来。那是个非常肥胖的老人，头发几乎掉光，戴着一顶银色尖刺的头冠（coronet）。

他望着钱德勒，没多少兴趣。“你的名字，是什么？”他喘着气问。他带着一种浓重的、磨灭不掉的口音，像哈布斯堡的遗老，或是俄国的外交官。钱德勒一下就听出来了。这口音他听过不知多少遍——出诸他自己的嘴唇。

===Original===

---

The man’s name was Koitska, he said in his accented wheeze. If he had another name he did not waste it on Chandler. He took as few words as possible to order Chandler to be seated and to be still.

Koitska squinted at the copy of Gibran’s *The Prophet*. He did not glance at Chandler, but Chandler felt himself propelled out of his seat, to hand the book to Koitska, then returning. Koitska turned its remaining pages with an expression of bored repugnance, like a man picking off his arm. He seemed to be waiting for something.

A door closed on the floor below, and in a moment a girl came into the room.

She was tall, dark and not quite young. Chandler, struck by her beauty, was sure that he had seen her, somewhere, but could not place her face. She wore a coronet like the fat man’s, intertwined in a complicated hairdo, and she got right down to business. “Chandler, is it? All right, love, what we want to know is what this is all about.” She indicated the book.

===Chinese===

---

这人名叫科伊茨卡（Koitska），他用他那带口音的喘息说了。他若还有别的名字，也没浪费在钱德勒身上。他用尽可能少的话，吩咐钱德勒坐下、不许出声。

科伊茨卡眯眼打量那册纪伯伦的《先知》。他没有看钱德勒一眼，可钱德勒感到自己被推出座位，把书递到科伊茨卡手里，又坐了回去。科伊茨卡翻着剩下的书页，一脸厌倦的嫌恶，像一个人在自己胳膊上捏拾什么东西似的。他像是在等什么。

楼下有一扇门关上了，片刻之后，一个姑娘走进屋来。

她高挑，深肤色，已不十分年轻。钱德勒被她的美貌打了个正着，确信自己在哪儿见过她，却安放不下这张脸。她戴着一顶和那胖子一样的头冠，编进一个繁复的发式里，而她直奔正题。“钱德勒，是吧？好了，亲爱的，我们想知道的是，这一切到底是怎么回事。”她指了指那本书。

===Original===

A relief that was like pain crossed Chandler’s mind. So that was why he was here! Whoever these people were, however they managed to rule men’s minds, they were not quite certain of their perfect power. To them the sad, futile Orphalese represented a sort of annoyance﻿—not important enough to be a threat﻿—but something which had proved inconvenient at one time and therefore needed investigating. As Chandler was the only survivor they had deemed it worth their godlike whiles to transport him four thousand miles so that he might satisfy their curiosity.

Chandler did not hesitate in telling them all about the people of Orphalese. There was nothing worth concealing, he was quite sure. No debts are owed to the dead; and the Orphalese had proved on their own heads, at the last, that their ritual of pain was only an annoyance to the possessors, not a tactic that could long be used against them.

It took hardly five minutes to say everything that needed saying about Guy, Meggie and the other doomed and suffering inhabitants of the old house on the mountain.

===Chinese===

一种近乎疼痛的宽慰掠过钱德勒的心头。原来他是为这个被弄来的！不管这些人是何方神圣，不管他们用什么法子支配人心，他们对自身无上的权柄并非全然放心。在他们眼里，那凄惨而徒劳的奥法利斯人只代表一种小小的不快——还重要不到构成威胁——不过是件一度碍过事、因而需要查究的东西。钱德勒既是唯一的幸存者，他们便认为值得屈尊劳神，把他运送四千英里，好满足他们的好奇心。

钱德勒毫不迟疑地把奥法利斯人的底细原原本本讲给他们听。他十分肯定，没有什么值得隐瞒的。对死人是不欠情分的；而奥法利斯人临了也用自己的脑袋证明了：他们的苦痛仪式对附身者不过是桩烦恼，算不得一条能长久用来对付他们的计策。

要把盖伊、梅吉，以及山上那栋老宅里其余那些在劫难逃的受苦居民说个明白，用不了五分钟。

===Original===

Koitska hardly spoke. The girl was his interrogator, and sometimes translator as well, when his English was not sufficient to comprehend a point. With patient detachment she kept the story moving until Koitska with a bored shrug indicated he was through.
