Then she smiled at Chandler and said, “Thanks, love. Haven’t I seen you somewhere before?”

“I don’t know. I thought the same thing about you.”

“Oh, everybody’s seen me. Lots of me. But﻿—well, no matter. Good luck, love. Be nice to Koitska and perhaps he’ll do as much for you.” And she was gone.

Koitska lay unmoving on his couch for a few moments, rubbing a fat nose with a plump finger. “Hah,” he said at last. Then, abruptly, “And now, de qvestion is, vot to do vit you, eh? I do not t’ink you can cook, eh?”

===Chinese===

科伊茨卡难得开口。姑娘是他的审问官，有时还兼翻译，每当他那点英语不够领会某个要点的时候。她带着耐心的超然，让叙述源源不断地进行下去，直到科伊茨卡厌倦地耸耸肩，示意问完了。

随后她朝钱德勒微微一笑：“谢啦，亲爱的。我是不是在哪儿见过你？”

“不知道。我也这么想过——关于你。”

“哦，谁都见过我。见过好多回的我呢。不过——唉，不要紧。祝你好运，亲爱的。对科伊茨卡好点儿，说不定他也会同样回报你。”说完她就走了。

科伊茨卡在躺椅上一动不动地躺了片刻，用一根圆滚滚的手指揉着肥鼻子。“哈。”他终于开了口。随即又突兀地说：“那么现在，问题是，拿你怎么办，嗯？我看啊，你不会做饭，嗯？”

===Original===

---

With unexpected clarity Chandler realized he was on trial for his life. “Cook? No, I’m afraid not. I mean, I can boil eggs,” he said. “Nothing fancy.”

“Hah,” grumbled Koitska. “Vel. Ve need a couple, three doctors, but I do not t’ink you vould do.”

Chandler shook his head. “I’m an electrical engineer,” he said. “Or was.”

“Vas?”

“I haven’t had much practice. There has not been a great deal of call for engineers, the last year or two.”

“Hah.” Koitska seemed to consider. “Vel,” he said, “it could be﻿… yes, it could be dat ve have a job for you. You go back downstairs and﻿—no, vait.” The fat man closed his eyes and Chandler felt himself seized and propelled down the stairs to what had once been a bay of a built-in garage. Now it was fitted up with workbenches and the gear of a radio ham’s dreams.

===Chinese===

---

钱德勒以一种出乎意料的清醒意识到：这是在审判他的生死。“做饭？不会，恐怕不会。我是说，煮鸡蛋我还行。”他说，“花哨的就不会了。”

“哈。”科伊茨卡咕哝道。“唔。我们要两三个大夫，可我看你干不来。”

钱德勒摇摇头。“我是电气工程师。”他说，“或者说，从前是。”

“‘从前’？”

“我没怎么干过本行了。这一两年，没什么地方用得着工程师。”

“哈。”科伊茨卡似乎琢磨了一下。“唔，”他说，“兴许……对，兴许我们有个活儿给你。你回楼下去，然后——不，等等。”胖子闭上眼睛，钱德勒感到自己被攫住，推搡着下了楼梯，进了原先一间内置车库的车位。如今那里摆开了几张工作台，装备之精，够无线电发烧友做梦。

===Original===

Chandler walked woodenly to one of the benches. His own voice spoke to him. “Ve got here someplace﻿—*da*, here is cirguit diagrams and de specs for a sqvare-vave generator. You know vot dat is? Write down de answer.” Chandler, released with a pencil in his hand and a pad before him, wrote *Yes*. “Okay. Den you build vun for me. I areddy got vun but I vant another. You do dis in de city, not here. Go to Tripler, dey tells you dere vere you can work, vere to get parts, all dat. Couple days you come out here again, I see if I like how you build.”

Clutching the thick sheaf of diagrams, Chandler felt himself propelled outside and back into the little car. The interview was over.

He wondered if he would be able to find his way back to Honolulu, but that problem was then postponed as he discovered he could not start the car. His own hands had already done so, of course, but it had been so quick and sure that he had not paid attention; now he found that the ignition key was marked only in French, which he could not speak. After trial and error he discovered the combination that would start the engine and unlock the steering wheel, and then gingerly he toured the perimeter of the lot until he found an exit road.

===Chinese===

钱德勒木头木脑地走到一张工作台前。他自己的嗓音对他开了口：“这儿搁着……*对*，这儿是电路图，还有方波发生器的规格书。你知道那是什么吧？把答案写下来。”钱德勒被放开了，手里一支铅笔，面前一本拍纸簿，他写了*是*。“好。那你就给我造一台。我已经有一台了，可我还想要一台。这活儿你在城里干，别在这儿干。去特里普勒（Tripler），他们会告诉你能在哪儿干活、上哪儿弄零件，全套都有。过两天你再来一趟，我看看你造得合不合我的意。”

攥着厚厚一沓图纸，钱德勒感到自己被推到屋外，塞回那辆小车里。问话结束了。

他不知道自己还找不找得着回檀香山的路，可这个问题随即搁下了，因为他发现汽车发动不起来。他自己的那双手当然早就发动过，只是又快又稳，他根本没留意；此刻他才发现，点火开关上的标记全是法文，而法文他一窍不通。试了几番错了几番，他摸清了既能让引擎点火又能打开方向盘锁的组合，然后小心翼翼地绕着停车场的边缘转，终于找到一条出口路。

===Original===

It was close to midnight, he judged. Stars were shining overhead; there was a rising moon. He then remembered, somewhat tardily, that he should not be seeing stars. The lane he had come in on had been overhung on both sides with trees.

A few minutes later he realized he was quite lost.

Chandler stopped the car, swore feelingly, got out and looked around.

There was nothing much to see. The roads bore no markers that made sense to him. He shrugged and rummaged through the glove compartment on the chance of a map; there was none, but he did find what he had almost forgotten, a half-empty pack of cigarettes. It had been﻿—he counted﻿—nearly a week since he had smoked. He lit up.

===Chinese===

他估摸着已近午夜。头顶星光闪烁；月亮正在升起。他这才有些后知后觉地想起来：他本不该看得见星星。他进来时走的那条巷道，两边都是交覆的树木。

几分钟后，他发现自己彻底迷了路。

钱德勒停下车，真切地咒骂了一通，下车四下张望。

没什么可看的。路上的标记他一个也看不明白。他耸耸肩，翻检杂物箱，指望能摸出一张地图；地图没有，倒翻出了他几乎忘了的那样东西——半包抽剩的香烟。他算了算——他已经有将近一个星期没抽烟了。他点上一支。

===Original===

---

It was a pleasant evening, too. He felt almost relaxed. He stood there, wondering just what might be about to happen next﻿—with curiosity more than fear﻿—and then he felt a light touch at his mind.

It was nothing, really. Or nothing that he could quite identify. It was though he had been nudged. It seemed that someone was about to usurp his body again, but that did not develop.

As he had about decided to forget it and get back in the car he saw headlights approaching.

===Chinese===

---

这也是个宜人的夜晚。他几乎觉得放松。他站在那里，揣想着接下来会发生什么——好奇多于恐惧——随后，他感到心灵被轻轻碰了一下。

那其实什么也不是。或者说，不是什么他能辨认清楚的东西。就像被人用胳膊肘轻轻搡了一下。似乎有谁又要征用他的身体，却终究没有下文。

他刚打算不理它、回到车上去，就看见有车灯驶近。

===Original===

A low, lean sports car slowed as it came near, stopping beside him, and a girl leaned out, almost invisible in the darkness. “There you are, love,” she said cheerfully. “Thought I spotted someone. Lost?”

She had a coronet, and Chandler recognized her. It was the girl who had interrogated him. “I guess I am,” he admitted.

The girl leaned forward. “Come in, dear. Oh, that thing? Leave it here, the silly little bug.” She giggled as they drove away from the Renault. “Koitska wouldn’t like you wandering around. I guess he decided to give you a job?”

“How did you know?”

===Chinese===

一辆低矮的跑车驶近时减了速，停在他身旁，一个姑娘探出身来，在黑暗里几乎看不见。“你可在这儿呢，亲爱的。”她快活地说，“我觉着像是有个人。迷路了？”

她戴着头冠，钱德勒认出了她。正是审问过他的那个姑娘。“怕是迷了。”他承认。

姑娘俯身向前。“上车吧，亲爱的。哦，那玩意儿？就撂在这儿，那只傻乎乎的小甲虫。”驶离那辆雷诺时，她咯咯直笑。“科伊茨卡可不愿意你到处乱逛。我猜，他决定给你活儿干了？”

“你怎么知道的？”

===Original===

She said softly, “Well, love, you’re here, you know. Otherwise﻿—never mind. What are you supposed to be doing?”

“Going to Tripler, whatever that is. In Honolulu, I guess. Then I have to build some radio equipment.”

“Tripler’s actually on the other side of the city. I’ll take you to the gate; then you tell them where you want to go. They’ll take care of it.”

“I don’t have any money for fare.”

===Chinese===

她轻声说：“这个嘛，亲爱的，你这不是好端端的嘛。要不然——算了，不要紧。他们叫你去干什么？”

“去特里普勒，管它是什么地方。我猜是在檀香山。然后我得装一台什么无线电设备。”

“特里普勒其实是在城的另一头。我送你到大门口；然后你告诉他们你想去哪儿。他们会安排的。”

“我一分车钱也没有。”

===Original===

She laughed. After a moment she said, “Koitska’s not the worst. But I’d mind my step if I were you, love. Do what he says, the best you can. You never know. You might find yourself very fortunate.﻿…”

“I already think that. I’m alive.”

“Why, love, that point of view will take you far.” The sports car slid smoothly to a stop at the barricade and, in the floodlights above the machine-gun nests, she looked more closely at Chandler. “What’s that on your forehead, dear?”

Somehow the woolen cap had been lost. “A brand,” he said shortly. “*H* for ‘hoaxer.’ I did something when one of you people had me, and they thought I’d done it on my own.”

“Why﻿—why, this is wonderful!” the girl said excitedly. “No wonder I thought I’d seen you before. Don’t you remember? I was in the forewoman at your trial!”

===Chinese===

她笑了。过了一会儿她说：“科伊茨卡不算最坏的。不过我要是你，就会处处留神，亲爱的。他怎么说，你就怎么干，尽力干好。说不准的。说不定你会发现自己运气好得很。……”

“我已经这么想了。我还活着。”

“哎呀，亲爱的，这种人生态度会让你受益无穷。”跑车平稳地滑行到路障前停下，在机枪工事上方一排探照灯的强光里，她凑近端详钱德勒。“你额头上是什么，亲爱的？”

不知怎么，那顶绒线帽丢了。“烙印。”他简短地说。“*H*，‘骗子’的意思。你们哪位借着我的身体干过一档子事，他们却以为是我自己干的。”

“哎呀——哎呀，这可太妙了！”姑娘兴奋地说，“难怪我觉得见过你。你不记得了？你受审那会儿，我就是陪审团团长啊！”

## VII / 七

===Original===

A pink and silver bus let Chandler off at Fort Street in downtown Honolulu and he walked a few blocks to the address he had been given. The name of the place was Parts ’n Plenty. He found it easily enough. It was a radio parts store; by the size of it, it had once been a big, well-stocked one; but now the counters were almost bare.

A thin-faced man with khaki-colored skin looked up and nodded. Chandler nodded back. He fingered a bin of tuning knobs, hefted a coil of two-strand antenna wire and said, “A fellow at Tripler told me to come here to pick up equipment, but I’m damned if I know what I’m supposed to do when I locate it. I don’t have any money.”

The dark-skinned man got up and came over to him. “Figured you for a mainlander. No sweat. Have you got a list?”

“I can make one.”

“All right. Catalogues on the table behind you, if you want them.” He offered Chandler a cigarette and sat against the edge of the counter, reading over Chandler’s shoulder. “Ho,” he said suddenly. “Koitska’s square-wave generator again, right?” Chandler admitted it, and the man grinned. “Every couple months he sends somebody along. He doesn’t really need the generator, you know. He just wants to see how much you know about building it, Mr.﻿—?”

“Chandler.”

===Chinese===

一辆粉银两色的公共汽车把钱德勒放在檀香山市中心的福特街（Fort Street），他走了几个街区，来到人家给他的那个地址。这地方名叫“零件多多”（Parts ’n Plenty）。他没费什么事就找到了。那是一家无线电零件店；从规模看，从前曾是家货色齐全的大店；可如今柜台几乎空了。

一个瘦脸、卡其色皮肤的男人抬头点点头。钱德勒也点头回礼。他拨弄着一格旋钮，掂了掂一卷双股天线线，开口道：“特里普勒有个人让我上这儿来取设备，可设备取到了该干什么，我是一概不知。我又没有钱。”

那深色皮肤的男人起身走了过来。“一看你就是大陆来的。没事。你有清单吗？”

“我可以现列一张。”

“行。要的话，目录在你身后桌上。”他递给钱德勒一支烟，靠着柜台边坐下，越过钱德勒的肩膀看他列单。“嚯，”他忽然说，“又是科伊茨卡的方波发生器，对吧？”钱德勒承认了，那人咧嘴一笑。“隔上两三个月他就派个人来。其实他用不着那台发生器，你知道吧。他不过是想看看你对其中的门道懂多少，您贵姓——？”

“钱德勒。”

===Original===

“Glad to know you. I’m John Hsi. But don’t go easy on the job just because it’s a waste of time, Chandler; it could be pretty important to you.”

Chandler absorbed the information silently and handed over his list. The man did not look at it. “Come back in about an hour,” he said.

“I won’t have any money in an hour, either.”

“Oh, that’s all right. I’ll put it on Koitska’s bill.”

Chandler said frankly, “Look, I don’t know what’s going on. Suppose I came in and picked up a thousand dollars’ worth of stuff, would you put that on the bill, too?”

“Certainly,” said Hsi optimistically. “You thinking about stealing them? What would you do with them?”

“Well.﻿ …” Chandler puffed on his cigarette. “Well, I could﻿—”

“No, you couldn’t. Also, it wouldn’t pay, believe me,” Hsi said seriously. “If there is one thing that doesn’t pay, it is cheating on the Exec.”

===Chinese===

“幸会。我是约翰·徐（John Hsi）。不过，钱德勒，别因为这是白费工夫就糊弄这活儿；它对你没准儿相当要紧。”

钱德勒默默把这信息咽了下去，把清单递过去。那人看也没看。“约莫一个钟头后再来。”他说。

“过一个钟头我也还是没钱。”

“哦，没事。我记到科伊茨卡的账上。”

钱德勒坦白地说：“听着，我压根不知道这是怎么回事。假如我进店搬走一千美元的货，你也照记在账上？”

“当然。”徐乐呵呵地说，“你在琢磨着偷？偷了你拿它们干什么？”

“呃。……”钱德勒喷了口烟。“呃，我可以——”

“不，你干不成。再说也不合算，信我的。”徐正色道，“天下要说不合算的事，头一件就是欺蒙执政者（the Exec）。”

===Original===

“Now, that’s another good question,” said Chandler. “Who is the Exec?”

Hsi shook his head. “Sorry. I don’t know you, Chandler.”

“You mean you’re afraid even to answer a question?”

“You’re damned well told I am. Probably nobody would mind what I might tell you﻿ … but ‘probably’ isn’t good enough.”

Exasperated, Chandler said, “How the devil am I supposed to know what to do next? So I take all this junk back to my room at Tripler and solder up the generator﻿—then what?”

“Then Koitska will get in touch with you,” Hsi said, not unkindly. “Play it as it comes to you, Chandler, that’s the best advice I can offer.” He hesitated. “Koitska’s not the worst of them,” he said; and then, daringly, “and maybe he’s not the best, either. Just do whatever he told you. Keep on doing it until he tells you to do something else. That’s all. I mean, that’s all the advice I can give you. Whether it’s going to be enough to satisfy Koitska is something else again.”

===Chinese===

“这倒又是个好问题，”钱德勒说，“执政者到底是谁？”

徐摇了摇头。“抱歉。我跟你又不熟，钱德勒。”

“你是说，连回答一个问题你都怕？”

“你还真说对了，我就是怕。我告诉你的话，多半没人会来计较……可‘多半’不够保险。”

钱德勒恼了：“那我到底怎么知道下一步该干什么？我就把这些破烂搬回特里普勒的房间，把发生器焊起来——然后呢？”

“然后科伊茨卡会来找你。”徐说，语气不算不友善。“来什么接什么，钱德勒，我能给的最佳忠告就是这个。”他迟疑了一下。“科伊茨卡不是他们里头最糟的，”他说；接着又斗胆添了一句，“兴许也不是最好的。他叫你干什么，你就干什么。一直干下去，直到他叫你干别的为止。就这样。我是说，我能给的意见就这么多。至于这些够不够让科伊茨卡满意，那是另一回事了。”

===Original===

---

There is not much to do in a strange town when you have no money. Chandler’s room at what once had been Tripler General Hospital was free; the bus was free; evidently all the radio parts he could want were also free. But he did not have the price of a cup of coffee or a haircut in the pockets of the suntan slacks the desk man at Tripler had issued him. He wandered around the streets of Honolulu, waiting for the hour to be up.

At Tripler a doctor had also examined his scar and it was now concealed under a neat white bandage; he had been fed; he had bathed; he had been given new clothes. Tripler was a teeming metropolis in itself, a main building some ten stories high, a scattering of outbuildings connected to it by covered passages, with thousands of men and women busy about it. Chandler had spoken to a good many of them in the hour after waking up and before boarding the bus to Honolulu, and none of them had been free with information either.

Honolulu had not suffered greatly under the rule of the Exec. Remembering the shattered stateside cities, Chandler thought that this one had been spared nearly all the suffering of the rule of the world by the Exec, whoever they were. Dawdling down King Street, in the aromatic reek of the fish markets, Chandler could have thought himself in any port city before the grisly events of that Christmas when the planet went possessed. Crabs waved sluggishly at him from bins. Great pink-scaled fish rested on nests of ice, waiting to be sold. Smells of frying food came from half a dozen restaurants. It was only the people who were different. There was a solid sprinkling of those who, like himself, were dressed in insigneless former Army uniforms﻿—obviously conscripts on Exec errands﻿—and a surprising minority who, from overheard snatches of conversation, had come from countries other than the U.S.A. Russian mostly, Chandler guessed; but Russian or U.S., wearing suntans or aloha shirts, everyone he saw was marked by the visible signs of strain. There was no laughter.

===Chinese===

---

身无分文的时候，在陌生的城里没什么可干的。钱德勒在特里普勒总医院（Tripler General Hospital）旧址里的房间是免费的；公共汽车免费；显然，他想要的全部无线电零件也免费。可是在特里普勒前台发给他的那条黄褐色便裤的口袋里，连一杯咖啡或一次理发的钱都没有。他在檀香山的街上游荡，等着那一个钟头熬过去。

在特里普勒，还有医生查过他的伤疤，如今它掩在一块干净的白绷带底下；他吃过饭；洗过澡；领了新衣裳。特里普勒本身就是一座熙熙攘攘的都市：主楼约莫十层高，零星散布的附属楼群由带顶的廊道与它相连，成千上万的男男女女在楼里楼外忙碌。从醒来到登上进城的公共汽车之间的那一个钟头里，钱德勒跟其中不少人搭过话，没有一个肯痛快吐露消息的。

檀香山在执政者的治下没吃多大的苦。想起大陆上那些砸烂的城市，钱德勒觉得，这一座几乎躲过了执政者——不管他们是谁——统治世界的全部苦难。沿着国王街（King Street）信步而下，浸在鱼市场浓烈的腥香里，钱德勒恍惚觉得自己置身于随便哪一座港口城市——在那个圣诞节、这颗行星集体中邪之前。螃蟹在货筐里有气无力地朝他挥螯。大条粉鳞的鱼卧在冰窝上待售。六七家餐馆里飘出煎炸食物的香味。只有人是不同的。像他一样穿无徽记旧军装的人扎实散在人群里——显然是被拉来给执政者跑腿的壮丁——还有人数出人意料的一小撮，从偷听来的只言片语判断，来自美国之外的国家。多半是俄国人，钱德勒猜；可不管是俄国人还是美国人，穿黄褐衣裤还是阿罗哈衬衫，他看到的每个人都带着肉眼可见的紧绷。没有笑声。

===Original===

Chandler saw a clock within the door of a restaurant; half an hour still to kill. He turned and wandered up, away from the water, toward the visible bulk of the hills; and in a moment he saw what made Honolulu’s collective face wear its careworn frown.

It was an open square﻿—perhaps it had once been a war memorial﻿—and in the center of it was a fenced-off paved area where people seemed to be resting. It struck Chandler as curious that so many persons should have decided to take a nap on what surely was an uncomfortable bed of flat concrete; he approached and saw that they were not resting. Not only his eyes but his ears conveyed the message﻿—and his nose, too, for the mild air was fetid with blood and rot.

These were not sleeping men and women. Some were dead; some were unconscious; all were maimed. The pavement was slimed with their blood. None had the strength to scream, but several were moaning and even some of the unconscious ones gasped like the breathing of a man in diabetic coma. Passersby walked briskly around the metal fence, and if their glances were curious it was at Chandler they looked, not at the tortured wrecks before them. He understood that the sight of the dying men and women was familiar﻿—was painful﻿—and thus was ignored; it was himself who was the curiosity, for staring at them. He turned and fled, trying not to vomit.

===Chinese===

钱德勒看见一家餐馆门里挂着钟；还有半个钟头要打发。他转身朝上逛，背向海边，朝看得见的山影走去；不多时，他就看见了让檀香山整张脸挂满愁容的东西。

那是一处开阔的广场——从前也许是一座战争纪念碑——广场中央围起一片铺砌的场地，里面的人像在歇息。钱德勒觉得奇怪：怎么会有这么多人决定在平光的混凝土这么张肯定硌人的床上打盹；他走近了，看清他们不是在歇息。把消息传给他的不只是眼睛，还有耳朵——还有鼻子，因为温润的空气里充满了血和腐臭。

这些不是睡着了的男男女女。有的已经死了；有的不省人事；全都残了。路面被他们的血浸得滑腻。没有一个还有力气尖叫，但有几个在呻吟，连一些昏迷的人也在喘息，像糖尿病昏迷病人的那种呼吸。行人绕着铁栅栏快步走过，他们的目光若说好奇，落在的也是钱德勒身上，而不是面前那些受刑的残躯。他明白了：这些垂死男女的景象是大家看惯了的——看着心痛——于是就视而不见；反倒是他自己成了稀罕，因为他盯着看。他转身逃开，强忍着不吐出来。

===Original===

---

He was still shaken when he returned to Parts ’n Plenty. The hour was up but Hsi shook his head. “Not yet. You can sit down over there if you like.” Chandler slumped into the indicated swivel chair and stared blankly at the wall. This was far worse than anything he had seen stateside. The random terror of murders and bombs was at least a momentary thing, and when it was done it was done. This was sustained torture. He buried his head in his hands and did not look up until he heard the sound of a door opening.

Hsi, his face somehow different, was manipulating a lever on the outside of a door while a man inside, becoming visible as the door opened, was doing the same from within. It looked as though the lock on the door would not work unless both levers operated; and the man on the inside, whom Chandler had not seen before, was dressed, oddly, only in bathing trunks. His face wore the same expression as Hsi’s. Chandler guessed (with practice it was becoming easy!) that both were possessed.

The man inside wheeled out two shopping carts loaded with electronic equipment of varying kinds, wordlessly received some empty ones from Hsi; and the door closed on him again.

Hsi tugged the lever down, turned, blinked and said, “All right, Chandler. Your stuff’s here.”

===Chinese===

---

回到“零件多多”时，他还没缓过来。钟点已到，徐却摇了摇头。“还没好。愿意的话，去那边坐坐。”钱德勒瘫进他指的那张转椅，直勾勾望着墙。这比他在大陆见过的任何场面都糟得多。凶杀和炸弹那种随机降临的恐怖，至少是一时的事，过去就过去了。这是绵延不绝的酷刑。他把头埋进双手，直到听见一扇门开启的声音才抬起来。

徐——他的脸不知怎么变了样——正扳动一扇门外侧的操纵杆，门里还有个男人，随着门开显露出来，也在里面扳着同样的杆。看样子，这门的锁非得两根杆同时动作才开得了；里头那人钱德勒从没见过，古怪的是，他只穿了一条泳裤。他的脸同徐的表情如出一辙。钱德勒猜（练得多了，如今这已经容易了！）：两人都被附身了。

里面那人推出两辆装满各式电子设备的购物推车，无声地从徐手里接过几辆空车；门又在他身后合上了。

徐把操纵杆拉到底，转过身，眨了眨眼，说：“好了，钱德勒。你的东西到了。”

===Original===

Chandler approached. “What was that all about?”

“Go to hell!” Hsi said with sudden violence. “I﻿—Oh never mind. Sorry. But I told you already, ask somebody else your questions, not me.” He gloomily began to pack the items on Chandler’s list into a cardboard carton. Then he glanced at Chandler and said, apologetically, “These are tough times, buddy. I guess there’s no harm in answering *some* questions. You want to know why most of my stock’s locked behind an armor-plate door? Well, you ought to be able to figure that out for yourself, anyway. The Exec doesn’t like to have people playing with radios. Bert stays in the stockroom; I stay out here; twice a day the bosses open the door and we fill whatever orders they’ve approved. A little rough on Bert, of course. It’s a ten-hour day in the stockroom for him, and nothing to do. But it could be worse. Oh, that’s for sure, friend: It could be worse.”

“Why the bathing suit? Hot in there?”

“Hot for Bert if they think he’s smuggling stuff out,” said Hsi. “You been here long enough to see the Monument yet?”

Chandler shook his head, then grimaced. “You mean up about three blocks that way? Where the people﻿—?”

===Chinese===

钱德勒走近。“刚才是怎么一回事？”

“见你的鬼去！”徐猝然发作道，“我——噢，算了。对不起。可我说过了，有问题问别人去，别问我。”他闷闷地动手把钱德勒清单上的物件往一只纸箱里装。随后他瞥了钱德勒一眼，带着歉意说：“世道艰难哪，伙计。我想，回答*某些*问题总没大碍。你想知道我大部分货为什么锁在装甲板门后头？唉，这个你自己也该琢磨得出来。执政者不喜欢有人摆弄无线电。伯特（Bert）守着库房；我守在外头；一天两回，上头的把门打开，我们照他们核准的订单发货。对伯特当然狠了点。他一天在库房里待十个钟头，还没事可干。可总还有更糟的。哦，那是一定的，朋友：总还有更糟的。”

“干吗穿泳裤？里头热？”

“要是他们疑心伯特往外夹带东西，那他就‘热’了。”徐说，“来了这些时候，纪念碑（the Monument）你见过了吧？”

钱德勒摇头，随即脸色一变。“你是说从这儿往那边三个街区？人躺在那儿的地方——？”

===Original===

“That’s right,” said Hsi admiringly, “three blocks mauka from here, where the people﻿—Where the people are serving as a very good object lesson to you and me. About a dozen there, right? Small for this time of year, Chandler. Usually there are more. Notice anything special about them?”

“They were butchered! Some of them looked like their legs had been burned right off. Their eyes gouged out, their faces﻿—” Chandler brought up sharply. It had been bad enough looking at those wretched, writhing semi-cadavers; he did not want to talk about them.

The parts man nodded seriously. “Sometimes there are more, and sometimes they’re worse hurt than that. Have you got any idea how they get that way? They do it to themselves, that’s how. My own brother was out there for a week, last Statehood Day. He jumped feet first into a concrete mixer, and it took him seven days to die after I put him on my shoulder and carried him out there. I didn’t like it, of course, but I didn’t exactly have any choice; I wasn’t running my own body at the time. Neither was he when he jumped. He was made to do it, because he used to have Bert’s job and he thought he’d take a little short-wave set home. Like I said, you don’t want to cheat on the Exec because it doesn’t pay.”

“But what the devil am I supposed to﻿—”

Hsi held up his hand. “Don’t ask me how to keep out of that Monument bunch, Chandler. *I* don’t know. Do what you’re told and don’t do anything you aren’t told to do; that is the whole of the law. Now do me a favor and get out of here so I can pack up these other orders.” He turned his back on Chandler.

===Chinese===

“正是。”徐语气里带着赞叹，“从这儿往山那头（mauka）三个街区，人在那儿——在那儿的人，正给你我充当一份绝佳的活教材。那儿有十来个，对吧？对这个季节来说算少的了，钱德勒。通常更多。他们身上有什么特别的地方，你留意到没有？”

“他们是遭了宰割！有些人看着像两条腿被整个烧掉了。眼睛被抠了出来，脸——”钱德勒猛地收住了话头。光是看着那些可怜的、抽搐的半具尸体已经够受的了；他不想谈论他们。

零件商郑重地点点头。“有时候人更多，有时候伤得比那还重。你知道他们是怎么落到那步田地的吗？是他们自己下的手，就这么回事。去年建州日（Statehood Day），我亲兄弟在那儿躺了一个礼拜。他双脚朝前跳进了混凝土搅拌机；我把他扛上肩膀、背到那儿之后，他足足熬了七天才死。我当然不愿意，可我实在没什么可选的；那时候使唤我身子的不是我。他跳的时候也不是他。他是被逼着干的，因为他从前干的是伯特这份差事，动了心思，想带一台小短波机回家。跟我说的那样：别欺蒙执政者，因为不合算。”

“可我究竟该怎么——”

徐举起手。“别问我怎么才能不进纪念碑那堆人里去，钱德勒。*我*不知道。叫你做的做，没叫你做的一概不做；这就是全部的法律。行了，帮个忙，出去吧，我好把这些别的订单装了。”他把背转向了钱德勒。

## VIII / 八

===Original===

By the morning of the fourth day on the island of Oahu, Chandler had learned enough of the ropes to have signed a money-chit at the Tripler currency office against Koitska’s account.

That was about all he had learned, except for a few practical matters like where meals were served and the location of the freshwater swimming pool at the back of the grounds. He was killing time using the pool when, in the middle of a jacknife from the ten-foot board, he felt himself seized. He sprawled into the water with a hard splashing slap, threshed about and, as he came to the surface, found himself giggling.

“Sorry, dear,” he apologized to himself, “but we don’t carry our weight in the same places, you know. Get that square-what’sit thingamajig, like an angel, and meet me in front by the flagpole in twenty minutes.”

He recognized the voice, even if his own vocal chords had made it. It was the girl who had driven him back from the interview with Koitska, the one who had casually announced she had saved his life at his hoaxing trial. Chandler swam to the side of the pool and toweled as he trotted toward his quarters. She was from Koitska now, of course; which meant that his “test” was about to be graded.

Quickly though he dressed, she was there before him, standing beside a low-slung sports car and chatting with one of the groundskeepers. An armful of leis dangled beside her, and although she wore the coronet which was evidence of her status the gardener did not seem to fear her. “Come along, love,” she called to Chandler. “Koitska wants your thingummy. Chuck it in the trunk if it’ll fit, and we’ll head waikiki wikiwiki. Don’t I say that nicely? But I only fool the malihinis, like you.”

She chattered away as the little car dug its rear wheels into the drive and leaped around the green and out the gate.

===Chinese===

到了瓦胡岛（Oahu）的第四天早晨，钱德勒已经把门道摸得差不多了，足可在特里普勒（Tripler）的货币兑换处签下一张记在科伊茨卡（Koitska）账上的赊账单。

除此之外他学到的也就仅此而已，无非几桩实际小事：在哪儿开饭，场地后部那座淡水泳池的位置。他正泡在泳池里消磨时光，从十英尺跳板上做一个屈体跳水做到一半，忽然感到自己被攫住了。他重重地拍进水里，溅起响亮的水花，胡乱扑腾着，等冒出水面，却发现自己正在咯咯傻笑。

“对不住啦，亲爱的，”他向自己赔不是，“可你知道，咱俩的体重可不长在同样的地方。去把那个方的——什么来着——小玩意儿拿上，乖乖的像个小天使，二十分钟后到前头旗杆底下见我。”

他听出了这个嗓音，尽管那是他自己的声带发出来的。是那个送他从科伊茨卡的约见处回来的姑娘，就是随口宣布过自己在他那场行骗审判中救了他一命的那一个。钱德勒游到池边，一路小跑着回住处，边跑边用毛巾擦身。她如今是科伊茨卡那边的人了，当然；这就是说，他那场“测验”快要判分了。

尽管他已经尽快穿戴，她还是先到了，站在一辆低矮的跑车旁，跟一个园丁闲聊。她身侧挂着一抱夏威夷花环（lei），而虽然她戴着那顶足以表明身份的头冠（coronet），园丁似乎并不怕她。“来吧，亲爱的，”她朝钱德勒喊道，“科伊茨卡要你那玩意儿。要是塞得进后备箱就扔进去，咱们就一路 wikiwiki 直奔威基基。我说得地道吧？不过我也就哄哄你这样的外来客（malihini）。”

小车后轮在车道上一蹬，绕过草坪冲出大门，她一路絮絮地说个不停。

===Original===

The wind howled by them, the sun was bright, the sky was piercingly blue. Riding next to this beautiful girl, it was hard for Chandler to remember that she was one of those who had destroyed his world. It was a terrible thing to have so much hatred and to feel it so diluted. Not even Koitska seemed a terrible enough enemy to accept such a load of detestation; it was hate without an object, and it recoiled on the hater, leaving him turgid and constrained. If he could not hate his onetime friend Jack Souther for defiling and destroying his wife, it was almost as hard to hate Souther’s anonymous possessor. It could even have been Koitska. It could even have been this girl by his side. In the strange, cruel fantasies with which the Execs indulged themselves it was likely enough that they would sometimes assume the body, and the role, of the opposite sex. Why not? Strange, ruthless morality; it was impossible to evaluate it by any human standards.

It was also impossible to think of hatred with her beside him. They soared around Honolulu on a broad expressway and paralleled the beach toward Waikiki. “Look, dear. Diamond Head! Mustn’t ignore it﻿—very bad form﻿—like not going to see the night-blooming cereus at the Punahou School. You haven’t missed that, have you?”

“I’m afraid I have﻿—”

“Rosalie. Call me Rosalie, dear.”

“I’m afraid I have, Rosalie.” For some reason the name sounded familiar.

“Shame, oh, shame! They say it was wonderful night before last. Looks like cactus to me, but﻿—”

===Chinese===

风从他们身旁呼啸而过，阳光明亮，天空蓝得刺眼。坐在这位美丽姑娘的身边，钱德勒很难记起：她正是毁掉他那个世界的人之一。心里蓄着那么深的仇恨，却又眼看着它被稀释成这样，实在可怕。就连科伊茨卡，似乎也够不上一个配承这份切齿之恨的敌人；那是一种没有对象的恨，反过来蛰咬怀恨的人，令他郁结憋闷。倘若他连昔日的朋友杰克·萨瑟玷污、毁掉他的妻子都恨不起来，那么去恨萨瑟背后那个无名的附身者，也就同样艰难。那甚至可能就是科伊茨卡。甚至可能就是身边这个姑娘。执政者（Exec）们纵情沉溺的那些离奇而残酷的幻想里，他们时不时换上异性的身体和角色，实在大有可能。有什么不可以的？奇怪的、无情的道德观；拿任何人类的标准去衡量它都是徒劳。

她在身边，仇恨也无从想起。他们驰上一条宽阔的高速公路绕檀香山（Honolulu）而行，沿海滩平行着朝威基基（Waikiki）驶去。“看哪，亲爱的。钻石山（Diamond Head）！可不能怠慢它——太失礼了——就像到了普纳荷学校（Punahou School）却不去看夜里开的昙花一样。那个你还没错过吧？”

“恐怕已经错过了——”

“罗莎莉。叫我罗莎莉吧，亲爱的。”

“恐怕是错过了，罗莎莉。”不知怎么，这名字听着耳熟。

“罪过，噢，罪过！他们说前天晚上开得美极了。在我看来就像棵仙人掌，可是——”

===Original===

Chandler’s mental processes had worked to a conclusion. “Rosalie *Pan*!” he said. “Now I know!”

“Know what? You mean﻿—” she swerved around a motionless Buick, parked arrogantly five feet from the curb﻿—“you mean you didn’t know who I was? And to think I used to pay five thousand a year for publicity.”

Chandler said, smiling, and almost relaxed, “I’m sorry, but musical comedies weren’t my strong point. I did see you once, though, on television. Then, let’s see, wasn’t there something about you disappearing﻿—”

She nodded, glancing at him. “There sure was, dear. I almost froze to death getting out to that airport. Of course, it was worth it, I found out later. If I hadn’t been took, as they say, I would’ve been dead, because you remember what happened to New York about an hour later.”

“You must have had some friends,” Chandler began, and let it trail off. So did the girl. After a moment she began to talk about the scenery again, pointing out the brick-red and purple bougainvillea, describing how the shoreline had looked before they’d “cleaned it up.” “Oh, thousands and thousands of the *homeliest* little houses. You’d have hated it. So we have done at least a few good things, anyway,” she said complacently, and began gently to probe into his life story. But as they stopped before the T.W.A. message center, a few moments later, she said, “Well, love, it’s been fun. Go on in; Koitska’s expecting you. I’ll see you later.” And her eyes added gently: *I hope*.

===Chinese===

钱德勒的脑子这才转出了结果。“罗莎莉·*潘*（Pan）！”他说，“这下我知道了！”

“知道什么？你是说——”她一把方向，绕过一辆大模大样停在离路缘五英尺开外的别克——“你是说，你不知道我是谁？想想看，我从前一年可是花五千块养宣传班子的。”

钱德勒微笑着说，人也几乎松快了下来：“抱歉，音乐喜剧可不是我的强项。不过我在电视上见过你一次。然后——让我想想——不是还有一段你失踪了之类的——”

她瞥了他一眼，点点头。“可不是嘛，亲爱的。我赶去机场那一路，差点没冻死。当然啦，后来我才发现，这很值。要不是像他们说的那样被‘收’了，我早死了，因为你总记得一个钟头之后纽约出了什么事。”

“你当时一定交了些朋友吧。”钱德勒起了个头，又任它渐渐淡去。姑娘也没接下去。过了一会儿，她重新谈起风景来，指着砖红和紫色的九重葛，描绘他们“清理”之前海岸线的模样。“噢，成千上万*丑得不能再丑*的小房子。你会讨厌死的。所以说，好歹我们也干过几件好事。”她怡然自得地说，随即开始温柔地探问他的身世。可几分钟后，车在环球航空（T.W.A.）讯息中心门前停下时，她说：“好啦，亲爱的，很开心。进去吧；科伊茨卡在等你。回头见。”她的眼睛温和地补了一句：*但愿如此*。

===Original===

---

Chandler got out of the car, turned﻿ … and felt himself taken. His voice said briskly, “*Zdrastvoi, Rosie. Gd’yeh Koitska?*”

Unsurprised the girl pointed to the building. “*Kto govorit?*”

Chandler’s voice answered in English, with a faint Oxford accent: “It is I, Rosie, Kalman. Where’s Koitska’s tinkertoy? Oh, all right, thanks; I’ll just pick it up and take it in. Hope it’s all right. I must say one wearies of breaking in these new fellows.”

Chandler’s body ambled around to the trunk of the car, took out the square-wave generator on its breadboard base and slouched into the building. It called ahead in the same language and was answered wheezily from above: Koitska. “*Zdrastvoi. Iditye suda ko mneh. Kto, Kalman?*”

“*Konyekhno!*” cried Chandler’s voice and he was carried in and up to where the fat man lounged in a leather-upholstered wheelchair. There was a conversation, long minutes of it, while the two men poked at the generator. Chandler did not understand a word until he spoke to himself: “You﻿—what’s your name.”

“Chandler,” Koitska filled in.

===Chinese===

---

钱德勒下了车，转过身……便感到自己被占了。他的嗓音轻快地说：“*Zdrastvoi, Rosie. Gd’yeh Koitska?*”

姑娘毫不惊讶，朝那栋楼一指。“*Kto govorit?*”

钱德勒的嗓音改用英语回答，带着一丝淡淡的牛津腔：“是我，罗西，卡尔曼（Kalman）。科伊茨卡的那个小玩意儿在哪儿？哦，好吧，谢谢；我自己拿了带进去就是。希望没妨碍。不得不说，调教这些新来的家伙真叫人腻烦。”

钱德勒的身体溜达到车尾，从后备箱里取出装在面包板底座上的方波发生器，拖着步子进了楼。它用同一种语言朝里招呼了一声，楼上有人气喘吁吁地应声：科伊茨卡。“*Zdrastvoi. Iditye suda ko mneh. Kto, Kalman?*”

“*Konyekhno!*”钱德勒的嗓音应声喊道，接着他便被带着进了屋、上了楼，来到那胖子歪坐着的皮面轮椅旁。两人摆弄着那台发生器，一谈就是好几分钟。钱德勒一个字也听不懂，直到那声音对他自己开了口：“你——你叫什么名字。”

“钱德勒。”科伊茨卡替他补上。

===Original===

“You, Chandler. D’you know anything at all about submillimeter microwaves? Tell Koitska.” Briefly Chandler felt himself free﻿—long enough to nod; then he was possessed again, and Koitska repeated the nod. “Good, then. Tell Koitska what experience you’ve had.”

Again free, Chandler said, “Not a great deal of actual experience. I worked with a group at Caltech on spectroscopic measurements in the million megacycle range. I didn’t design any of the equipment, though I helped put it together.” He recited his degrees until Koitska raised a languid hand.

“*Shto*, I don’t care. If ve gave you diagrams you could build?”

“Certainly, if I had the equipment. I suppose I’d need﻿—”

But Koitska stopped him again. “I know vot you need,” he said damply. “Enough. Ve see.” In a moment Chandler was taken again, and his voice and Koitska’s debated the matter for a while, until Koitska shrugged, turned his head and seemed to go to sleep.

Chandler marched himself out of the room and out into the driveway before his voice said to him: “You’ve secured a position, then. Go back to Tripler until we send for you. It’ll be a few days, I expect.”

===Chinese===

“你，钱德勒。你对亚毫米微波到底懂不懂？告诉科伊茨卡。”钱德勒短暂地感到自己自由了——刚够点一个头；接着他又被附了身，由科伊茨卡把那个点头重复了一遍。“很好。那么，把你干过的经历告诉科伊茨卡。”

又一次获得自由的钱德勒说：“实打实的经验不算多。我在加州理工学院（Caltech）跟一个小组干过百万兆周频段的光谱测量。设备没有一件是我设计的，不过装配我搭过手。”他背着自己的学位背个没完，直到科伊茨卡懒洋洋地抬起一只手。

“*Shto*，我不管这些。要是给你图纸，你造得出来？”

“当然，只要有设备就行。我想我会需要——”

可科伊茨卡又打断了他。“我知道你须要什么，”他冷淡地说，“够了。往后看吧。”不一会儿钱德勒又被占了，他的嗓音同科伊茨卡就此事你来我往争了一阵，直到科伊茨卡耸耸肩，扭过头去，像是睡着了。

钱德勒迈着正步走出房间、走上车道，他的嗓音才对他开口：“那么，你算是谋到一个职位了。回特里普勒去，等我们派人来叫。我估计要过几天。”

===Original===

And Chandler was free again.

He was also alone. The girl in the Porsche was gone. The door of the T.W.A. building had latched itself behind him. He stared around him, swore, shrugged and circled the building to the parking lot at back, on the chance that a car might be there for him to borrow.

Luckily, there was. There were four, in fact, all with keys in them. He selected a Ford, puzzled out the likeliest road back to Honolulu and turned the key in the starter.

It was fortunate, he thought, that there had been several cars; if there had been only one he would not have dared to take it, for fear of stranding Koitska or some other exec who might easily blot him out in annoyance. He did not wish to join the wretches at the Monument.

It was astonishing how readily fear had become a part of his life.

The trouble with this position he had somehow secured﻿—one of the troubles﻿—was that there was no union delegate to settle employee grievances. Like no transportation. Like no clear idea of working hours, or duties. Like no mention at all﻿—of course﻿—of wages. Chandler had no idea what his rights were, if any at all, or of what the penalties would be if he overstepped them.

===Chinese===

于是钱德勒又自由了。

他还是孤零零一个人。开保时捷的姑娘已经走了。环球航空大楼的门在他身后自动闩上了。他环顾四周，骂了一句，耸耸肩，绕到楼后头的停车场去，指望那儿能有辆车供他“借”一借。

还算走运，有。其实有四辆，钥匙都插在上面。他挑了一辆福特，琢磨出最可能通回檀香山的路线，把钥匙拧到了点火挡。

他想，幸亏车有好几辆；要是只有一辆，他可不敢动，生怕断了科伊茨卡或者哪位执政者的代步——那样的人一恼起来，抹掉他不过抬抬手的事。他可不想去纪念碑（the Monument）加入那帮可怜虫的行列。

恐惧竟然就这样轻易地成了他生活的一部分，真是惊人。

他不知怎么谋来的这份差事有个麻烦——麻烦之一——就是没有工会代表来受理雇员的申诉。比如说，没有通勤。比如说，上班时间和职责一概不清。再比如，工资嘛——当然——只字不提。钱德勒不知道自己有什么权利（如果居然有的话），也不知道越了界会受什么处罚。

===Original===

The maimed victims at the Monument supplied a clue, of course. He could not really believe that that sort of punishment would be applied for minor infractions. Death was so much less trouble. Even death was not really likely, he thought, for a simple lapse.

He *thought*.

He could not be sure, of course. He could be sure of only one thing: He was now a slave, completely a slave, a slave until the day he died. Back on the mainland there was the statistical likelihood of occasional slavery-by-possession, but there it was only the body that was enslaved, and only for moments. Here, in the shadow of the execs, it was all of him, forever, until death or a miracle turned him loose.

===Chinese===

当然，纪念碑前那些被弄残的受害者提供了一条线索。他实在不能相信，那类惩罚会施加在小过失上。死刑省事得多。就算死刑，他心想，为一次普普通通的闪失也未必轮得上。

他*是这么想的*。

当然，他拿不准。他只对一件事有把握：他现在是个奴隶，彻头彻尾的奴隶，一直到死的那天为止的奴隶。回到大陆上，按概率偶尔也有因被附身而沦为的奴役，可那里被奴役的只是肉体，而且只有片刻工夫。在这里，在执政者们的阴影底下，被奴役的是他的全部，直到永远，除非死亡或者奇迹放他走。

===Original===

---

On the second day following he returned to his room at Tripler after breakfast, and found a Honolulu city policeman sitting hollow-eyed on the edge of his bed. The man stood up as Chandler came in. “So,” he grumbled, “you take so long! Here. Is diagrams, specs, parts lists, all. You get everything three days from now, then we begin.”

The policeman, no longer Koitska, shook himself, glanced stolidly at Chandler and walked out, leaving a thick manila envelope on the pillow. On it was written, in a crabbed hand: *All secret! Do not show diagrams!*

Chandler opened the envelope and spilled its contents on the bed.

===Chinese===

---

过了两天，他早餐后回到特里普勒的房间，发现一个檀香山市警察正眼窝深陷地坐在他床沿上。钱德勒一进门，那人就站了起来。“这么着，”他嘟囔道，“你可真够慢的！拿去。图纸、规格、零件清单，全在这儿。三天后东西到齐，咱们就开工。”

那警察——已经不再是科伊茨卡了——抖了抖身子，木然瞥了钱德勒一眼，走了出去，把一只厚厚的牛皮纸信封留在枕头上。信封上是几个字迹歪斜潦草的字：*全是机密！图纸不得示人！*

钱德勒拆开信封，把里面的东西倒在床上。

===Original===

An hour later he realized that sixty minutes had passed in which he had not been afraid. It was good to be working again, he thought, and then that thought faded away again as he returned to studying the sheaves of circuit diagrams and closely typed pages of specifications. It was not only work, it was hard work, and absorbing. Chandler knew enough about the very short wavelength radio spectrum to know that the device he was supposed to build was no proficiency test; this was for real. The more he puzzled over it the less he could understand of its purpose. There was a transmitter and there was a receiver. Astonishingly, neither was directional: that ruled out radar, for example. He rejected immediately the thought that the radiation was for spectrum analysis, as in the Caltech project﻿—unfortunate, because that was the only application with which he had firsthand familiarity; but impossible. The thing was too complicated. Nor could it be a simple message transmitter﻿—no, perhaps it could, assuming there was a reason for using the submillimeter bands instead of the conventional, far simpler short-wave spectrum. Could it? The submillimeter waves were line-of-sight, of course, but would ionosphere scatter make it possible for them to cover great distances? He could not remember. Or was that irrelevant, since perhaps they needed only to cover the distances between islands in their own archipelago? But then, why all the power? And in any case, what about this fantastic switching panel, hundreds of square feet of it even though it was transistorized and subminiaturized and involving at least a dozen sophisticated technical refinements he hadn’t the training quite to understand? AT&T could have handled every phone call in the United States with less switching than this﻿—in the days when telephone systems spanned a nation instead of a fraction of a city. He pushed the papers together in a pile and sat back, smoking a cigarette, trying to remember what he could of the theory behind submillimeter radiation.

===Chinese===

一个钟头后他才发觉，这六十分钟里他一直没有害怕。又能干活了，真好，他想；随后这个念头也淡了下去，他重新埋头研究起那一沓沓电路图和打字打得密密麻麻的规格书。这不仅是活儿，而且是苦活儿，让人入迷的苦活儿。关于极短波长的无线电频谱，钱德勒懂得足够多，足以知道自己要造的这台装置绝不是什么水平测验；这是动真格的。可他越琢磨，越不明白它的用途。有一台发射机，还有一台接收机。蹊跷的是，两台都不带方向性：这一下就排除了雷达之类的可能。他立刻否掉了这辐射是用于光谱分析的念头——就像加州理工那个项目那样；可惜，因为那是他唯一有第一手经验的用途；但不可能。这东西太复杂了。它也不可能是台简单的讯息发射机——不，也许可能，假如有什么理由非用亚毫米波段、而不用常规简便得多的短波频段的话。可能吗？亚毫米波当然只能视距传播，可电离层散射能不能让它传得很远？他想不起来了。又或者这根本无关紧要，因为人家也许只需要在自己那片群岛的岛与岛之间传这么远？可那样的话，干吗要那么大的功率？再说，这块异想天开的交换面板又怎么说？哪怕已经晶体管化、超小型化，它还是有几百平方英尺，里面至少有一打他受的训练不足以完全看懂的高级技术巧思。想当年电话网覆盖的是整个国家、而不是一座城市的零头，美国电话电报公司（AT&T）处理全美国的每一通电话，用的交换设备也没这么多。他把纸拢成一摞，靠回椅背，点了支烟，努力回想自己还记得多少亚毫米辐射背后的理论。

===Original===

At half a million megacycles and up, the domain of quantum theory began to be invaded. Rotating gas molecules, constricted to a few energy states, responded directly to the radio waves. Chandler remembered late-night bull sessions in Pasadena during which it had been pointed out that the possibilities in the field were enormous﻿—although only possibilities, for there was no engineering way to reach them, and no clear theory to point the way﻿—suggesting such strange ultimate practical applications as the receiverless radio, for example. Was that what he had here?

He gave up. It was a question that would burn at him until he found the answer, but just now he had work to do, and he’d better be doing it.

Skipping lunch entirely, he carefully checked the components lists, made a copy of what he would need, checked the original envelope and its contents with the man at the main receiving desk for his safe, and caught the bus to Honolulu.

===Chinese===

到了五十万兆周及更高的频率，量子理论的领地便开始受到侵入。旋转的气体分子被限制在寥寥几个能态上，直接对无线电波发生响应。钱德勒记得在帕萨迪纳的那些深夜神聊，有人指出过这个领域潜力惊人——可惜只是潜力而已，因为既没有工程手段够得着它，也没有明晰的理论指路——大家畅想过种种稀奇古怪的终极实际用途，比如无接收机收音机。他手里这个，会不会就是那种东西？

他放弃了。这个问题会一直煎熬他，直到找出答案为止；可眼下他有活儿要干，还是赶紧干起来为好。

他索性连午饭都没吃，仔细核对了元件清单，把自己需要的誊了一份，把原信封连同内容交到总收发台那位先生那里替他保管进保险柜，然后赶上了去檀香山的公共汽车。

===Original===

At the Parts ’n Plenty store, Hsi read the list with a faint frown that turned into a puzzled scowl. When he put it down he looked at Chandler for a few moments without speaking.

“Well, Hsi? Can you get all this for me?” The parts man shrugged and nodded. “Koitska said in three days.”

Hsi looked startled, then resigned. “That puts it right up to me, doesn’t it? All right. Wait a moment.”

He disappeared in the back of the store, where Chandler heard him talking on what was evidently an intercom system. He came back in a few minutes and slipped Chandler’s list into a slit in the locked door. “Tough for Bert,” he said. “He’ll be working all night, getting started﻿—but I can take it easy till tomorrow. By then he’ll know what we don’t have, and I’ll find some way to get it.” He shrugged again, but his face was lined. Chandler wondered how one went about finding, for example, a thirty megawatt klystron tube; but it was Hsi’s problem. He said:

“All right, I’ll see you Monday.”

“Wait a minute, Chandler.” Hsi eyed him. “You don’t have anything special to do, do you? Well, come have dinner with me. Maybe I can get to know you. Then maybe I can answer some of your questions, if you like.”

===Chinese===

在“零件多多”（Parts ’n Plenty）商店里，徐（Hsi）读着那份清单，眉头先是微微一皱，随即皱成了困惑的疙瘩。他把清单放下，一言不发地打量了钱德勒好一会儿。

“怎么样，徐？这些你都能给我弄齐吗？”零件商耸耸肩，点点头。“科伊茨卡说三天之内。”

徐先是愕然，随即认了命。“这可把担子全压我身上了，是不是？行吧。等一下。”

他消失在店铺后头，钱德勒听见他在一套显然是对讲系统的东西上讲话。几分钟后他回来了，把钱德勒的清单塞进那扇上了锁的门的投缝里。“苦了伯特（Bert）了，”他说，“他得干个通宵，先把摊子支起来——我倒可以松快到明天。到那会儿他就知道咱们缺什么了，我再想法子弄去。”他又耸了耸肩，可脸上刻满了纹路。钱德勒纳闷，像一只三十兆瓦的速调管这种东西，人究竟上哪儿去弄；不过那是徐的问题。他说：

“好，那我们星期一见。”

“等一等，钱德勒。”徐端详着他。“你没有什么要紧事吧？那就来跟我吃顿饭。兴许我能跟你处熟一点。那样的话，要是你愿意，兴许我还能回答你几个问题。”

===Original===

---

They took a bus out Kapiolani Boulevard, then got out and walked a few blocks to a restaurant named Mother Chee’s. Hsi was well known there, it seemed. He led Chandler to a booth at the back, nodded to the waiter, ordered without looking at the menu and sat back. “You malihinis don’t know much about food,” he said, humorously patronizing. “I think you’ll like it. It’s all fish, anyway.”

The man was annoying. Chandler was moved to say, “Too bad, I was hoping for duck in orange sauce, perhaps some snow peas﻿—”

Hsi shook his head. “There’s meat, all right, but not here. You’ll only find it in the places where the execs sometimes go.﻿ … Tell me something, Chandler. What’s that scar on your forehead.”

Chandler touched it, almost with surprise. Since the medics had treated it he had almost forgotten it was there. He began to explain, then paused, looking at Hsi, and changed his mind. “What’s the score? You testing me, too? Want to see if I’ll lie about it?”

===Chinese===

---

两人乘公共汽车沿卡皮奥拉尼大道（Kapiolani Boulevard）出城，下车后又走了几个街区，来到一家叫“齐妈妈”（Mother Chee’s）的餐馆。看样子，徐在这儿是熟客。他把钱德勒领到最里头的卡座，朝侍者点点头，看也不看菜单就点了菜，然后靠回座位。“你们这些外来客啊，吃是懂得不多的，”他说，带点滑稽的居高临下，“我想你会喜欢的。反正全是鱼。”

这人真有点讨人嫌。钱德勒被逗得开了口：“真糟糕，我还盼着来份橙汁烩鸭，再来点雪豆什么的——”

徐摇了摇头。“肉嘛，有的是，可不在这儿。只有执政者们偶尔光顾的地方才找得着。……跟我说说，钱德勒。你额头上那道疤是怎么回事。”

钱德勒几乎是诧异地摸了摸它。自打医护人员给它治过之后，他几乎忘了它的存在。他开始解释，随即顿住，望着徐，改了主意。“这是唱的哪一出？你也在考我？想看看我会不会在这上头撒谎？”

===Original===

Hsi grinned. “Sorry. I guess that’s what I was doing. I do know what an *H* stands for; we’ve seen them before. Not many. The ones that do get this far usually don’t last long. Unless, of course, they are working for somebody whom it wouldn’t do to offend,” he explained.

“So what you want to know, then, is whether I was really hoaxing or not. Does it make any difference?”

“Damn right it does, man! We’re slaves, but we’re not animals!” Chandler had gotten to him; the parts man looked startled, then sallow, as he observed his own vehemence.

“Sorry, Hsi. It makes a difference to me, too. Well, I wasn’t hoaxing. I was possessed, just like any other everyday rapist-murderer, only I couldn’t prove it. And it didn’t look too good for me, because the damn thing happened in a pharmaceuticals plant. That was supposed to be about the only place in town where you could be sure you wouldn’t be possessed, or so everybody thought. Including me. Up to the time I went ape.”

===Chinese===

徐咧嘴一笑。“抱歉。我猜我就是在干这个。*H* 代表什么我当然知道；我们以前见过。不多。能走到这一步的，通常活不长。当然喽——除非他们在替哪个得罪不起的人当差。”他解释道。

“这么说，你想弄清楚的就是：我到底是不是真在行骗。这有什么要紧吗？”

“他妈的当然要紧，伙计！我们是奴隶，可我们不是畜生！”钱德勒戳到他痛处了；这位零件商一脸错愕，随即发了白，他自己也觉察出了自己的激愤。

“对不起，徐。这对我来说也要紧。这么说吧：我不是行骗。我是被附身了，跟随便哪个日常的强奸犯杀人犯一模一样，只是我证明不了。而且我的处境糟透了，因为那档子该死的事偏偏出在一家制药厂里。那可是全镇上差不多唯一敢担保不会被附身的地方——至少人人都这么想。包括我。直到我这回发了疯。”

===Original===

Hsi nodded. The waiter approached with their drinks. Hsi looked at him appraisingly, then did a curious thing. He gripped his left wrist with his right hand, quickly, then released it again. The waiter did not appear to notice. Expertly he served the drinks, folded small pink floral napkins, dumped and wiped their ashtray in one motion﻿—and then, so quickly that Chandler was not quite sure he had seen it, caught Hsi’s wrist in the same fleeting gesture just before he turned and walked away.

Without comment Hsi turned back to Chandler. He said, “I believe you. Would you like to know why it happened? Because I think I can tell you. The execs have all the antibiotics they need now.”

“You mean﻿—” Chandler hesitated.

“That’s right. They did leave some areas alone, as long as they weren’t fully stocked on everything they might want for the foreseeable future. Wouldn’t you?”

“I might,” Chandler said cautiously, “if I knew what I was﻿—being an exec.”

Hsi said, “Eat your dinner. I’ll take a chance and tell you what I know.” He swallowed his whiskey-on-the-rocks with a quick backward jerk of the head. “They’re mostly Russians﻿—you must know that much for yourself. The whole thing started in Russia.”

===Chinese===

徐点了点头。侍者端着他们的酒过来了。徐朝他打量了一眼，接着做了个古怪的动作。他右手飞快地攥了一下自己的左腕，又松开。侍者仿佛没有察觉。他熟练地斟酒上桌，折好小小的粉色花餐巾，倒灰、擦烟灰缸一气呵成——然后，就在转身走开之前，又用同样一闪而逝的手势攥了一下徐的手腕，快得钱德勒不能肯定自己是不是真看见了。

徐一言不发地转回身来对着钱德勒。他说：“我相信你。你想不想知道那件事为什么会发生？因为我想我是能告诉你的。执政者们的抗生素，如今已经要多少有多少了。”

“你是说——”钱德勒迟疑了。

“正是。只要还没把可预见的将来可能想要的一切囤足，他们就确实放过了一些地方不闹。换了你，不也一样？”

“也许吧，”钱德勒谨慎地说，“要是我知道当个执政者是怎么回事的话。”

徐说：“吃你的饭吧。我豁出去，把我知道的讲给你听。”他头向后一仰，一口把加冰威士忌倒了进去。“他们多半是俄国人——这一点你自己总归看得出来。整个这场事，是从俄国开始的。”

===Original===

Chandler said, “Well, that’s pretty obvious. But Russia was smashed up as much as anywhere else. The whole Russian government was killed﻿—wasn’t it?”

Hsi nodded. “They’re not the government. Not the exec. Communism doesn’t mean any more to them than the Declaration of Independence does﻿—which is nothing. It’s very simple, Chandler: they’re a project that got out of hand.”

===Chinese===

钱德勒说：“嗯，这倒相当明显。可俄国也被砸得跟别处一样稀烂。俄国政府整个被杀光了——不是吗？”

徐点点头。“他们不是政府。也不是执政者。共产主义对他们来说，跟《独立宣言》一样毫无意义——彻头彻尾的毫无意义。事情很简单，钱德勒：他们是一项失去了控制的项目。”

===Original===

---

Back four years ago, he said, in Russia, it started in the last days of the Second Stalinite Regime, before the Neo-Krushchevists took over power in the January Push.

The Western World had not known exactly what was going on, of course. The “mystery wrapped in a riddle surrounded by an enigma” had become queerer and even more opaque after Kruschchev’s death and the revival of such fine old Soviet institutions as the Gay Pay Oo. That was the development called the Freeze, when the Stalinites seized control in the name of the sacred Generalissimo of the Soviet Fatherland, a mighty-missile party, dedicated to bringing about the world revolution by force of sputnik. The neo-Krushchevists, on the other hand, believed that honey caught more flies than vinegar; and, although there were few visible adherents to that philosophy during the purges of the Freeze, they were not all dead. Then, out of the Donbas Electrical Workshop, came sudden support for their point of view.

===Chinese===

---

往回数四年，他说，在俄国，事情发端于第二次斯大林分子政权的末年，在新赫鲁晓夫分子们借“一月夺权”（the January Push）上台之前。

西方世界当然一直没弄清那边到底在闹什么。自从赫鲁晓夫一命归西、“格伯乌”（Gay Pay Oo）一类老而弥香的苏维埃机构死灰复燃之后，那个“裹在谜语里、罩在疑云中的谜”变得愈发古怪、愈发看不透了。那段时期便是人称“大冻结”（the Freeze）的年月：斯大林分子们打着神圣的苏联祖国大元帅的旗号攫取了大权——那是一个信奉导弹的强权党，立志凭人造卫星的武力促成世界革命。而新赫鲁晓夫分子们呢，信的是蜜比醋招苍蝇；虽说在“大冻结”的清洗岁月里，这门哲学的信徒明面上所剩无几，他们倒也并没有死绝。接着，顿巴斯电气工厂（the Donbas Electrical Workshop）突然为他们的观点送来了支持。

===Original===

It was a weapon. It was more than a weapon, an irresistable tool﻿—more than that, the way to end all disputes forever. It was a simple radio transmitter (Hsi said)﻿—or so it seemed, but its frequencies were on an unusual band and its effects were remarkable. It controlled the minds of men. The “receiver” was the human brain. Through this little portable transmitter, surgically patch-wired to the brain of the person operating it, his entire personality was transmitted in a pattern of very short waves which could invade and modulate the personality of any other human being in the world. For that matter, of any animal, as long as the creature had enough “mind” to seize﻿—

“What’s the matter?” Hsi interrupted himself, staring at Chandler. Chandler had stopped eating, his hand frozen midway to his mouth. He shook his head.

“Nothing. Go on.” Hsi shrugged and continued.

===Chinese===

那是一件武器。它不止是武器，是一件无可抗拒的工具——还不止如此，是一劳永逸终结一切争端的法门。那是一台简简单单的无线电发射机（徐说）——至少看着简单，可它的频率落在一条不寻常的波段上，效果惊世骇俗。它控制人的心智。“接收机”就是人脑。通过这台便携的小发射机——它经外科手术接线，连进操作者的大脑——操作者整个的人格便化作一串极短的电波发射出去，得以侵入并调制世上任何另一个人的人格。说起来，任何动物也行，只要那畜生的“心智”够得上被攫取——

“你怎么了？”徐停下话头，盯着钱德勒。钱德勒停住了进食，举到半途的手僵在了那里。他摇了摇头。

“没什么。接着讲。”徐耸耸肩，继续说下去。

===Original===

While the Western World was celebrating Christmas﻿—the Christmas before the first outbreak of possession in the outside world﻿—the man who invented the machine was secretly demonstrating it to another man. Both of them were now dead. The inventor had been a Pole, the other man a former Party leader who, four years before, had rescued the inventor’s dying father from a Siberian work camp. The Party leader had reason to congratulate himself on that loaf cast on the water. There were only three working models of the transmitter﻿—what ultimately was refined into the coronet Chandler had seen on the heads of Koitska and the girl﻿—but that was enough for the January push.

The Stalinites were out. The neo-Krushchevists were in.

A whole factory in the Donbas was converted to manufacturing these little mental controllers as fast as they could be produced﻿—and that was fast, for they were simple in design to begin with and were quickly refined to a few circuits. Even the surgical wiring to the brain became unnecessary as induction coils tapped the encephalic rhythms. Only the great amplifying hookup was really complicated. Only one of those was necessary, for a single amplifier could serve as re-broadcaster﻿—modulator for thousands of the headsets.

“Are you sure you’re all right?” Hsi demanded.

Chandler put down his fork, lit a cigarette and beckoned to the waiter. “I’m all right. I just want another drink.”

He needed the drink. For now he knew what he was building for Koitska.

===Chinese===

正当西方世界欢庆圣诞节的时候——就是附身之祸头一回在外界爆发前的那个圣诞节——机器的发明者正秘密地把它演示给另一个人看。这两个人如今都已死了。发明者是个波兰人，另一个则是一位昔日的党的领袖；四年前，他把发明者垂死的父亲从西伯利亚劳改营里救了出来。这位党的领袖有理由为自己当年“撒在水面上的那块面包”暗自庆幸。发射机当时只有三台能用的样机——也就是日后精化成钱德勒在科伊茨卡和那姑娘头上见过的头冠的那种东西——可对于一月夺权来说，已经够了。

斯大林分子下了台。新赫鲁晓夫分子上了台。

顿巴斯的一整座工厂都改了行，能产多快就产多快地制造这些小小的心智控制器——产量也真上得去，因为它们起初设计就简单，很快又精简到寥寥几个电路。连往大脑里动手术布线都不再需要了，感应线圈直接采拾脑部节律就够了。真正复杂的只有那套庞大的放大接线装置。而那种装置只需要一台，因为单单一台放大器就能充当中继播发——调制器，供成千上万顶头冠使用。

“你真没事吗？”徐追问道。

钱德勒放下叉子，点了支烟，朝侍者招招手。“我没事。我只是想再来一杯。”

这杯酒他确实需要。因为现在他知道了，自己是在替科伊茨卡造什么。

===Original===

---

The waiter brought two more drinks and carried away the uneaten food. “We don’t know exactly who did what after that,” Hsi said, “but somehow or other it got out of hand. I think it was the technical crew of the factory that took over. I suppose it was an inevitable danger.” He grinned savagely. “I can just imagine the Party workers in the factory,” he said, “trying to figure out how to keep them in line﻿—bribe them or terrify them? Give them dachas or send a quota to Siberia? Neither would work, of course, because there isn’t any bribe you can give to a man who only has to stretch out his hand to take over the world, and you can’t frighten a man who can make you slit your own throat. Anyway, the next thing that happened﻿—the following Christmas﻿—was when they took over the world. It wasn’t a Party movement at all any more. A lot of the workers were Czechs and Hungarians and Poles, and the first thing they wanted to do was to even a few scores.

“So here they are! Before they let the whole world go bang they got out of range. They got themselves out of Russia on two Red Navy cruisers, about a thousand of them; then they systematically triggered off every ballistic missile they could find﻿ … and they could find all of them, sooner or later, it was just a matter of looking. As soon as it was safe they moved in here. Best place in the world for them.

“There are only a thousand or so of them here on the Islands, and nobody outside the Islands even knows where they are. If they did, what good would it do them? They can kill anyone, anywhere. They kill for fun, but sometimes they kill for a reason too. When one of them goes wandering for kicks he makes it a point to mess up all the transport and communications facilities he comes across﻿—especially now, since they’ve stockpiled everything they’re likely to need for the next twenty years. We don’t know what they’re planning to do when the twenty years are up. Maybe they don’t care. Would you?”

Chandler drained his drink and shook his head. “One question,” he said. “Who’s ‘we’?”

===Chinese===

---

侍者又端来两杯酒，把没动过的饭菜撤了下去。“后来谁干了什么，我们就说不准了，”徐说，“反正不知怎么的，事情脱了缰。我想是工厂里的技术班子接管了一切。我猜这是个躲不过的险。”他狞笑了一下。“我完全可以想象工厂里那些党干部的样子，”他说，“琢磨着怎么拿捏这帮人——是收买还是吓唬？给他们赏达恰（dacha，乡间宅邸），还是定个名额送西伯利亚？两样当然都行不通，因为对一个只消伸伸手就能接管全世界的人，你拿不出任何他看得上的贿赂；而一个能让你亲手割断自己喉咙的人，你也吓不住。总之，接下来发生的事——第二年圣诞节——就是他们接管了世界。那已经根本不是什么党的运动了。工人里有好多捷克人、匈牙利人和波兰人，他们想干的第一件事，就是清算几笔旧账。

“所以他们就在这儿了！在放任整个世界轰然炸响之前，他们先退到了射程之外。他们分乘两艘红海军巡洋舰，大约一千来号人，把自己撤出了俄国；然后有系统地引爆了他们能找到的每一枚弹道导弹……而所有导弹他们迟早都找得到，无非是个找不找的问题。等到安全了，他们就搬进了这儿。对他们来说，天底下最好的地方。

“他们只有一千人上下待在群岛上，群岛之外没人知道他们在哪儿。就算知道了，又顶什么用？他们杀得了任何人，在任何地方。他们杀着玩，可有时候杀也有杀的道理。他们中谁要出去游荡找乐子，必定会顺手把沿途碰上的交通、通信设施统统毁掉——尤其是现在，反正今后二十年可能用得着的东西，他们已经囤足了。二十年到期之后他们打算干什么，我们不知道。也许他们根本不在乎。换了你在乎吗？”

钱德勒干了杯中的酒，摇了摇头。“一个问题，”他说，“‘我们’是谁？”

===Original===

Hsi carefully unwrapped a package of cigarettes, took one out and lit it. He looked at it as though he were not enjoying it; cigarettes had a way of tasting stale these days. As they were. “Just a minute,” he said.

Tardily Chandler remembered the quick grasp of the waiter’s fingers on Hsi’s wrist, and that the waiter had been hovering, inconspicuously close, all through their meal. Hsi was waiting for the man to return.

In a moment the waiter was back, looking directly at Chandler. He looped his own wrist with his fingers and nodded. Hsi said softly, “‘We’ is the Society of Slaves. That’s all of us﻿—slaves﻿—but only a few of us belong to the Society. We﻿—”

There was a crash of glass. The waiter had dropped their tray.

===Chinese===

徐小心地撕开一包烟，抽出一支点上。他望着这支烟，像是不觉得香；这些日子香烟动不动就走味。也确实走了味。“稍等。”他说。

钱德勒这才后知后觉地想起侍者的手指在徐腕上那一记飞快的轻攥，想起整顿饭工夫那人一直不显山不露水地在近旁转悠。徐是在等他回来。

不一会儿侍者回来了，直望着钱德勒。他用手指圈了一下自己的手腕，点了点头。徐轻声说：“‘我们’，就是奴隶协会（the Society of Slaves）。咱们人人都是——奴隶——可入会的只是少数。我们——”

一声玻璃碎响。侍者把托盘摔了。

===Original===

Across the table from Chandler, Hsi looked suddenly changed. His left hand lay on the table before him, his right hand poised over it. Apparently he had been about to show Chandler again the sign he had made.

But he could not do it. His hand paused and fluttered, like a captured bird. Captured it was. Hsi was captured. Out of Hsi’s mouth, with Hsi’s voice, came the light, tonal rhythms of Rosalie Pan. “*This* is an unexpected pleasure, love! I never expected to see you here. Enjoying your meal?”

===Chinese===

在钱德勒对面的桌子那一侧，徐的神色忽然变了。他的左手摊在面前的桌上，右手悬在它上方。看样子，他正要再把那个暗号做给钱德勒看。

可他做不成。他的手停在半空，扑动起来，像一只被捉住的鸟。也确实被捉住了。徐被捉住了。从徐的嘴里，用徐的嗓音，传出了罗莎莉·潘（Rosalie Pan）那轻快悦耳的语调。“*这*可真是意外的惊喜，亲爱的！没想到会在这儿碰上你。饭吃得还香吗？”

## X / 十

===Original===

Three days later Koitska’s voice, coming from Chandler’s lips, summoned him out to the T.W.A. shack again.

Wise now in the ways of this world, Chandler commandeered a police car and was hurried out to the South Gate, where the guards allowed him a car of his own. The door of the building was unlocked and Chandler went right up.

He was astonished. The fat man was actually sitting up. He was fully dressed﻿—more or less; incongruously he wore flowered shorts and a bright red, short-sleeve shirt, with rope sandals. He said, “You fly a *gilikopter*? No? No difference. Help me.” An arm like a mountain went over Chandler’s shoulders. The man must have weighed three hundred pounds. Slowly, wheezing, he limped toward the back of the room and touched a button.

A door opened.

===Chinese===

三天后，一个声音借助钱德勒的嘴唇——科伊茨卡（Koitska）的声音——再次把他召往环球航空公司（T.W.A.）的那间棚屋。

如今他已谙熟这个世界的门道，钱德勒征用了一辆警车，被急急送出南门，门岗的守卫们拨了辆车归他自己用。楼门没有上锁，钱德勒径直走了上去。

他吃了一惊。那胖子居然坐了起来。他穿得整整齐齐——马马虎虎地整齐；很不相称地，他穿着印花短裤、鲜红的短袖衬衫，趿着绳编凉鞋。他说：“你会开*吉利直升机*（gilikopter）？不会？没差别。扶我一把。”一座山似的胳膊搭上了钱德勒的肩膀。这人准有三百磅重。他喘着粗气，慢慢挪到房间后头，按了一个按钮。

一扇门开了。

===Original===

Chandler had not known before that there was an elevator in the building. That was one of the things the exec did not consider important for his slaves to know. It lowered them with great grace and delicacy to the first floor, where a large old Cadillac, ancient but immaculately kept, the kind that used to be called a “gangster’s car,” waited in a private parking bay.

Chandler followed Koitska’s directions and drove to an airfield where a small, Plexiglas-nosed helicopter waited. More by the force of Chandler pushing him from behind than through his own fat thighs, Koitska puffed up the little staircase into the cabin. Originally the copter had been fitted for four passengers. Now there was the pilot’s seat and a seat beside it, and in the back a wide, soft couch. Koitska collapsed onto it. His face blanked out﻿—he was, Chandler knew, somewhere else, just then.

In a moment his eyes opened again. He looked at Chandler with no interest at all, and turned his face to the wall.

===Chinese===

钱德勒原先不知道楼里还有电梯。这类事，执政者（exec）认为不必要让他的奴隶们知道。电梯雍容而轻巧地把他们送到一楼，一部宽敞的老式凯迪拉克正停在专用车位上等着——车虽古老，保养得却纤尘不染，就是从前俗称“黑帮座车”的那一种。

钱德勒按科伊茨卡的指引，把车开到一处机场，一架有机玻璃机头的小直升机等在那里。与其说科伊茨卡靠自己的胖大腿，不如说全凭钱德勒从背后推搡，他才喘着粗气爬上小小的舷梯，进了机舱。这架直升机原本设四个乘客座。如今只剩驾驶座和旁边一个座位，后舱则是一张宽大松软的卧榻。科伊茨卡瘫了上去。他的脸一下子空了——钱德勒知道，那一刻他到别处去了。

片刻后他的眼睛又睁开了。他全无兴致地看了钱德勒一眼，把脸转向舱壁。

===Original===

After a moment he wheezed. “Sit down. At de controls.” He breathed noisily for a while. Then, “It von’t pay you to be interested in Rosalie,” he said.

Chandler was startled. He craned around in the seat but saw only Koitska’s back. “I’m not! Or anyway﻿—” But he had no place to go in that sentence, and in any case Koitska no longer seemed interested.

After a moment Koitska stirred, settled himself more comfortably, and Chandler felt himself taken. He turned to face the split wheel and the unfamiliar pedals and watched himself work the controls. It was an admirable performance. Whoever Chandler was just then﻿—he could not guess﻿—he was a first-class helicopter pilot.

===Chinese===

过了一会儿他喘息着说：“坐下。到操纵跟前去。”他大声地喘了一阵。然后说：“你对罗莎莉（Rosalie）起兴趣，对你可没好处。”

钱德勒吓了一跳。他在座位里扭身回望，却只看见科伊茨卡的背。“我没有！或者说——”可这句话他接不下去了，何况科伊茨卡也似乎不再感兴趣。

片刻后科伊茨卡动了动，把自己安置得更舒服些，钱德勒感到自己被接了过去。他转身面对那副分瓣式驾驶盘和陌生的脚蹬，看着自己操纵起来。那是一场令人叹服的表演。刚才那一瞬间钱德勒是谁——他猜不出——但那是一流的直升机飞行员。

===Original===

---

They crossed a wide body of ocean and approached another island; from one quick glance at a navigation map that his eyes had taken, Chandler guessed it to be Hilo. He landed the craft expertly on the margin of a small airstrip, where two DC-3s were already parked and being unloaded, and felt himself free again.

Two husky young men, apparently native Hawaiians by their size, rolled up a ramp and assisted Koitska down it and into a building. Chandler was left to his own devices. The building was rundown but sound. Around it stalky grass clumped, long uncut, and a few mauve and scarlet blossoms, almost hidden, showed where someone had once tended beds of bougainvillea and poinsettias. He could not guess what the building had been doing there, looking like a small office-factory combination out in the remote wilds, until he caught sight of a sign the winds had blown against a wall: **Dole**. Apparently this had been headquarters for one of the plantations. Now it was stripped almost clean inside, a welter of desks and rusted machines piled heedlessly where there once had been a parking lot. New equipment was being loaded into it from the cargo planes. Chandler recognized some of it as from the list he had given the parts man, Hsi. There also seemed to be a gasoline-driven generator﻿—a large one﻿—but what the other things were he could not guess.

Besides Koitska, there were at least five coronet-wearing execs visible around the place. Chandler was not surprised. It would have to be something big to winkle these torpid slugs out of their shells, but he knew what it was, and that it was big enough to them indeed; in fact, it was their lives. He deduced that Koitska’s plans for his future comfort required a standby transmitter to service the coronets, in case something went wrong. And clearly it was this that they were to put together here.

===Chinese===

---

他们飞越一片宽阔的海面，逼近另一座岛；从他的眼睛匆匆扫过的一张航空图上，钱德勒猜那是希洛（Hilo）。他熟练地把飞机降落在一条小型跑道边缘，那里已经停着两架DC-3，正在卸货。随后他又感到自己自由了。

两个魁梧的年轻人——从块头看显然是土生土长的夏威夷人——推来一架坡板，搀扶科伊茨卡下去，进了楼。钱德勒没人管了，只好自便。这栋楼破败却结实。楼四周丛生着长长的茎草，久未刈割，几丛淡紫和猩红的花几乎被埋没，昭示着从前有人侍弄过叶子花和一品红的花圃。他猜不出这样一栋楼、一副小型办公加厂房的作派，待在这片荒僻野地里是干什么的，直到他看见一块被风刮到墙根的牌子：**都乐**（Dole）。这里显然曾是某座种植园的总部。如今楼里已被抽剥得几乎精光，一堆堆桌椅和锈机器胡乱摞在原先的停车场上。新设备正从运输机里卸下来往楼里搬。钱德勒认出其中一些，正是他开给零件商徐（Hsi）的那张单子上的东西。似乎还有一台汽油发电机——大号的——其余那些是什么，他就猜不出了。

除科伊茨卡外，这地方还看得见至少五个戴头冠（coronet）的执政者。钱德勒并不意外。要诱使这些怠惰的鼻涕虫从壳里探出来，非得是件大事不可，而他知道那是什么，也知道对他们而言确实够大——事实上，那就是他们的性命。他推断，科伊茨卡为他日后的舒坦所做的筹划，需要一台备用发射机来伺候那些头冠，以防万一出岔子。显然，他们要在这里装配的就是它。

===Original===

For ten hours, while the afternoon became dark night, they worked at a furious pace. When the sun set one of the execs gestured and the generator was started, rocking on its rubber-tired wheels as its rotors spun and fumes chugged out, and they worked on by strings of incandescent lights. It was pick-and-shovel work for Chandler, no engineering, just unloading and roughly grouping the equipment where it was ready to be assembled. The execs did not take part in the work. Nor were they idle. They busied themselves in one room of the building with some small device﻿—Chandler could not see what﻿—and when he looked again it was gone. He did not see them take it away and did not know where it was taken. Toward midnight he suddenly realized that it was likely some essential part which they would not permit anyone but themselves to handle, and that, no doubt, was why they had come in person, instead of working through proxies.

Just before they left Koitska and two or three of the other execs quizzed him briefly. He was too tired to think beyond the questions, but they seemed to be trying to find out if he was able to do the simpler parts of the construction without supervision, and they seemed satisfied with the answers. He flew the helicopter home, with someone else guilding his arms and legs, but he was half asleep as he did it, and he never quite remembered how he managed to get back to his room at Tripler.

===Chinese===

整整十个钟头，从午后干到夜色深浓，他们以一种狂暴的速度赶工。日头落山时，一个执政者打了个手势，发电机便开动起来，转子飞旋，烟气突突地喷，机身架在胶轮上摇摇晃晃；人们就着一串串白炽灯继续干。对钱德勒来说这是抡镐挥锹的粗活，谈不上工程，无非是卸货，再把设备大致归置到待装配的位置上。执政者们不参与干活，却也并不闲着。他们在楼里一个房间中围着一件什么小东西忙碌——钱德勒看不清是什么——等他再看时，那东西没了。他没看见他们把它拿走，也不知道拿去了哪里。近午夜时他忽然明白了：那多半是某个关键部件，他们绝不肯让旁人经手；毫无疑问，这正是他们亲自到场、而不通过代理行事的原因。

临走前，科伊茨卡和另外两三个执政者简短地盘问了他一番。他累得顾不上多想，但他们似乎是想弄清，他能否无人监督地完成装配中较简单的部分，而他们对他的回答似乎还算满意。回程的直升机是别人在操纵他的手脚飞的，他一路半睡半醒，后来怎么回到特里普勒（Tripler）他自己房里的，他再也记不清了。

===Original===

---

The next morning he went back to Parts ’n Plenty with an additional list, covering replacement of some parts that had been damaged. Hsi glanced at it quickly and nodded. “All this stuff I have. You can pick it up this afternoon if you like.”

Chandler offered him a cigarette out of a stale pack. “About the other night﻿—”

Hsi began to perspire, but he said, casually enough, “Interested in baseball?”

“Baseball?”

===Chinese===

---

第二天早晨，他揣着一张补充单子又去了“零件多多”（Parts ’n Plenty），单子上是些损坏部件的替换品。徐扫了一眼，点点头。“这些东西我都有。愿意的话，今天下午就可以来取。”

钱德勒从一盒走味的烟里递给他一支。“那天夜里的事——”

徐额头开始冒汗，但他的话说得相当随意：“对棒球感兴趣吗？”

“棒球？”

===Original===

Hsi said, as though there had been nothing incongruous about the question, “There’ll be a Little League game this afternoon. Back of the school on Punahou and Wilder. I thought I might stop by, then we can come back and pick up the rest of your gear. Two o’clock. Hope I’ll see you.”

Chandler walked away thoughtfully. He had no real intention of going there, but something in Hsi’s attitude suggested more than a ball game; after a quick and poor lunch he decided to go.

The field was a dirty playground, scuffed out of what had probably once been an attractive campus. The players were ten-year-olds, of the mixture of hair colors and complexions typical of the islands. Chandler was puzzled. Surely even the wildest baseball rooter wouldn’t go far out of his way for this, and yet there was an audience of at least fifty adults watching the game. And none seemed to be related to the ballplayers. The Little Leaguers played grave, careful ball, and the audience watched them without a word of parental encouragement or joy.

Hsi approached him from the shadow of the school building. “Glad you could make it, Chandler. No, no questions. Just watch.”

===Chinese===

徐说下去，仿佛这问题没有半点突兀：“今天下午有一场少年棒球联盟（Little League）的比赛。就在普纳荷街和怀尔德街交口那所学校的后面。我想我兴许会过去看看，回头咱们再回来取你剩下的东西。两点钟。希望见到你。”

钱德勒若有所思地走开了。他原本并没真打算去，可徐的态度里透着比一场球赛更多的东西；胡乱吃过一顿匆匆的午饭后，他决定去。

球场是一片脏兮兮的操场，从一片大概曾经很悦目的校园里生生磨出来的。球员都是十岁的孩子，发色肤色驳杂，正是海岛典型的混血模样。钱德勒纳闷了。就算是天字第一号的棒球迷，也犯不上专程跑来看这个呀，可场边竟至少有五十个成年人在看球。而且看样子没有一个和球员沾亲带故。少年联盟的孩子们打得一本正经、小心翼翼，观众看着他们，没有一句做父母的喝彩或欢喜。

徐从校舍的阴影里朝他走来。“真高兴你来了，钱德勒。别问，什么都别问。看就是了。”

===Original===

In the fifth inning, with the score aggregating around thirty, there was an interruption. A tall, redheaded man glanced at his watch, licked his lips, took a deep breath and walked out onto the diamond. He glanced at the crowd, while the kids suspended play without surprise. Then the redheaded man nodded to the umpire and stepped off the field. The ballplayers resumed their game, but now the whole attention of the audience was on the redheaded man.

Suspicion crossed Chandler’s mind. In a moment it was confirmed, as the redheaded man raised his hands waist high and clasped his right hand around his left wrist﻿—only for a moment, but that was enough.

The ball game was a cover. Chandler was present at a meeting of what Hsi had called The Society of Slaves, the underground that dared to pit itself against the execs.

Hsi cleared his throat and said, “This is the one. I vouch for him.” And that was startling too, Chandler thought, because all these wrist-circled men and women were looking at *him*.

===Chinese===

第五局，比分累计已有三十分上下，场上出现了中断。一个高个子红发男人看了看表，舔了舔嘴唇，深吸一口气，走进内场。他扫视人群，孩子们毫不意外地暂停了比赛。随后红发男人朝裁判点了点头，退出场地。球员们继续比赛，可此刻观众的全部注意力都落在了红发男人身上。

一个疑念掠过钱德勒的心头。很快它就被证实了：红发男人把双手抬到齐腰的高度，右手扣住了自己的左腕——只是一瞬，但已经够了。

球赛只是幌子。钱德勒置身于一场集会之中——徐所说的奴隶协会（The Society of Slaves），那个胆敢与执政者们对抗的地下组织。

徐清了清嗓子，说：“就是这个人。我为他担保。”这也够叫人心惊的，钱德勒想，因为所有这些腕子被扣住过的男男女女，都在盯着*他*看。

===Original===

---

“All right,” said the redheaded man nervously, “let’s get started then. First thing, anybody got any weapons? Sure? Take a look﻿—we don’t want any slipups. Turn out your pockets.”

There was a flurry and a woman near Chandler held up a key ring with a tiny knife on it “Penknife? Hell, yes; get rid of it. Throw it in the outfield. You can pick it up after the meeting.” A hundred eyes watched the pearly object fly. “We ought to be all right here,” said the redheaded man. “The kids have been playing every day this week and nobody looked in. But *watch your neighbor*. See anything suspicious, don’t wait. Don’t take a chance. Holler ‘Kill the umpire!’ or anything you like, but holler. Good and loud.” He paused, breathing hard. “All right, Hsi. Introduce him.”

The parts man took Chandler firmly by the shoulder. “This fellow has something for us,” he said. “He’s working for the exec Koitska, building what can’t be anything else but a duplicate of the machine that they use to control us. He﻿—”

“Wait a minute!” A bearded man came forward and peered furiously into Chandler’s face. “Look at his head! Don’t you see he’s branded?”

===Chinese===

---

“好，”红发男人紧张地说，“那就开始吧。第一件事，谁身上带武器了？确定？都摸摸——我们可不能出岔子。把口袋都翻出来。”

一阵窸窣，钱德勒身边一个女人举起一串钥匙，上头挂着一把小刀。“铅笔刀？当然也算；处理掉。扔到外场去。散会以后再捡。”一百只眼睛望着那件小东西抛物般飞出去。“这地方应该没事，”红发男人说，“孩子们这礼拜天天在这儿打球，没人往里看过。但*盯住你旁边的人*。看见任何可疑的，别等。别赌运气。就喊‘杀了裁判！’或者随你喊什么，反正要喊。喊响一点。”他停下来喘了几口粗气。“好了，徐。介绍他吧。”

零件商一把攥住钱德勒的肩膀。“这位老兄给我们带来了点东西，”他说，“他在替执政者科伊茨卡干活，造的那台东西，除了他们用来控制我们的那台机器的复制品，不可能是别的。他——”

“等一下！”一个大胡子男人挤上前来，怒冲冲地逼视钱德勒的脸。“看看他的脑门！你们没看见他烙了印吗？”

===Original===

Chandler touched his scar as the man with the beard hissed, “Damned hoaxer! This is the lowest species of life on the face of the earth﻿—someone who pretended to be possessed in order to do some damned dirty act What was it, hoaxer? Murder? Burning babies alive?”

Hsi economically let go of Chandler’s shoulder, half turned the bearded man with one hand and swung with the other. “Shut up, Linton. Wait till you hear what he’s got for us.”

The bearded man, sprawling and groggy, slowly rose as Hsi explained tersely what he had guessed of Chandler’s work﻿—as much as Chandler himself knew, it seemed. “Maybe this is only a duplicate. Maybe it won’t be used. But maybe it will﻿—and Chandler’s the man who can sabotage it! How would you like that? The execs switching over to this equipment while the other one is down for maintenance﻿—and their headsets don’t work!”

===Chinese===

钱德勒摸了摸自己的伤疤，大胡子男人嘶声骂道：“该死的骗子！这是地表上最低等的生物——装作被附身好去干那种卑鄙龌龊勾当的东西。是什么来着，骗子？谋杀？活烧婴儿？”

徐利落地放开钱德勒的肩膀，一只手把大胡子半拨了个转，另一只手就是一拳。“闭嘴，林顿（Linton）。等听完他给我们带来什么你再嚷。”

大胡子东倒西歪、晕头转向地慢慢爬起来，徐一边简短地讲着他从钱德勒的活计里猜到的东西——看样子跟钱德勒自己知道的一样多。“也许这只是台复制品。也许根本不会启用。可万一启用呢——而钱德勒，正是能对它动手脚的人！你们想想要是那样：正机停机检修，执政者们换用这台设备——结果他们的头戴机不好使了！”

===Original===

There was a terrible silence, except for the sounds of the children playing ball. Two runs had just scored. Chandler recognized the silence. It was hope.

Linton broke it, his blue eyes gleaming above the beard. “No! Better than that. Why wait? We can *use* this fellow’s machine. Set it up, get us some headsets﻿—and we can control the execs themselves!”

===Chinese===

一阵可怕的沉默，只剩下孩子们打球的声响。刚刚得了两分。钱德勒认得这沉默。那是希望。

打破沉默的是林顿，蓝眼睛在胡子上面灼灼发光。“不！比那更好。干吗等？我们可以*用*这家伙造的机器！把它架起来，弄几副头戴机——咱们就能反过来控制执政者们！”

===Original===

---

The silence was even longer; then there was a babble of discussion, but Chandler did not take part in it. He was thinking. It was a tremendous thought.

Suppose a man like himself were actually able to do what they wanted of him. Never mind the practical difficulties﻿—learning how it worked, getting a headset, bypassing the traps Koitska would surely have set to prevent just that. Never mind the penalties for failure. Suppose he could make it work, and find fifty headsets, and fit them to the fifty men and women here in this clandestine meeting of the Society of Slaves.﻿ ﻿…

Would there, after all, be any change worth mentioning in the state of the world?

Or was Lord Acton, always and everywhere, right? Power corrupts. Absolute power corrupts absolutely. The power locked in the coronets of the exec was more than flesh and blood could stand; he could almost sense the rot in those near him at the mere thought.

===Chinese===

---

沉默更长了；随后响起一片嘁嘁喳喳的讨论，钱德勒没有参与。他在思考。那是一个惊天动地的念头。

设想一个像他这样的人，真的能办到他们要求的事。且不管那些实际的难关——弄懂它的原理，搞到一副头戴机，绕开科伊茨卡必定为防这一手而设下的圈套。且不管失败的代价。设想他能让它运转起来，还找来五十副头戴机，戴到奴隶协会这场秘密集会里这五十个男女的头上。……

到头来，世界的局面会有什么值得一提的改变吗？

还是说，阿克顿勋爵（Lord Acton）那句话，无论何时何地都成立？权力使人腐化，绝对的权力使人绝对腐化。禁锢在执政者头冠里的那种权力，远非血肉之躯所能承受；仅仅这么一想，他几乎就能嗅到身旁众人身上散出的腐味。

===Original===

But Hsi was throwing cold water on the idea. “Sorry, but I know that much: One exec can’t control another. The headpieces insulate against control. Well.” He glanced at his watch. “We agreed on twenty minutes maximum for this meeting,” he reminded the redheaded man, who nodded.

“You’re right.” He glanced around the group. “I’ll make the rest of it fast. News: You all know they got some more of us last week. Have you all been by the Monument? Three of our comrades were still there this morning. But I don’t think they know we’re organized, they think it’s only individual acts of sabotage. In case any of you don’t know, the execs can’t read our minds. Not even when they’re controlling us. Proof is we’re all still alive. Hanrahan knew practically every one of us, and he’s been lying out there for a week with a broken back, ever since they caught him trying to blow up the guard pits at East Gate. They had plenty of chance to pump him if they could. *They can’t.* Next thing. No more individual attacks on one exec. Not unless it’s a matter of life and death, and even then you’re wasting your time unless you’ve got a gun. They can grab your mind faster than you can cut a throat. Third thing: Don’t get the idea there are good execs and bad execs. Once they put that thing on their heads they’re all the same. Fourth thing. You can’t make deals. They aren’t that worried. So if anybody’s thinking of selling out﻿—I’m not saying anyone is﻿—forget it.” He looked around. “Anything else?”

“What about germ warfare in the water supply?” somebody ventured.

===Chinese===

可徐正在给这个念头泼冷水。“抱歉，这个我还是知道的：一个执政者控制不了另一个。头戴装置本身就能隔绝控制。就这样。”他看了看表。“我们说好这次集会最长二十分钟。”他提醒红发男人，后者点了点头。

“你说得对。”他环视众人。“剩下的我拣快的说。消息：大家都知道，上礼拜他们又抓了我们一些人。都去纪念碑（the Monument）那儿看过了吗？今早我们还有三位同志挂在那儿。不过我认为他们不知道我们有组织，他们以为只是零星的破坏行动。怕有人不知道，再重复一遍：执政者们读不了我们的心。哪怕正在控制我们的时候也读不了。证据就是我们都还活着。汉拉汉（Hanrahan）几乎认识我们每一个人，自从他们抓到他炸东门守卫坑，他断了脊梁在那儿躺了一个礼拜。他们要是能撬开他的嘴，有的是机会。*他们不能。*第二件事。不许再单独袭击哪一个执政者。除非是生死关头，而就算是生死关头，手里没枪也是白搭。他们攫住你的头脑，比你割断一条喉咙还快。第三件事：别做梦有什么好执政者坏执政者。那东西往头上一扣，他们全是一个样。第四件事。跟他们做不成交易。他们没那么慌。所以谁要是想出卖大家——我没说谁在想——趁早死了这条心。”他环顾一圈。“还有别的吗？”

“往水源里投细菌武器这一手呢？”有人斗胆问。

===Original===

“Still looking into it. No report yet. All right, that’s enough for now. Meeting’s adjourned. Watch the ball game for a while, then drift away. *One at a time.*”

Hsi was the first to go, then a couple of women together, then a sprinkling of other men. Chandler was in no particular hurry, although it seemed time to leave anyway, because the ball game appeared to be over. A ten-year-old with freckles on his face was at the plate, but he was leaning on his bat, staring at Chandler with wide, serious eyes.

===Chinese===

“还在研究。还没有结论。好，暂时到此为止。散会。先看一会儿球，然后陆续散开。*一次走一个。*”

徐头一个走，接着两个女人同行，然后男人们三三两两地散去。钱德勒并不着急，虽然看起来也该走了，因为球赛像是收场了。一个脸上有雀斑的十岁孩子站在本垒板上，却倚着球棒，睁着一双严肃的大眼睛，直勾勾地盯着钱德勒。

===Original===

Chandler felt a sudden chill.

He turned, began to walk away﻿—and felt himself seized.

===Chinese===

钱德勒突然感到一阵寒意。

他转过身，抬脚要走——却感到自己被攫住了。

===Original===

---

He walked slowly into the schoolhouse, unable to look around. Behind him he heard a confused sob, tears and a child’s voice trying to blubber through: “Something *funny* happened.”

If the child had been an adult it might have been warning enough. But the child had never experienced possession before, was not sure enough, was clear into the schoolhouse before the remaining members of the Society of Slaves awoke to their danger. He heard a quick cry of *They got him!* Then Chandler’s legs stopped walking and he addressed himself savagely. A few yards away a stout Chinese lady was mopping the tiles; she looked up at him, startled, but no more startled than Chandler was himself. “You idiot!” Chandler blazed. “Why do you have to get mixed up in this? Don’t you know it’s wrong, love? Stay here!” Chandler commanded himself. “Don’t you *dare* leave this building!”

And he was free again, but there was a sudden burst of screams from outside.

===Chinese===

---

他缓缓走进校舍，没法回头张望。身后他听见一阵含混的呜咽，泪水和一个孩子的声音挣着要说出来：“发生了*好怪*的事。”

那孩子若是个成年人，这警告也许就够了。可这孩子从没经历过附身，吃不准是怎么回事，等奴隶协会余下的会员们醒悟到危险时，他已经走进校舍深处了。他听见一声急促的呼喊：*他们抓住他了！*接着钱德勒的双腿停步，他对自己破口大骂。几步开外，一位壮实的中国太太正在拖地砖；她受惊地抬头看他，但再惊也惊不过钱德勒本人。“你这个蠢货！”钱德勒咆哮，“你干嘛非得掺和进来？你不知道这是犯罪吗，亲爱的？待在这儿！”钱德勒命令自己，“你*敢*离开这栋楼试试！”

随后他又自由了，可外面陡然爆起一片尖叫。

===Original===

Bewildered, Chandler stood for a moment, as little able to move as though the girl still had him under control. Then he leaped through a classroom to a window, staring. Outside in the playground there was wild confusion. Half the spectators were on the ground, trying to rise. As he watched, a teenage boy hurled himself at an elderly lady, the two of them falling. Another man flung himself to the ground. A woman swung her pocketbook into the face of the man next to her. One of the fallen ones rose, only to trip himself again. It was a mad spectacle, but Chandler understood it: What he was watching was a single member of the exec trying to keep a group of twenty ordinary, unarmed human beings in line. The exec was leaping from mind to mind; even so, the crowd was beginning to scatter.

Without thought Chandler started to leap out to help them; but the possessor had anticipated that. He was caught at the door. He whirled and ran toward the woman with the mop; as he was released, the woman flung herself upon him, knocking him down.

By the time he was able to get up again it was far too late to help﻿ ﻿… if there ever had been a time when he could have been of any real help.

===Chinese===

钱德勒茫茫然站了片刻，动弹不得，仿佛那姑娘仍攥着他。随即他冲过一间教室，扑到窗前张望。外面操场上乱成一片。半数观众倒在地上，挣扎着要爬起来。他眼看着一个十几岁的男孩扑向一位老太太，两人一起摔倒；另一个男人自己扑倒在地；一个女人抡起手提包砸在旁边男人脸上；一个倒地的刚爬起来，又自己绊了自己一跤。那是一派疯癫的景象，但钱德勒看懂了：他目睹的，是一名执政者想凭一己之力镇住二十个普通的、手无寸铁的人。那执政者在一颗颗头脑之间跳跃腾挪；饶是如此，人群还是开始溃散。

钱德勒不假思索就要跳出去帮他们；可那附主早料到了这一手。他在门口被攫住。他猛地转身，朝那个拖地的女人跑去；就在他被放开的一瞬，那女人扑到他身上，把他撞倒在地。

等他再爬起来，去救人已经太迟了……况且，是否真有过一个他帮得上忙的时刻，也难说得很。

===Original===

He heard shots. Two policeman had come running into the playground, with guns drawn.

The exec who had looked at him out of the boy’s eyes, who had penetrated this nest of enemies and extricated Chandler from it, had taken first things first. Help had been summoned. Quick as the coronets worked, it was no time at all until the nearest persons with weapons were located, commandeered and in action.

Two minutes later there no longer was resistance.

Obviously more execs had come to help, attracted by the commotion perhaps, or summoned at some stolen moment after the meeting had first been invaded. There were only five survivors on the field. Each was clearly controlled. They rose and stood patiently while the two police shot them, shot them, paused to reload and shot again. The last to die was the bearded man, Linton, and as he fell his eyes brushed Chandler’s.

===Chinese===

他听见了枪声。两名警察拔枪冲进了操场。

那个曾从男孩眼中打量他、潜入这座敌巢又把钱德勒从中摘出来的执政者，办起事来轻重分明。援手已被召来。头冠的运作快得很：定位、征用、驱动最近的带枪者投入行动，前后不过一瞬。

两分钟后，抵抗不复存在。

显然来了更多的执政者帮忙，也许是循着骚动而来，也许是在集会暴露后的某个见缝插针的当口被召来的。场上只剩下五个活着的人。每一个显然都被控制着。他们站起来，耐心地立着，任那两名警察开枪、开枪、停下装弹、再开枪。最后死的是大胡子林顿，倒下去的时候，他的目光擦过了钱德勒。

===Original===

Chandler leaned against a wall.

It had been a terrible sight. The nearness of his own death had been almost the least of it.

He had no doubt of the identity of the exec who had saved him and destroyed the others. Though he had heard the voice only as it came from his own mouth, he could not miss it. It was Rosalie Pan.

He looked out at the redheaded man, sprawled across the foul line behind third base, and remembered what he had said. There weren’t any good execs or bad execs. There were only execs.

===Chinese===

钱德勒靠在一堵墙上。

方才的景象骇人至极。他自己的死期如此之近，在那一切里几乎算是最不重要的一桩。

救了他、又毁了其余众人的那个执政者是谁，他没有半点疑问。那声音虽只是从他自己的嘴里发出，他却绝不会听错。那是罗莎莉·潘（Rosalie Pan）。

他望向外面那个红发男人，横陈在三垒后方的界线上，想起了他说过的话。没有什么好执政者、坏执政者。只有执政者。

## XI / 十一

===Original===

Whatever Chandler’s life might be worth, he knew he had given it away and the girl had given it back to him.

He did not see her for several days, but the morning after the massacre he woke to find a note beside his bed table. No one had been in the room. It was his own sleeping hand that had written it, though the girl’s mind had moved his fingers:

> If you get mixed up in anything like that again I won’t be able to help you. So don’t! Those people are just using you, you know. Don’t throw away your chances. Do you like surfboarding?

> — Rosie

But by then there was no time for surfboarding, or for anything else but work. The construction job on Hilo had begun, and it was a nightmare. He was flown to the island with the last load of parts. No execs were present in the flesh, but in the first day Chandler lost count of how many different minds possessed his own. He began to be able to recognize them by a limp as he walked, by tags of German as he spoke, by a stutter, a distinctive gesture of annoyance, an expletive. As he was a trained engineer he was left to labor by himself for hours on end. It was worse for the others.
