There seemed to be a dozen execs hovering invisible around all the time; no sooner was a worker released by one than he was seized by another. The work progressed rapidly, but at the cost of utter exhaustion. By the end of the fourth day Chandler had eaten only two meals and could not remember when he had slept last. He found himself staggering when free, and furious with the fatigue-clumsiness of his own body when possessed. At sundown on the fourth day he found himself free for a moment and, incredibly, without work of his own to do just then, until someone else completed a job of patchwiring. He stumbled out into the open air and had time only to gaze around for a moment before his eyes began to close. This must once have been a lovely island. Even unkempt as it was, the trees were tall and beautiful. Beyond them a wisp of smoke was pale against the dark-blue evening sky; the breeze was scented.﻿ ﻿… He woke and found he was already back in the building, reaching for his soldering gun.

===Chinese===

不管钱德勒这条命值几个钱，他知道，是他自己把它送了出去，而那姑娘又把它还给了他。

此后他一连几天没见到她，但屠杀过后的那个早晨，他醒来发现床头几上放着一张字条。没有任何人进过房间。那是他自己熟睡的手写下的，动他手指的却是那姑娘的心智：

> 你要是再掺和进那种事里，我就帮不了你了。所以别！那些人只是在利用你，你知道的。别把你的机会白白扔掉。你喜欢冲浪吗？

> ——罗西（Rosie）

可那时候已经没有工夫冲浪，除了干活，什么工夫都没有了。希洛的装配工程开工了，那是一场噩梦。他跟着最后一批零件被空运到岛上。没有一位执政者肉身在场，可头一天里，附上他身的心智有多少个不同的，钱德勒就数不清了。他渐渐能认出他们来：从走路时的一条瘸腿，从说话时蹦出的几个德语词，从一记口吃、一个特有的恼怒手势、一句口头禅。由于他是受训过的工程师，人家还肯让他一连独自干上几个钟头。其他人就更惨了。似乎总有十来个执政者无影无形地在四周盘旋；一个工人刚被这一个放开，立刻又被那一个攫住。工程进展飞快，代价却是彻底的精疲力竭。到第四天结束时，钱德勒统共只吃过两顿饭，上一觉是什么时候睡的都不记得了。他发现自己一旦自由就摇摇晃晃，一旦被附身又对自己那副累笨的身体暴怒不已。第四天日落时分，他有片刻的自由，而且难以置信地，手头居然一时没有自己的活儿——直到别人把一段接线补完。他踉跄着走到露天里，只来得及环顾一眼，眼皮就开始往下沉。这里从前准是座可爱的岛。纵然无人打理，树木依然高大秀美。树梢之外，一缕炊烟衬着深蓝的暮空，显得苍白；微风带着香气。……他一睁眼，发现自己已经回到楼里，手正伸向焊枪。

===Original===

There came a point at which even the will of the execs was unable to drive the flogged bodies farther, and then they were permitted to sleep for a few hours. At daybreak they were awake again. The sleep was not enough. The bodies were slow and inaccurate. Two of the Hawaiians, straining a hundred-pound component into place, staggered, slipped﻿—and dropped it.

Appalled, Chandler waited for them to kill themselves.

But it seemed that the execs were tiring too. One of the Hawaiians said irritably, with an accent Chandler did not recognize: “That’s pau. All right, you morons, you’ve won yourselves a vacation; we’ll have to fly you in replacements. Take the day off.” And incredibly all eleven of the haggard wrecks stumbling around the building were free at once.

The first thought of every man was to eat, to relieve himself, to remove a shoe and ease a blistered foot﻿—to do any of the things they had not been permitted to do. The second thought was sleep.

===Chinese===

到了某一个节骨眼上，连执政者们的意志也没法再驱赶这些被抽打过的躯体了，这才准他们睡上几个钟头。天一亮他们又都被弄醒。觉根本不够。躯体迟钝而不听使唤。两个夏威夷人抬着一只百磅重的部件往卡座里送，脚下一晃，一滑——部件脱了手。

钱德勒骇然，等着他们把自己弄死。

可执政者们看来也乏了。一个夏威夷人烦躁地开了口，带着一种钱德勒听不出的口音：“这下帕乌（pau，完事）了。行了，你们这帮蠢货，给你们自己挣来一天假；我们只好再给你们空运替换的人手来。今天歇工吧。”于是难以置信地，围着这栋楼跌跌撞撞的十一具形容枯槁的残躯，一下子全自由了。

每个人的头一个念头都是吃，是解手，是脱掉一只鞋、让磨出泡的脚松快松快——去做一切他们不被准许做的事。第二个念头是睡觉。

===Original===

Chandler dropped off at once, but he was overtired; he slept fitfully, and after an hour or two of turning on the hard ground sat up, blinking red-eyed around. He had been slow. The cushioned seats in the aircraft and cars were already taken. He stood up, stretched, scratched himself and wondered what to do next, and he remembered the thread of smoke he had seen﻿—when? three nights ago?﻿—against the evening sky.

In all those hours he had not had time to think one obvious thought: There should have been no smoke there! The island was supposed to be deserted.

He stood up, looked around to get his bearings, and started off in the direction he remembered.

===Chinese===

钱德勒倒头就睡，可他是过劳的人；睡得断断续续，在硬地上翻了一两个钟头，便坐起身，红着眼睛眨巴着四下张望。他迟了一步。飞机和汽车里有垫子的座位都让人占了。他站起来，伸了个懒腰，挠了挠身子，琢磨接下来干什么，然后想起了那缕他曾望见的烟——什么时候来着？三个晚上以前？——衬在暮色天空上的那一缕。

这许多个钟头里，他一直没工夫去想一个明摆着的念头：那里本不该有烟！这岛本该是座荒岛。

他站起身，环顾四周认清方位，朝他记得的方向出发了。

===Original===

---

It was good to own his body again, in poor condition as it was. It was delicious to be allowed to think consecutive thoughts.

The chemistry of the human animal is such that it heals whatever thrusts it may receive from the outside world. Short of death, its only incapacitating wound comes from itself; from the outside it can survive astonishing blows, rise again and flourish. Chandler was not flourishing, but he had begun to rise.

Time had been so compressed and blurred in the days since the slaughter at the Punahou School that he had not had time to grieve over the deaths of his briefly-met friends, or even to think of their quixotic plans against the execs. Now he began to wonder.

He understood with what thrill of hope he had been received﻿—a man like themselves, not an exec, whose touch was at the very center of the exec power. But how firm was that touch? Was there really anything he could do?

===Chinese===

---

重新拥有自己的身体真好，哪怕这身体破败不堪。能被允许连贯地思考，真是美妙。

人这种动物的化学机制就是如此：外部世界加诸它的任何创击，它都能愈合。除死之外，它唯一致命的创伤来自它自己；而来自外部的，再骇人的重击它也能挺过来，重新站起，重新繁盛。钱德勒谈不上繁盛，但他已经开始站起来了。

自普纳荷学校（Punahou School）那场屠杀以来的这些天，时间被压缩得模糊一团，他一直没工夫为那些结识未久的朋友之死悲伤，甚至没工夫想想他们那些堂吉诃德式的反执政者大计。现在，他开始想了。

他明白自己是被怎样一种希望的战栗接纳的——一个和他们一样的人，不是执政者，一伸手就能碰到执政者权柄的正中心。可这一伸手到底有多硬的把握？他真有什么能做的吗？

===Original===

It seemed not. He barely understood the mechanics of what he was doing, far less the theory behind it. Conceivably knowing where this installation was he could somehow get back to it when it was completed. In theory it might be that there was a way to dispense with the headsets and exert power from the big board itself.

A Cro-Magnard at the controls of a nuclear-laden jet bomber could destroy a city. Nothing stopped him. Nothing but his own invincible ignorance. Chandler was that Cro-Magnard; certainly power was here to grasp, but he had no way of knowing how to pick it up.

Still﻿—where there was life there was hope. He decided he was wasting time that would not come again. He had been wandering along a road that led into a small town, quite deserted, but this was no time for wandering. His place was back at the installation, studying, scheming, trying to understand all he could. He began to turn, and stopped.

“Great God,” he said softly, looking at what he had just seen. The town was deserted of life, but not of death.

===Chinese===

看来没有。他连自己在干的东西的机械构造都只懂个皮毛，更别说背后的理论了。勉强可以说的是，知道了这座设施的位置，等它建成之后，他或许还能设法回到这里。理论上，也许存在一种办法：甩开头戴机，直接从主控制台上行使权力。

一个坐在满载核弹的喷气轰炸机操纵台前的克罗马农人，可以毁掉一座城市。什么也拦不住他。拦住他的只有他自己那战无不胜的无知。钱德勒就是那个克罗马农人；权力确实就摆在这里任人抓取，可他无从知道该怎么把它拿起来。

不过——留得性命在，总还有希望。他断定自己是在浪费一去不返的时间。他方才正沿着一条通往一座小镇的路信步走着，镇子全然荒废，可这不是闲逛的时候。他的位置在那座设施那儿，去研究，去谋划，尽力多弄懂一点。他刚要转身，却停住了。

“伟大的上帝啊。”他轻声说，望着自己刚看见的东西。这镇子没有了生者，却不缺死者。

===Original===

---

There were bodies everywhere.

They were long dead, perhaps years. They seemed natural and right as they lay there. It was not surprising they had escaped his notice at first. Little was left but bones and an occasional desiccated leathery rag that might have been a face. The clothing was faded and rotted away; but enough was left of the bodies and the clothes to make it clear that none of these people had died natural deaths. A rusted blade in a chest cage showed where a knife had pierced a heart; a small skull near his feet (with a scrap of faded blue rompers near it) was shattered. On a flagstone terrace a family group of bones lay radiating outward, like a rosette. Something had exploded there and caught them all as they turned to flee. There was a woman’s face, grained like oak and eyeless, visible between the fender of a truck and a crushed-in wall.

Like exhumed Pompeii, the tragedy was so ancient that it aroused only wonder. The whole town had been blotted out.

The execs did not take chances; apparently they had sterilized the whole island﻿—probably had sterilized all of them except Oahu itself, to make certain that their isolation was complete, except for the captive stock allowed to breed and serve them in and around Honolulu.

Chandler prowled the town for a quarter of an hour, but one street was like another. The bodies did not seem to have been disturbed even by animals, but perhaps there were none big enough to show traces of such work.

===Chinese===

---

尸体遍地都是。

他们死了很久了，也许有几年。横陈在那里，他们显得自然而又妥帖。起初没引起他的注意，并不奇怪。剩下的差不多只有骸骨，偶尔还有一片干缩如革的破布，也许是张脸。衣物都已褪色朽烂；但残存的骸骨和衣着足以说明，这些人没有一个死于自然。一柄锈刃插在胸腔里，标出一刀穿心的位置；他脚边一枚小小的颅骨（旁边搭着褪色的蓝色连身童衣的一角）被砸得粉碎。石板露台上，一家的骸骨呈放射状四散躺着，像一朵玫瑰花结。什么东西在那里炸开了，在他们转身逃命时把他们一网打尽。一张女人的脸——木纹般皴裂、没有眼睛——从卡车挡泥板和一面砸塌的墙之间露出来。

像出土的庞贝古城，这场悲剧古老得只唤起惊叹。全镇的人都已被抹掉。

执政者们不冒任何风险；显然他们把整座岛都“消了毒”——多半把除瓦胡岛（Oahu）以外的所有岛都消了毒，以确保与世隔绝的彻底，只留下一群圈养的活口，准许他们在檀香山（Honolulu）内外生养繁衍、供他们驱使。

钱德勒在镇子里转悠了一刻钟，可一条街和另一条街没什么两样。尸体似乎连野兽都没有惊动过，不过也许这里根本没有大到能留下痕迹的野兽。

===Original===

Something moved in a doorway.

Chandler thought at once of the smoke he had seen, but no one answered his call and, though he searched, he could neither see nor hear anything alive.

The search was a waste of time. It also wasted his best chance to study the thing he was building. As he returned to the cinder-block structure at the end of the airstrip he heard motors and looked up to see a plane circling in for a landing.

He knew that he had only a few minutes. He spent those minutes as thriftily as he could, but long before he could even grasp the circuitry of the parts he had not himself worked on he felt a touch at his mind. The plane was rolling to a stop. He and all of them hurried over to begin unloading it.

===Chinese===

一个门洞里有什么东西动了一下。

钱德勒立刻想起他望见的那缕烟，可他呼喊无人应答，尽管四下搜寻，他也看不见、听不见任何活物。

这一搜是白费时间。还浪费了他研究自己那台造物的最好机会。等他回到跑道尽头那栋煤渣砖建筑时，他听见了引擎声，抬头看见一架飞机正盘旋着进场降落。

他知道只有几分钟工夫。他把这几分钟用得尽可能俭省，可离弄懂那些他没亲手装过的部件的电路还差得远，他就感到心智被碰了一下。飞机滑行着停稳了。他和所有人一起赶紧跑过去卸货。

===Original===

The plane was stopped with one wingtip almost touching the building, heading directly into it﻿—convenient for unloading, but a foolish nuisance when it came time to turn it and take off again, Chandler’s mind thought while his body lugged cartons out of the plane.

But he knew the answer to that. Takeoff would be no problem, any more than it would for the other small transports at the far end of the strip.

These planes were not going to return, ever.

===Chinese===

飞机停得翼尖几乎贴着楼，机头正对楼身——卸货是方便了，可到时候要掉头再起飞就麻烦得蠢笨——钱德勒的脑子这么想着，他的身体正把一箱箱货从飞机里往外拖。

可他知道答案。起飞不成问题——跑道远端那些小运输机也一样不成问题。

这些飞机永远不打算回去了。

===Original===

---

The work went on, and then it was done, or all but, and Chandler knew no more about it than when it was begun. The last little bit was a careful check of line voltages and a balancing of biases. Chandler could help only up to a point, and then two execs, working through the bodies of one of the Hawaiians and the pilot of a Piper Tri-Pacer who had flown in some last-minute test equipment﻿—and remained as part of the labor pool﻿—laboriously worked on the final tests.

Spent, the other men flopped to the ground, waiting.

They were far gone. All of them, Chandler as much as the others. But one of them rolled over, grinned tightly at Chandler and said, “It’s been fun. My name’s Bradley. I always think people ought to know each other’s names in cases like this. Imagine sharing a grave with some utter stranger!”

“Grave?”

Bradley nodded. “Like Pharaoh’s slaves. The pyramid is just about finished, friend. You don’t know what I’m talking about?” He sat up, plucked a blade of stemmy grass and put it between his teeth. “I guess you haven’t seen the corpses in the woods.”

===Chinese===

---

工程继续，然后就完了，或者说差不多完了，而钱德勒对它的了解，与开工时相比毫无长进。最后一小段是仔细核对线路电压和平衡偏置。钱德勒只能帮到某一步，然后由两名执政者——借一个夏威夷人和一名轻型飞机飞行员的身体干活，那人开一架派珀Tri-Pacer（Piper Tri-Pacer）送来了最后一批测试设备，也就留了下来充作劳力——吃力地做完了最终测试。

其他人精疲力竭，瘫倒在地，等着。

他们已经垮得很深了。全都一样，钱德勒不比别人轻。可有一个人翻过身来，冲钱德勒绷着脸咧嘴一笑，说：“这一路挺开心。我叫布拉德利（Bradley）。我一向认为，这种场合下大家应该互通姓名。想想看，跟一个彻头彻尾的陌生人合葬一座坟！”

“坟？”

布拉德利点点头。“就像法老的奴隶。金字塔眼看就要完工了，朋友。你不知道我在说什么？”他坐起来，拔了一根硬茎草叼在牙缝里。“我猜你还没见过林子里的那些尸体。”

===Original===

Chandler said, “I found a town half a mile or so over there, nothing in it but skeletons.”

“No, heavens, nothing that ancient. These are nice fresh corpses, out behind the junkheap there. Well, not *fresh*. They’re a couple of weeks old. I thought it was neat of the execs to dispose of the used-up labor out of sight of the rest of us. So much better for morale﻿ ﻿… until Juan Simoa and I went back looking for a plain, simple electrical extension cord and found them.”

With icy calm Chandler realized that the man was talking sense. Used-up labor: the men who had unloaded the first planes, no doubt﻿—worked until they dropped, then efficiently disposed of, as they were so cheap a commodity that they were not worth the trouble of hauling back to Honolulu for salvage. “I see,” he said. “Besides, dead men tell no tales.”

“*And* spread no disease. Probably that’s why they did their killing back in the tall trees. Always the chance some exec might have to come down here to inspect in person. Rotting corpses just aren’t sanitary.” Bradley grinned again. “I used to be a doctor at Molokai.”

===Chinese===

钱德勒说：“我在那边半英里外找到过一座镇子，里头除了骷髅什么都没有。”

“不，天哪，没那么老掉牙的。这些可是新鲜体面的尸体，就扔在那堆废品后头。呃，也算不上*新鲜*。摆了有两个礼拜了。我还夸执政者们做事漂亮呢，把用尽的劳力处理到我们其余人看不见的地方。对士气好得多嘛……直到我和胡安·西莫亚（Juan Simoa）回去找一根普普通通的电源延长线，撞见了他们。”

钱德勒以冰冷的镇定意识到，这人说的句句是实情。用尽的劳力：最早卸那几架飞机的人们，毫无疑问——干到倒下为止，随即被高效地处理掉，因为他们这种商品贱得很，不值得费事运回檀香山回收利用。“我明白了，”他说，“再说，死人还不会走漏风声。”

“*而且*还不传播疾病。八成就是为了这个，他们把杀人挪到密林深处去干。总归有可能哪位执政者得亲自下来视察。腐烂的尸体实在不卫生。”布拉德利又咧嘴一笑。“我从前在莫洛凯岛（Molokai）当医生。”

===Original===

“Lep﻿—” began Chandler, but the doctor shook his head.

“No, no, never say ‘leprosy.’ It’s ‘Hansen’s disease.’ Whatever it is, the execs were sure scared of it. They wiped out every patient we had, except a couple who got away by swimming; then for good measure they wiped out most of the medical staff too, except for a couple like me who were off-island and had the sense to keep quiet about where they’d worked. I used,” he said, rolling over his back and putting his hands behind his head, “in the old days to work on pest-control for the Public Health Service. We sure knocked off a lot of rats and fleas. I never thought I’d be one of them.” He was silent.

Chandler admired his courage very much. The man had fallen asleep.

Chandler looked at the others. “You going to let them kill us without a struggle?” he demanded.

The remaining Hawaiian was the only one to answer. He said, “You just don’t know how much *pilikia* you’re in. It isn’t what we *let* them do.”

“We’ll see,” Chandler promised grimly. “They’re only human. I haven’t given up yet.”

===Chinese===

“麻——”钱德勒刚开口，医生却摇了摇头。

“不，不，永远别说‘麻风病’。要说‘汉森氏病’。管它是什么呢，执政者们肯定是怕它的。他们把我们那儿的病人杀了个精光，只有两个泅水逃掉了；接着为保险起见，又把医务人员也灭了差不多，只留下几个像我这样当时不在岛上、又有脑子绝口不提自己在哪儿干过的。从前，”他说着翻过身仰面躺下，双手垫在脑后，“我给公共卫生署干的是害虫防治。我们确实消灭过好多老鼠和跳蚤。我从没想过自己会变成它们中的一员。”他沉默了。

钱德勒非常佩服他的勇气。这人已经睡着了。

钱德勒看向其他人。“你们就这么任凭他们宰杀，一点不挣扎吗？”他质问。

回答的只有剩下的那个夏威夷人。他说：“你根本不知道你陷在多大的*麻烦*（pilikia）里。这不是我们*让*他们怎么做的问题。”

“走着瞧，”钱德勒阴沉地许诺，“他们终究只是人。我还没放弃。”

===Original===

---

But in the end he could not save himself; it was the girl who saved him. That night Chandler tossed in troubled sleep, and woke to find himself standing, walking toward the Tri-Pacer. The sun was just beginning to pink the sky and no one else was moving. “Sorry, love,” he apologized to himself. “You probably need to bathe and shave, but I don’t know how. Shave, I mean.” He giggled. “Anyway, you’ll find everything you need at my house.”

He climbed into the plane. “Ever fly before?” he asked himself. “Well, you’ll love it. Here we go. *Close* the door﻿ ﻿… *snap* the belt﻿ ﻿… *turn* the switch.” He admired the practiced ease with which his body started the motor, raced it with a critical eye on the instruments, turned the plane and lifted it off, up, into the rising sun.

“Oh, dear. You *do* need a bath,” he told himself, wrinkling his nose humorously. “No harm. I’ve the nicest tub﻿—pink, deep﻿—and nine kinds of bath salts. But I wish you weren’t so tired, love, because it’s a long flight and you’re wearing me out.” He was silent as he bent to the correct compass heading and cranked a handle over his head to adjust the trim. “Koitska’s going to be so *huhu*,” he said, smiling. “Never fear, love, I can calm him down. But it’s easier to do with you in one piece, you know, the other way’s too late.”

He was silent for a long time, and then his voice began to sing.

They were songs from Rosalie’s own musical comedies. Even with so poor an instrument as Chandler’s voice to work with, she sang well enough to keep both of them entertained while his body brought the plane in for a landing; and so Chandler went to live in the villa that belonged to Rosalie Pan.

===Chinese===

---

可到头来他救不了自己；救他的是那姑娘。那天夜里钱德勒在不安的睡眠中翻来覆去，醒来时发现自己正站着，正朝那架Tri-Pacer走去。太阳刚开始把天边染出粉红，再没有别人在动。“对不起，亲爱的，”他对自己赔不是，“你多半需要洗个澡、刮个脸，可我不知道怎么弄。是刮脸，我是说。”他咯咯笑了。“反正，你缺的东西我家里都有。”

他爬进飞机。“以前飞过吗？”他问自己，“没事儿，你会爱上的。咱们走。*关*舱门……*扣*安全带……*拧*点火开关。”他欣赏着自己这具身体那副娴熟利落的做派：起动马达，目光挑剔地扫着仪表试了试转速，掉转机头，把飞机抬离地面，向上，迎着初升的太阳。

“哎呀，天哪。你*实在*该洗个澡了。”他对自己说，诙谐地皱了皱鼻子，“不碍事。我家有只顶棒的浴缸——粉红色，深深的——还有九种浴盐。可我多希望你没那么累呀，亲爱的，这一路远着呢，你都快把我拖垮了。”他俯身校准罗盘航向、摇着头顶的手柄调整配平时，安静了一会儿。“科伊茨卡准会气得*冒烟*（huhu）。”他笑吟吟地说，“别怕，亲爱的，我能把他哄住。不过这事儿你囫囵着身子办起来容易些，你明白，走了另一条路可就来不及啦。”

他沉默了很久很久，然后他的嗓音开始唱歌。

唱的是罗莎莉自己音乐喜剧里的歌。尽管手头只有钱德勒这副不怎么样的嗓子当乐器，她也唱得足以让机身里的两个人一路不闷，直到他的身体把飞机落了地；就这样，钱德勒搬进了罗莎莉·潘名下的那座别墅。

## XII / 十二

===Original===

“Love,” she said, “there are worse things in the world than keeping me amused when I’m not busy. We’ll go to the beach again one day soon, I promise.” And she was gone again.

Chandler was a concubine﻿—not even that; he was a male geisha, convenient to play gin rummy with, or for company on the surfboards, or to make a drink.

He did not quite know what to make of himself. In bad times one hopes for survival. He had hoped; and now he had survival, perfumed and cushioned, but on what mad terms! Rosalie was a pretty girl, and a good-humored one. She was right. There were worse things in the world than being her companion; but Chandler could not adjust himself to the role.

===Chinese===

“亲爱的，”她说，“世上比我闲下来时逗我开心更糟的事，多的是。过不了几天咱们再去海滩，我保证。”说完她又走了。

钱德勒是个面首——甚至连面首都算不上；他是个男艺伎，陪她打打金拉米牌，陪她踩踩冲浪板，或者调一杯酒，图个方便。

他有点不知道该拿自己怎么办。时世艰危，人指望的无非是活下来。他指望过；如今他活下来了，香喷喷、软垫垫的，可这是何等疯狂的代价！罗莎莉是个漂亮姑娘，性子也随和。她说得对。世上确有比做她的陪伴更糟的境遇；可钱德勒就是无法让自己安于这个角色。

===Original===

It angered him when she got up from the garden swing and locked herself in her room﻿—for he knew that she was not sleeping as she lay there, though her eyes were closed and she was motionless. It infuriated him when she casually usurped his body to bring an ashtray to her side, or to stop him when his hands presumed. And it drove him nearly wild to be a puppet with her friends working his strings.

He was that most of all. One exec who wished to communicate with another cast about for an available human proxy nearby. Chandler was that for Rosalie Pan: her telephone, her social secretary, and on occasion he was the garment her dates put on. For Rosalie was one of the few execs who cared to conduct any major part of her life in her own skin. She liked dancing. She enjoyed dining out. It was her pleasure to display herself to the worshippers at Luigi the Wharf Rat’s and to speed down the long combers on a surfboard. When another exec chose to accompany her it was Chandler’s body which gave the remote “date” flesh.

He ate very well indeed﻿—in surprising variety. He drank heavily sometimes and abstained others. Once, in the person of a Moroccan exec, he smoked an opium pipe; once he dined on roasted puppy. He saw many interesting things and, when Rosalie was occupied without him, he had the run of her house, her music library, her pantry and her books. He was not mistreated. He was pampered and praised, and every night she kissed him before she retired to her own room with the snap-lock on the door.

He was miserable.

===Chinese===

她从花园秋千上起身，把自己锁进房里，这让他恼火——因为他知道，她躺在那里并不是睡觉，尽管她闭着眼，一动不动。她随随便便就征用他的身体，替她把烟灰缸端到手边，或在他的双手放肆时叫停，这让他火冒三丈。而做一个木偶，任她的朋友们牵动他的提线，这几乎把他逼疯。

说到底，他多半就是这个用处。一个执政者想跟另一个通话，就在近旁物色一个可用的人身代任。钱德勒对罗莎莉·潘就是这么一号：她的电话，她的社交秘书，偶尔还是她那些约会对象披上的衣裳。因为罗莎莉是少数几个还愿意以自己的本来皮囊过活大部分日子的执政者。她喜欢跳舞。她喜欢下馆子。在码头耗子卢伊吉（Luigi the Wharf Rat）家向崇拜者们亮相，踩着冲浪板顺着长长的卷浪疾冲而下，都是她的乐事。而当另一位执政者想要陪伴她时，便是钱德勒的身体给那位远端的“约会对象”赋予血肉。

他吃得确实很好——花样多得出奇。他有时纵酒，有时滴酒不沾。有一回，他借一个摩洛哥执政者的身体抽了一筒鸦片；有一回，他吃了一餐烤幼犬。他见识了许多有趣的东西；罗莎莉顾不上他的时候，她的房子、她的音乐藏书室、她的食品储藏间和她的书，都任他进出。他没有受虐待。他被娇惯着、夸赞着，每天夜里，她都在回自己那间门上装着弹簧锁的房间之前，吻他一下。

他苦不堪言。

===Original===

He prowled the house in the nights after she had left him, unable to sleep. It had been bad enough on Hilo, under the hanging threat of death. But then, though he was only a slave, he was working at something that used his skill and training.

Now? Now a Pekingese could do nearly all she wanted of him. He despised in himself the knowledge that with a Pekingese’s cunning he was contriving to make himself indispensable to her﻿—her slippers fetched in his teeth, his silky mane by her hand to stroke﻿—if not these things in actuality, then their very near equivalents.

But what else was there for him?

===Chinese===

她丢下他之后，他在夜里绕着房子踱来踱去，睡不着。在希洛（Hilo）那时就已经够糟了，头顶悬着死亡的威胁。可在那时，他虽然只是个奴隶，干的却是用得上他的技术和训练的活计。

现在呢？现在一只京巴犬就差不多能办到她要他做的一切。用牙齿叼来她的拖鞋，把丝缎般的长毛送到她手边任她抚弄——他心里明白，自己正以京巴犬式的狡黠，设法让她离不开自己；就算实际上不是这些事，也相差无几——他为自己知道这一点而鄙夷自己。

可他还有什么别的路吗？

===Original===

There was nothing. She had spared his life from Koitska, and if he offended her, Koitska’s sentence would be carried out.

Even dying might be better than this, he thought.

Indeed, it might be better even to go back to Honolulu and life.

===Chinese===

没有。她从科伊茨卡手里救下他一条命；他若是触恼了她，科伊茨卡的判决就会执行。

他想，就连死掉，也许都比这样强。

的确，哪怕是回到檀香山、回到人间烟火里去，说不定都比这样强。

===Original===

---

In the morning he woke to find himself climbing the wide, carpeted steps to her room. She was not asleep; it was her mind that was guiding him.

He opened the door. She lay with a feathery coverlet pulled up to her chin, eyes open, head propped on three pillows; as she looked at him he was free. “Something the matter, love? You fell asleep sitting up.”

“Sorry.” She would not be put off. She made him tell her his resentments. She was very understanding and very sure as she said, “You’re not a dog, love. I won’t have you thinking that way. You’re my friend. Don’t you think I need a friend?” She leaned forward. Her nightgown was very sheer; but Chandler had tasted that trap before and he averted his eyes. “You think it’s all fun for us. I understand. Tell me, if you thought I was doing important work﻿—oh, *crucial* work, love﻿—would you feel a little easier? Because I am. We’ve got the whole work of the island to do, and I do my share. We’ve got our plans to make and our future to provide for. There are so few of us. A single H-bomb could kill us all. Do you think it isn’t work, keeping that bomb from ever coming here? There’s all Honolulu to monitor, for they know about us there. We can’t like some disgusting nitwits like your Society of Slaves destroy *us*. There’s the problems of the world to see to. Why,” she said with pride, “we’ve solved the whole Indian-Pakistani population problem in the last two months. They’ll not have to worry about famine again for a dozen generations! We’re working on China now; next Japan; next﻿—oh, all the world. We’ll have three-quarters of the lumps gone soon, and the rest will have space to breathe in. It’s work!”

===Chinese===

---

早晨他醒来，发现自己正沿着铺着地毯的宽阔台阶往她房间去。她没有睡着；是她的意念在引导他。

他推开门。她躺着，羽绒被一直拉到下巴，眼睛睁着，头垫在三个枕头上；她一望向他，他就自由了。“怎么啦，亲爱的？你坐着坐着就睡着了。”

“对不起。”她不肯就此作罢。她逼着他说出满腹的怨气。她非常善解人意，也非常笃定地说：“你不是狗，亲爱的。我不许你那样想。你是我的朋友。你以为我就不需要朋友吗？”她向前俯身。她的睡裙薄如蝉翼；可钱德勒上过那个圈套的当，把眼睛避开了。“你以为我们过的全是好日子。我明白。告诉我，要是你知道我干的是重要的工作——哦，*性命攸关*的工作，亲爱的——你会不会好受一点？因为我干的就是。整个岛子的工作都担在我们身上，我担着我的一份。我们要制定计划，要为将来做打算。我们人数这么少。一颗氢弹就能把咱们全都报销。你以为让那颗氢弹永远到不了这里，就不算工作？整个檀香山都得监听着，因为他们知道我们的存在。咱们可不能让你们那个奴隶协会一类的讨厌蠢货把*咱们*给毁了。世界上种种问题都得有人照管。哎呀，”她自豪地说，“就在过去两个月里，我们把整个印度-巴基斯坦的人口问题解决了。往后十几代人都用不着再担心饥荒！我们现在正在处理中国；接下来是日本；再接下来——哦，全世界。用不了多久，我们就能让四分之三的赘瘤消失，剩下的也有地方喘气。这是工作！”

===Original===

She saw his expression and said earnestly, “No, don’t think that! You call it murder. It is, of course. But it’s the surgeon’s knife. We’re quicker and less painful than starvation, love﻿ … and if some of us enjoy the work of weeding out the unfit, does that change anything? It does not! I admit some of us are, well, *mean*. But not all. And we’re improving. The new people we take in are better than the old.”

She looked at him thoughtfully for a moment.

Then she shook her head. “Never mind,” she said﻿—apparently to herself. “Forget it, love. Go like an angel and fetch us both some coffee.”

===Chinese===

她看见他的神情，恳切地说：“不，别那么想！你管它叫谋杀。它当然是谋杀。可它是手术刀。我们比饿死快当，也比饿死少受罪，亲爱的……要是我们当中有人以淘汰不适者为乐，那又能改变什么？改变不了！我承认我们有些人，怎么说呢，*刻毒*。但不是全部。而且我们在进步。新吸收进来的人比老家伙们强。”

她若有所思地看了他片刻。

随后她摇了摇头。“算了，”她说——像是说给自己听的。“忘了它吧，亲爱的。乖乖的，像个天使似的，去给咱们俩弄点咖啡来。”

===Original===

---

Like an angel he went﻿ … not, he thought bitterly, like a man.

She was keeping something from him, and he was too stubborn to let her tease him out of his mood. “Everything’s a secret,” he complained, and she patted his cheek.

“It has to be that way.” She was quite serious. “This is the biggest thing in the world. I’m fond of you, love, but I can’t let that interfere with my duty.”

“*Shto, Rosie?*” said Chandler’s mouth thickly.

===Chinese===

---

他像个天使似的去了……而不是，他苦涩地想，像个人。

她有事瞒着他，而他又倔得很，不肯让她把自己哄出这股情绪。“什么都神神秘秘的。”他抱怨道，她拍了拍他的脸颊。

“只能这样。”她十分郑重。“这是天底下头一号的大事。我喜欢你，亲爱的，可我不能让这件事妨碍我的职责。”

“*Shto, Rosie?*”钱德勒的嘴含混地说。

===Original===

“Oh, there you are, Andrei,” she said, and spoke quickly in Russian.

Chandler’s brows knotted in a scowl and he barked: “*Nyeh mozhet bit!*”

“Andrei.﻿ …” she said gently. “*Ya vas sprashnivayoo.﻿ …*”

“*Nyet!*”

“*No Andrei.﻿ …*”

Rumble, grumble; Chandler’s body twitched and fumed. He heard his own name in the argument, but what the subject matter was he could not tell. Rosalie was coaxing; Koitska was refusing. But he was weakening. After minutes Chandler’s shoulders shrugged; he nodded; and he was free.

“Have some more coffee, love,” said Rosalie Pan with an air of triumph.

===Chinese===

“哦，你来了，安德烈（Andrei）。”她说，随即飞快地说起俄语来。

钱德勒的眉毛拧成一个疙瘩，吼道：“*Nyeh mozhet bit!*”

“安德烈。……”她温柔地说。“*Ya vas sprashnivayoo。……*”

“*Nyet!*”

“*No Andrei。……*”

咕哝声、牢骚声；钱德勒的身体抽动着、冒着火气。他在争吵里听见了自己的名字，可吵的是什么，他分辨不出。罗莎莉在哄；科伊茨卡在拒绝。可他松了口。几分钟后，钱德勒的肩膀耸了耸；他点了点头；随后他自由了。

“再来点咖啡，亲爱的。”罗莎莉·潘带着得胜的神气说。

===Original===

Chandler waited. He did not understand what was going on. It was up to her to enlighten him, and finally she smiled and said: “Perhaps you can join us, love. Don’t say yes or no. It isn’t up to you﻿ … and besides you can’t know whether you want it or not until you try. So be patient a moment.”

Chandler frowned; then felt his body taken. His lips barked: “*Khorashaw!*” His body got up and walked to the wall of Rosalie’s room. A picture on the wall moved aside and there was a safe. Flick, flick, Chandler’s own fingers dialed a combination so rapidly that he could not follow it. The door of the safe opened.

And Chandler was free, and Rosalie excitedly leaping out of the bed behind him, careless of the wisp of nylon that was her only garment, crowding softly, warmly past him to reach inside the safe. She lifted out a coronet very like her own.

She paused and looked at Chandler.

===Chinese===

钱德勒等着。他不明白这是怎么了。该由她来点醒他，而她终于笑着开口：“也许你能加入我们，亲爱的。先别答应，也别拒绝。这事轮不到你说了算……再说，不试一试，你自己也谈不上想要还是不想要。所以就耐心等一小会儿。”

钱德勒皱起眉头；随即感到身体被接管了。他的嘴唇吼出一个词：“*Khorashaw!*”他的身体起身，走向罗莎莉房间的墙边。墙上的一幅画移开，露出一个保险柜。咔嗒，咔嗒，钱德勒自己的手指拨起号码盘，快得他根本跟不上。保险柜的门开了。

钱德勒又自由了，罗莎莉兴奋地从床上跃到他身后，毫不在乎身上那缕尼龙是她唯一的衣物，柔软而温热地从他身边挤过去，探进保险柜。她捧出一顶和她自己那顶很像的头冠。

她停住，望着钱德勒。

===Original===

“You can’t do anything to harm us with this one, love,” she warned. “Do you understand that? I mean, don’t get the idea that you can tell anyone anything. Or do something violent. You can’t. I’ll be right with you, and Koitska will be monitoring the transmitter.” She handed him the coronet. “Now, when you see something interesting, you move right in. You’ll see how. It’s the easiest thing in the world, and﻿—Oh, here. Put it on.”

Chandler swallowed with difficulty.

She was offering him the tool that had given the execs the world. A blunter, weaker tool than her own, no doubt. But still it was power beyond his imagining. He stood there frozen as she slipped it on his head. Sprung electrodes pressed gently against his temples and behind his ears. She touched something.﻿ …

Chandler stood motionless for a moment and then, without effort, floated free of his own body.

===Chinese===

“拿着这东西，你伤害不了我们任何一根毫毛，亲爱的。”她提醒道。“你明白吗？我是说，别打主意以为能告诉谁点什么，或是干什么暴力的事。你做不到。我会时刻跟在你身边，科伊茨卡会监听着发射机。”她把头冠递给他。“好了，见到什么有意思的，你就径直进去。你会知道怎么做的。这是天底下最容易的事，而且——哦，来。戴上。”

钱德勒艰难地咽了口唾沫。

她递给他的，是那件曾把整个世界交到执政者们手里的工具。比起她自己那顶，无疑更钝、更弱。可它仍是超出他想象的力量。她把头冠扣上他头顶时，他僵立在那里。弹性电极轻轻抵住他两侧太阳穴和耳后。她碰了碰什么。……

钱德勒木立了片刻，随即毫无费力地脱出自己的身体，漂浮了起来。

===Original===

---

Floating. Floating; a jellyfish floating. Trailing tentacles that whipped and curled, floating over the sandbound claws and chitin that clashed beneath, floating over the world’s people, and them not even knowing, not even seeing.﻿ …

Chandler floated.

He was up, out and away. He was drifting. Around him was no-color. He saw nothing of space or size, he only saw, or did not see but felt-smelled-tasted, people. They were the sandbound. They were the creatures that crawled and struggled below, and his tentacles lashed out at them.

===Chinese===

---

漂浮。漂浮；一只水母般漂浮着。拖着抽打、卷曲的触手，漂在下方咔咔开合的缚沙的螯钳与甲壳之上，漂在世界众生之上，而他们浑然不知，浑然不见。……

钱德勒漂浮着。

他升起来，出去，远去。他在飘荡。四周是一种无色。他看不见空间，也看不见大小，他只是看见——或者并非看见，而是以感觉-嗅觉-味觉——感知到人。他们便是缚沙之人。他们就是下方那些爬行挣扎的生灵，而他的触手朝他们甩去。

===Original===

Beside him floated another. The girl? It had a shape, but not a human shape﻿—a pair of great projecting spheres, a cinctured area-rule shape. Female. Yes, undoubtedly the girl. It waved a member at him and he understood he was beckoned. He followed.

Two of sandbound ones were ahead.

The female shape slipped into one, he into the other. It was as easy to invest this form with his own will as it was to command the muscles of his hand. They looked at each other out of sandbound eyes. “You’re a boy!” Chandler laughed. The girl laughed: “You’re an old washerwoman!” They were in a kitchen where fish simmered on an electric stove. The boy-Rosie wrinkled his-her nose, blinked and was empty. Only the small almond-eyed boy was left, and he began to cry convulsively. Chandler understood. He floated out after her.

===Chinese===

他身旁漂着另一个。是那姑娘？它有个形状，却不是人的形状——一对凸出的大球，一副腰身收束的“面积律”形状。女性的。是的，毫无疑问是那姑娘。它朝他挥动一条肢体，他明白自己被召唤了。他跟了上去。

前方有两个缚沙之人。

那女性的形状滑进其中一个，他滑进另一个。把自己的意志灌注进这具形体，就跟指挥自己手上的肌肉一样容易。他们从缚沙者的眼睛里对望。“你是个男孩！”钱德勒笑了。姑娘笑了：“你是个洗衣老婆子！”他们在一间厨房里，电炉上炖着鱼。罗西借的那个男孩皱了皱她的鼻子，眨了眨眼，随即空了。只剩下那个小小的杏仁眼男孩，开始抽抽噎噎地哭。钱德勒明白了。他跟着她漂了出去。

===Original===

This way, this way, she gestured. A crowd of mudbound figures. She slipped into one, he into another. They were in a bus now, rocking along an inland road, all men, all roughly dressed. Laborers going to clear a new section of Oahu of its split-level debris, Chandler thought, and looked for the girl in one of the men’s eyes, could not find her, hesitated and﻿—floated. She was hovering impatiently. This way!

He followed, and followed.

They were a hundred people doing a hundred things. They lingered a few moments as a teenage couple holding hands in the twilight of the beach. They fled from a room where Chandler was an old woman dying on a bed, and Rosalie a stolid, uncaring nurse beside her. They played follow-the-leader through the audience of a Honolulu movie theater, and sought each other, laughing, among the fish stalls of King Street. Then Chandler turned to Rosalie to speak and﻿… it all went out﻿… the scene disappeared﻿… he opened his eyes, and he was back in his own flesh.

===Chinese===

这边，这边，她示意。一群泥缚的人影。她滑进一个，他滑进另一个。此刻他们在一辆公共汽车里，车身摇晃着行驶在一条通往岛内的路上，满车都是男人，个个衣着粗陋。是要去清除瓦胡岛新划地段的错层式废墟的劳工吧，钱德勒想，于是在一个男人的眼睛里寻找那姑娘，找不到，迟疑了一下，随即——漂浮起来。她正不耐烦地悬在半空。这边！

他跟着，又跟着。

他们做过一百个人，做着一百件事。他们当过一对在海滩暮色里牵手的少年情侣，流连片刻。他们逃离过一个房间——在那里，钱德勒是个躺在床上垂死的老妪，罗莎莉是床边一个木然无觉的护士。他们在檀香山一家电影院的观众席间玩“跟着领队走”，又在国王街（King Street）的鱼摊间笑着互相寻找。随后钱德勒转向罗莎莉要说话，于是……一切都熄了……场景消散了……他睁开眼睛，回到了自己的肉身里。

===Original===

He was lying on the pastel pile rug in Rosalie’s bedroom.

He got up, rubbing the side of his face. He had tumbled, it seemed. Rosalie was lying on the bed.

In a moment she opened her eyes.

“Well, love?”

He said hoarsely, “What made it stop?”

She shrugged. “Koitska turned you off. Tired of monitoring us, I expect﻿—it’s been an hour. I’m surprised his patience lasted this long.”

She stretched luxuriously, but he was too full of what had happened even to see the white grace of her body. “Did you like it, love? Would you like to have it forever?”

===Chinese===

他躺在罗莎莉卧室的彩色长绒地毯上。

他爬起来，揉着脸侧。看来他是栽倒过的。罗莎莉躺在床上。

过了一会儿，她睁开眼睛。

“怎么样，亲爱的？”

他声音沙哑地问：“是什么让它停下的？”

她耸耸肩。“科伊茨卡把你关掉了。监听咱们监听腻了，我猜——已经一个钟头了。他的耐心居然撑了这么久，我都奇怪。”

她惬意至极地伸展身体，可他满心都是方才发生的事，连她身体的白皙优雅都视而不见。“喜欢吗，亲爱的？想不想永远拥有它？”

## XIII / 十三

===Original===

For nine days Chandler’s status remained in limbo. He spent that day in a state of numb bemusement, remembering the men and women he had worn like garments, appalled and exhilarated. He did not see Rosalie again that day, she kept to her room and he locked out. He was still a lapdog, but a lapdog with a dream dangling before him. He went to sleep that night thinking that he was a dog who might become a god, and he had eight days left.

The next day Rosalie wheedled another hour of the coronet from Koitska. They explored the ice caves on Mount Rainier in the bodies of two sick, starving hermits and wandered arm in arm near the destroyed International Bridge at Niagara, breathing the spray of the unchanging Falls. He had seven days left.

They passed like a dream. He saw a great deal of the inner workings of the exec, more than before. He had privileges. He was up for membership in the club. Rosalie had proposed him. He talked with two Czechoslovakian ballet dancers in their persons, and a succession of heavily accented Russians and Poles and Japanese through the mouth of the beach boy who came to tend Rosalie’s garden. He thought they liked him and was pleased that he penetrated where he had not been allowed before﻿ … until he realized that these freedoms were in themselves a threat. They allowed him this contact so that they could look him over. If they rejected him they would have to kill him, because he had seen too much. But by then a week had passed, and another day, and though he did not know it he had only one day left. Rosalie did what she could to make the days of waiting easy for him.

===Chinese===

此后九天，钱德勒的身份一直悬而未决。事发当天，他是在一种麻木的恍惚中度过的，回味着那些被他像衣裳一样穿在身上的男男女女，既惊骇又亢奋。那天他没有再见到罗莎莉：她守在自己房里，把他锁在了门外。他仍然是一条哈巴狗——只是这条哈巴狗眼前吊着一个梦。那天夜里他入睡时想着：自己是一条说不定能成神的狗，而他还剩下八天。

第二天，罗莎莉又从科伊茨卡那里为他磨来一个钟头的头冠。他们借两具病饿交迫的隐士的躯体，探了雷尼尔山（Mount Rainier）的冰洞，又在尼亚加拉那座被毁的国际大桥附近挽臂徜徉，呼吸着丝毫不曾改变的瀑布的水雾。他还剩下七天。

日子像梦一样过去了。他大大见识了一番执政者集团的内部运作，比以往见到的多。他有了种种特权。他的入会申请正在俱乐部里流转。罗莎莉是他的提名人。他借两位捷克斯洛伐克芭蕾舞演员本人的身体同他们交谈，还通过那个来打理罗莎莉花园的沙滩仔的嘴，同一连串口音浓重的俄国人、波兰人和日本人说话。他以为他们喜欢他，也为打进了从前不得其门而入的圈子而得意……直到他醒悟过来：这些自由本身就是一种威胁。他们容许他这些接触，为的是把他看个通透。若是拒绝他入会，他们就得杀他灭口，因为他见得太多了。可到那时，一个星期已经过去，又过了一天，虽然他自己并不知道，他只剩一天了。罗莎莉尽了她的所能，让他等待的日子好过一些。

===Original===

“Embarrassing, isn’t it? I went through it myself, love. Come have a drink.”

“When will I know?” he demanded fretfully.

“Well.” She hesitated. “I don’t suppose there’s any harm in telling you, love, under the circumstances﻿—”

He knew what the circumstances were.

“I guess I can tell you. You need just over seven hundred votes to come in. You’ve got﻿—” Her eyes glazed for a moment. She was looking through some clerk’s eyes, somewhere on the island. “You’ve got about a hundred and fifty so far. Takes time, doesn’t it? But it’s worth it in the end.”

“How many ‘no’ votes?”

“None.” She said gently, “You’ll never have but one, love, because that’s all it takes.”

He stared. The girl gook took up his hand and kissed it lightly. “One blackball’s enough, yes, but never fear. Rosie’s on your side.”

===Chinese===

“难为情，是不是？我也是这么熬过来的，亲爱的。来，喝一杯。”

“我什么时候能知道结果？”他焦躁地追问。

“这个嘛。”她迟疑了一下。“考虑到眼下这情形，告诉你想必也没什么害处，亲爱的——”

他知道这情形指的是什么。

“我想我可以告诉你。你还需要七百多票才能入会。你已经拿到了——”她的目光怔了一瞬。她正借岛上某个文书员的眼睛看着什么。“你已经拿到了大约一百五十票。得花时间，对吧？可到头来是值得的。”

“‘反对’票有几张？”

“一张也没有。”她温和地说，“你永远也不会摊上一张以上的，亲爱的，因为一张就够了。”

他怔怔地望着。那东方小妞捧起他的手，轻轻吻了一下。“一颗黑球就够了，是这样，可别怕。罗西站在你这边。”

===Original===

---

Restlessly Chandler stood up and made himself another drink. His head was beginning to buzz. They had been drinking on her sun terrace since early afternoon.

Rosalie came up beside him soothingly. “I know how you feel. Want me to tell you about when I went through it?”

“Sure,” he said, stirring the ice around in the glass and drinking it down. He made another drink absently, hardly hearing what she said, although the sound of her voice was welcome.

“Oh, that lousy headdress! It weighed twenty pounds, and they put it on with hatpins.” He caressed her absently. He had figured out that she was talking about the night New York was bombed. “I was in the middle of the big first-act curtain number when﻿—” her face was strained, even after years, even now that she was herself one of the godlike ones﻿—“when something took hold of me. I ran off the stage and right out through the front door. There was a cab waiting. As soon as I got in I was free, and the driver took off like a lunatic through the tunnel, out to Newark Airport. I tell you, I was scared! At the toll booth I screamed but my﻿—friend﻿—let go of the driver for a minute, smashed a trailer-truck into a police car, and in the confusion we got away. He took me over again at the airport. I ran bare as a bird into a plane that was just ready to take off. The pilot was under control.﻿ … We flew eleven hours, and I wore that damn feather headdress all the way.”

===Chinese===

---

钱德勒烦躁地站起身，又给自己调了一杯酒。他的脑袋开始嗡嗡作响。从午后不久起，他们就一直在她的日光露台上喝酒。

罗莎莉温柔地凑到他身边。“我明白你的滋味。想不想听听我当年是怎么熬过来的？”

“想听。”他说着，搅动杯中的冰块，一饮而尽。他又心不在焉地调了一杯，几乎没有听进她说的话，尽管她的嗓音听着受用。

“哦，那顶破头饰！足有二十磅重，他们还用帽针把它别在我头上。”他心不在焉地抚弄着她。他早已想明白，她说的是纽约挨炸的那一夜。“我正演到第一幕落幕的大场面，忽然——”哪怕事隔多年，哪怕如今她自己已位列诸神，她的脸仍然绷得紧紧的——“忽然有什么东西攥住了我。我冲下舞台，径直冲出了正门。门口正好有一辆出租车在等着。我一钻进去就自由了，司机疯子似的开车钻进隧道，直奔纽瓦克机场。我跟你说，我真吓坏了！过收费站时我尖叫起来，可我那位——朋友——把司机撒开了一分钟，把一辆拖挂车砸进一辆警车，乱糟糟之中我们脱了身。到了机场，他重新接管了我。我像只鸟儿一样一丝不挂，跑进一架正要起飞的飞机。飞行员是被控制着的。……我们飞了十一个钟头，那顶该死的羽毛头饰，我一路戴到了底。”

===Original===

She held out her glass for a refill. Chandler busied himself slicing a lime for her drink. Now she was talking about her friend. “I hadn’t seen him in six years. I was just a kid, living in Islip. He was with a Russian trade commission next door, in an old mansion. Well, he was one of the ones, back in Russia, that came up with these.” She touched her coronet. “So,” she said brightly, “he put me up for membership and by and by they gave me one. You see? It’s all very simple, except the waiting.”

Chandler pulled her down on the couch beside him and made a toast. “Your friend.”

“He’s a nice guy,” she said moodily, sipping her drink. “You know how careful I am about getting exercise and so on? It’s partly because of him. You would have liked him, love, only﻿—well, it turned out that he liked me well enough, but he began to like what he could get through the coronet a lot more. He got fat. A lot of them are awfully fat, love,” she said seriously. “That’s why they need people like me. And you. Replacements. Heart trouble, liver trouble, what can they expect when they lie in bed day in and day out, taking their lives through other people’s bodies? I won’t let myself go that way.﻿ … It’s a temptation. You know, almost every day I find some poor woman on a diet and spend a solid hour eating creampuffs and gravies. How they must hate me!”

She grinned, leaned back and kissed him.

Chandler put his arms around the girl and returned the kiss, hard. She did not draw away. She clung to him, and he could feel in the warmth of her body, the sound of her breath that she was responding. The drink made him reckless; the last two weeks made him doubtful; he was torn. He could tell that there was no resistance in her body, but the coronet made it in doubt; she could fling him away from her with one touch of the mind. Yet she didn’t do it﻿—

“*Vi myenya zvali?*” his own voice demanded, harsh and mocking.

===Chinese===

她递过杯子让他添酒。钱德勒忙着为她的酒切一片青柠。此刻她说到了她那位朋友。“我已有六年没见过他了。那时我还是个孩子，住在艾斯利普（Islip）。他在隔壁，跟着一家俄国贸易代表团，住在一栋老宅子里。后来嘛，他就是当年在俄国鼓捣出这些东西的人中的一个。”她碰了碰自己的头冠。“于是呢，”她轻快地说，“他做了我的入会提名人，没过多久，他们便给了我一项。明白了吧？一切都简单得很，就是等候最难熬。”

钱德勒把她拉到身边沙发上坐下，举杯致意。“敬你的朋友。”

“他是个好人，”她怏怏地说，啜着酒。“你知道我为什么那么注意锻炼身体什么的吗？一半也是因为他。你会喜欢他的，亲爱的，只是——唉，后来呢，他喜欢我固然喜欢得够呛，可他开始更喜欢能从头冠里得到的东西了。他发胖了。他们当中好多人都胖得吓人，亲爱的，”她正色道，“所以他们才需要我这样的人。还有你。替补。心脏病、肝病——天天躺在床上、借别人的身体过活的人，还能指望什么呢？我不会让自己落到那步田地。……这可是个诱惑。你知道吗，几乎每一天我都能觅到哪个正在节食的可怜女人，然后足足花上一个钟头大吃奶油泡芙和浇满浓汁的菜肴。她们该有多恨我！”

她咧嘴一笑，向后一仰，吻了他。

钱德勒搂住姑娘，用力回吻。她没有躲开。她偎依着他，从她身体的暖意、她呼吸的声音里，他能觉出她在回应。酒让他忘乎所以；过去两个星期让他满腹狐疑；他被撕扯着。他觉出她的身体里没有半分抗拒，可头冠让一切都悬而未决；她只消心念一动，就能把他从身边甩出去。然而她没有那样做——

“*Vi myenya zvali?*”他自己的嗓音开了口，粗暴而带着讥诮。

===Original===

---

The girl tried to push him away. Her eyes were bright and huge, staring at him. “Andrei!”

“*Da, Andrei! Kok eto dosadno!*”

“Andrei, please. I know that you are﻿—”

“Filthy!” screamed Chandler’s voice. “How can you? I do not allow this carrion to touch you so﻿—not vot is mine﻿—I do not allow him to live!” And Chandler dropped her and leaped to his feet. He fought. He struggled; but only in his mind, and helplessly; his body carried him out of the room, running and stumbling, out into the drive, into her waiting car and away.

He drove like a madman on roads he had never seen before. The car’s gears bellowed pain at their abuse, the tires screamed.

Chandler, prisoned inside himself, recognized that touch. Koitska! He knew who Rosalie Pan’s lover had been. If he had been in doubt his own voice, raucous and hysterical with rage, told him the truth. All that long drive it screamed threats and obscenities at him, in Russian and tortured English.

===Chinese===

---

姑娘想推开他。她的眼睛瞪得又大又亮，直勾勾地望着他。“安德烈！”

“*Da, Andrei! Kok eto dosadno!*”

“安德烈，求你。我知道你是——”

“肮脏！”钱德勒的嗓音尖叫道。“你怎么能？我不许这具腐尸这样碰你——不许，碰我的东西——我不许他活下去！”钱德勒撇下她，纵身跳起。他搏斗。他挣扎；却只在头脑里，无能为力；他的身体带着他冲出房间，跑着，跌撞着，跑上车道，钻进她那辆候着的汽车，一溜烟去了。

他像疯子一样在从没见过的道路上狂驰。变速箱在蹂躏之下发出痛苦的咆哮，轮胎声声尖叫。

被囚在自己身体里的钱德勒，认出了那触碰的手感。科伊茨卡！他知道了罗莎莉·潘的旧情人是谁。哪怕他先前还有疑问，他自己的嗓音——因狂怒而嘶哑、歇斯底里——也把真相告诉了他。一路漫长的疾驰中，那嗓音用俄语和七扭八歪的英语，冲他尖啸着威胁与咒骂。

===Original===

The car stopped in front of the T.W.A. facility and, still prisoned, his body hurried in, bruising itself deliberately against every doorpost and stick of furniture. “I could have smashed you in the car!” his voice screamed hoarsely. “It is too merciful. I could have thrown you into the sea! It is not painful enough.”

In the garage his body stopped and looked wildly around. “Knives, torches,” his lips chanted. “Shall I gouge out eyes? Slit throat?”

A jar of battery acid stood on a shelf, “*Da, da!*” screamed Chandler, stumbling toward it. “One drink eh? And I von’t even stay vith you to feel it, the pain﻿—just a moment﻿—then it eats the gut, the long slow dying.﻿ …” And all the time the body that was Chandler’s was clawing the cap off the jar, tilting it﻿—

He dropped the jar, and leaped aside instinctively as it splintered at his feet.

He was free!

Before he could move he was seized again, stumbled, crashed into a wall﻿—

And was free again.

===Chinese===

汽车停在环球航空的设施门前，他仍被囚着，他的身体急匆匆闯进去，故意朝每一根门柱、每一件家具上磕碰。“我本可以在车里就捏碎你！”他的嗓音嘶哑地尖叫。“那样太仁慈了。我本可以把你扔进海里！还不够疼。”

在车库里，他的身体停下，狂乱地四下张望。“刀子，喷灯，”他的嘴唇念念有词。“要不要剜眼睛？割喉咙？”

架子上立着一罐电池酸液。“*Da, da!*”钱德勒的嗓音尖叫着，朝它踉跄走去。“喝一口，嗯？而且我还不会陪着你感受它，那疼痛——就一小会儿——然后它咬进肠子，一场漫长而缓慢的死。……”与此同时，那具属于钱德勒的身体一直抠着罐子的盖子，把它倾侧过来——

他松手掉了罐子，在罐子于脚下摔碎的一瞬本能地跳开。

他自由了！

他还来不及动，又被攫住，踉跄了一下，撞上一堵墙——

又自由了。

===Original===

He stood waiting for a moment, unable to believe it; but he was still free. The alien invader did not seize his mind. There was no sound. No one moved. No gun fired at him, no danger threatened.

He *was* free; he took a step, turned, shook his head and proved it.

He was free and, in a moment, realized that he was in the building with the fat bloated body of the man who wanted to murder him, the body that in its own strength could scarcely stand erect.

It was suicide to attempt to harm an exec. He would certainly lose his life﻿—except﻿—that was gone already anyhow; he had lost it. He had nothing left to lose.

===Chinese===

他站着等了片刻，不敢相信；可他仍然自由着。那外来的入侵者没有攫住他的头脑。没有声音。没有人动。没有枪朝他射击，没有任何危险临头。

他*确实*自由了；他迈出一步，转过身，摇了摇头，证实了这一点。

他自由了，而且顷刻间便意识到：此刻他与那个想谋杀他的男人同处一楼——那具臃肿虚胖的身躯，那具单凭自身的力气几乎连站直都困难的身躯。

想加害一个执政者，无异于自杀。他必死无疑——只是——那条命反正早已没了；他已经把它丢掉了。他再没有什么可失去的了。

## XIV / 十四

===Original===

Chandler loped silently up the stairs to Koitska’s suite.

Halfway up he tripped and sprawled, half stunning himself against the stair rail. It had not been his own clumsiness, he was sure. Koitska had caught at his mind again, but only feebly. Chandler did not wait. Whatever was interfering with Koitska’s control, some distraction or malfunction of the coronet or whatever, Chandler could not bank on its lasting.

The door was locked.

He found a heavy mahogany chair, with a back of solid carved wood. He flung it onto his shoulders, grunting, and ran with it into the door, a bull driven frantic, lunging out of its querencia to batter the wall of the arena. The door splintered.

===Chinese===

钱德勒放轻脚步，无声地奔上通往科伊茨卡套房的楼梯。

上到一半他绊了一跤，整个人扑出去，半昏在楼梯扶手上。那不是他自己失手，他敢肯定。科伊茨卡又抓了一把他的心智，只是抓得虚弱。钱德勒不再等。不管是什么在干扰科伊茨卡的控制——某种分神，头冠失灵，或者别的什么——钱德勒都不能指望它一直持续。

门锁着。

他找到一把沉重的红木椅子，靠背是一整块雕花实木。他哼了一声把椅子抡上肩，扛着它冲向房门——像一头被逼疯的公牛，冲出自己的恋守角（querencia），去撞击斗牛场的围墙。门裂了。

===Original===

Chandler was gashed with long slivers of wood, but he was through the door.

Koitska lay sprawled along his couch, eyes staring.

Alive or dead? Chandler did not wait to find out but sprang at him hands outstreched. The staring eyes flickered; Chandler felt the pull at his mind. But Koitska’s strength was almost gone. The eyes glazed, and Chandler was upon him. He ripped the coronet off and flung it aside, and the huge bulk of Koitska swung paralytically off the couch and fell to the floor.

The man was helpless. He lay breathing like a steam engine, one eye pressed shut against the leg of a coffee table, the other looking up at Chandler.

Chandler was panting almost as hard as the helpless mass at his feet. He was safe for a moment. At the most for a moment, for at any time one of the other execs might dart down out of the mind-world into the real, looking at the scene through Chandler’s eyes and surely deducing what would be no more to his favor than the truth. He had to get away from there. If he seemed busy in another room perhaps they would go away again. Chandler turned his back on the paralyzed monster to flee. It would be even better to try to lose himself in Honolulu﻿—if he could get that far﻿—he did not in his own flesh know how to fly the helicopter that was parked in the yard or he would try to get farther still.

But as he turned he was caught.

===Chinese===

钱德勒被长长的木刺划开几道口子，但他穿过了门。

科伊茨卡瘫在长榻上，双眼圆睁。

活着还是死了？钱德勒不等弄清就双手前伸扑了上去。那双圆睁的眼睛颤了颤；钱德勒感到自己的心智被拽了一把。但科伊茨卡的力气几乎耗尽了。那双眼蒙上翳，钱德勒已扑到他身上。他扯下头冠掷到一边，科伊茨卡那庞大的身躯麻痹地一歪，从长榻上滚落在地板上。

这人毫无还手之力了。他躺在那里，像蒸汽机一样喘着，一只眼抵着茶几的桌腿压得闭紧，另一只眼仰望着钱德勒。

钱德勒喘得几乎跟脚下那团无助的肉山一样厉害。他安全了片刻。至多片刻，因为任何时候都可能有一个别的执政者从心灵世界窜进现实，借钱德勒的眼睛看一眼现场，并且必定会推断出一件对他绝无好处的事——实情即是如此。他必须离开这里。要是他看上去正在另一个房间里忙着，他们也许就会走开。钱德勒背过那瘫痪的怪物，准备逃。能设法隐没到檀香山人流里更好——如果他走得到那么远的话——院子里停的那架直升机，他自己的这身肉并不懂得怎么开，不然他还想逃得更远。

可他刚一转身，就被攫住了。

===Original===

---

Chandler turned to see Koitska lying there, and screamed.

His eyes were staring at Koitska. It was too late. He was possessed by someone, he did not know whom. Though it made little enough difference, he thought, watching his own hands reach out to touch the staring face.

His body straightened, his eyes looked around the room, he went to the desk. “Love,” he cried to himself, “what’s the matter with Koitska? Write, for God’s sake!” And he took a pencil in his hand and was free.

He hesitated, then scribbled: *I don’t know. I think he had a stroke. Who are you?*

===Chinese===

---

钱德勒转过身，看见科伊茨卡躺在那里，尖叫起来。

他的眼睛正瞪着科伊茨卡。太迟了。他被什么人附了身，不知道是谁。虽然这也没什么大差别，他想，一面看着自己的双手伸出去，触碰那张瞪视着的脸。

他的身体直起来，眼睛环视房间，走向书桌。“亲爱的，”他对自己喊道，“科伊茨卡怎么了？看在上帝分上，写下来！”于是他手握铅笔，获得了自由。

他犹豫了一下，潦草写道：*我不知道。我想他是中风了。你是谁？*

===Original===

The other mind slipped tentatively into his, scanning the paper. “Rosie, you idiot, who did you think?” he said furiously. “What have you done?”

*Nothing*, he began instinctively, then scratched the word out. Briskly and exactly he wrote: *He was going to kill me, but he had some kind of an attack. I took his coronet away. I was going to run.*

“Oh, you fool,” he told himself shrilly a moment later. Chandler’s body knelt beside the wheezing fat lump, taking its pulse. The faint, fitful throb meant nothing to Chandler; probably meant nothing to Rosie either, for his body stood up, hesitated, shook its head. “You’ve done it now,” he sobbed, and was surprised to find he was weeping real tears. “Oh, love, why? I could have taken care of Koitska﻿—somehow﻿—No, maybe I couldn’t,” he said frantically, breaking down. “I don’t know what to do. Do you have any ideas﻿—outside of running?”

It took him several seconds to write the one word, but it was really all he could find to write. *No.*

His lips twisted as his eyes read the word. “Well,” he said practically, “I guess that’s the end, love. I mean, I give up.”

He got up, turned around the room. “I don’t know,” he told himself worriedly. “There might be a chance﻿—if we could hush this up. I’d better get a doctor. He’ll have to use your body, so don’t be surprised if there’s someone and it isn’t me. Maybe he can pull Andrei through. Maybe Andrei’ll forgive you then﻿—Or if he dies,” Chandler’s voice schemed as his eyes stared at the rasping motionless hulk, “we can say you broke down the door to *help* him. Only you’ll have to put his coronet back on, so it won’t look suspicious. Besides that will keep anyone from occupying him. Do that, love. Hurry.” And he was free.

===Chinese===

那另一个心智试探着滑进他的意识，读纸上的字。“罗西，你这个笨蛋，你以为还能是谁？”他怒气冲冲地说，“你干了什么？”

*没干什么*，他本能地起笔，随即又把这个词划掉。他干脆而精确地写道：*他本来要杀我，可他犯了什么病。我摘走了他的头冠。我正要逃。*

片刻后他尖声对自己说：“哦，你这个傻瓜。”钱德勒的身体跪在那喘息的肥肉团旁边，试它的脉搏。那微弱而零乱的搏动对钱德勒毫无意义；对罗西多半也毫无意义，因为他的身体站了起来，迟疑着，摇了摇头。“这下你闯祸了。”他抽泣道，随即惊讶地发现自己淌下了真眼泪。“哦，亲爱的，为什么？我本来能料理科伊茨卡的——总有办法——不，也许我料理不了。”他狂乱地说，崩溃下来，“我不知道该怎么办。你有什么主意吗——除了逃跑之外？”

那一个词他写了好几秒钟，可他实在也只能想出这一个词来写。*没有。*

他的眼睛读到这个词，嘴唇扭了扭。“唉，”他实际地说，“我想这就是终点了，亲爱的。我是说，我放弃了。”

他站起来，在房间里转了一圈。“我不知道，”他忧心忡忡地对自己说，“也许还有一线机会——要是咱们能把这事瞒下来。我最好找个医生来。他得借用你的身体，所以等会儿换了个人、不是我，你别吃惊。也许他能把安德烈（Andrei）救过来。也许到那时安德烈会饶恕你——要是他死了呢，”钱德勒的嗓音一面谋划着，他的眼睛盯着那喘鸣不动的庞躯，“咱们就说是你破门进去*救*他的。只是你得把他的头冠给他戴回去，免得惹人起疑。再说，那也能免得别的什么人占住他。就这么办，亲爱的。快。”随后他自由了。

===Original===

Gingerly Chandler crossed the floor.

He did not like to touch the dying animal that wheezed before him, liked even less to give it back the weapon that, if it had only a few moments of sentience again, it would use to kill him. But the girl was right. Without the helmet any wandering curi-himself.1 The helmet would shield him from﻿—

Would shield anyone from﻿—

Would shield Chandler himself from possession if he used it!

He did not hesitate. He slipped the helmet on his head, snapped the switch and in a moment stood free of his own body, in the gray, luminous limbo, looking down at the pallid traceries that lay beneath.

===Chinese===

钱德勒战战兢兢地穿过地板。

他不愿碰眼前这头喘息的垂死牲畜，更不愿把它那件武器还给它——只要它再有片刻的神智，那武器就会被用来杀他。可姑娘说得对。不戴上头盔，任何游荡的好奇心智都可能进来占住他。头盔会护住他，不致——

会护住任何人，不致——

要是他自己用它，就会护住钱德勒本人不被附身！

他没有犹豫。他把头盔扣上脑袋，啪地扳动开关，片刻之后便脱出自己的身体而立，置身于那灰色的、泛着微光的中间界，俯瞰底下那些苍白的脉络纹路。

===Original===

---

He did not hesitate then either.

He did not pause to think or plan; it was as though he had planned every step, in long detail, over many years. Chandler for at least a few moments had the freedom to battle the execs on their own ground, the freedom that any mourning parent or husband in the outside world would know well how to use.

Chandler also knew. He was a weapon. He might die﻿—but it was not a great thing to die, millions had done it for nothing under the rule of the execs, and he was privileged to be able to die trying to kill *them*.

He stepped callously around the hulk on the floor and found a door behind the couch, a door and a hall, and at the end of that hall a large room that had once perhaps been a message center. Now it held rack after rack of electronic gear. He recognized it without elation. It had had to be there.

It was the main transmitter for all the coronets of the exec.

===Chinese===

---

那时他也没有犹豫。

他没有停下来思考或计划；仿佛每一步他都已细细谋划过多年。至少在接下来这几个片刻里，钱德勒获得了在执政者自己的地盘上与他们作战的自由——外面世界里任何一个丧子的父母、丧妻的丈夫，都深知这种自由该怎么用。

钱德勒也知道。他是一件武器。他可能会死——可死并不是什么了不得的事，在执政者们的统治下，几百万人已经死得一文不值，而他有幸能死在猎杀*他们*的尝试里。

他冷酷地绕过地上的庞躯，在长榻后面找到一扇门，一扇门和一条走廊，走廊尽头是一间大屋，从前也许是个讯息中心。如今屋里立着一排又一排电子设备。他认出了它，并不欣喜。它必然在这里。

这是供全体执政者所有头冠使用的主发射机。

===Original===

He had only to pull one switch﻿—that one there﻿—and power would cease to flow. The coronets would be dead. The execs would be only humans. In five minutes he could destroy enough parts so that it would be at least a week’s work to build it again, and in a week the slaves in Honolulu﻿—somehow he could reach them, somehow he would tell them of their chance﻿—could root out and destroy every exec on all the islands.

Of course, there was the standby transmitter he himself had helped to build.

He realized tardily that Koitska would have made some arrangement for starting that up by remote control.

He put down the tool-kit with which he had been advancing on the racks of transistors, and paused to think.

He was a fool, he saw after a moment. He could not destroy this installation﻿—not yet﻿—not until he had used it. He remembered to sit down so that his body would not crash to the floor, and then he sent himself out and up, to scan the nearby area.

There was no one there, nobody within a mile or more, except the feeble glimmer that was dying Koitska. He did not enter that body. He returned to his own long enough to barricade the door﻿—it had a strong-looking lock, but he shouldered furniture against it too﻿—and then he went up and out, grateful to Rosalie, who had taught him how to navigate in the curious world of the mind, flashing across water, under a mind-controlled plane, to the island of Hilo.

===Chinese===

他只需拉开一个开关——那边那个——电流就会断绝。头冠就会死透。执政者们就只是凡人。五分钟之内他就能毁掉足够多的部件，让人至少要花一个礼拜才能重建；而一个礼拜之内，檀香山的奴隶们——他总有办法找到他们，总有办法把他们的机会告诉他们——足以把各岛上的执政者连根拔除，统统消灭。

当然，还有那台他亲手帮着装起来的备用发射机。

他迟迟才醒悟，科伊茨卡必定安排了远距离启动它的手段。

他放下一直提着、正朝晶体管机架逼近的工具箱，停下来思考。

过了一会儿他明白了：自己是个傻瓜。他还不能毁掉这座设施——还不是时候——要先用过它才行。他记着坐下来，免得自己的身体轰然栽倒在地板上，然后他把自己送出去、送上去，扫描附近的区域。

那里没有人，一英里方圆之内没有人，除了那一点垂死的科伊茨卡的微弱微光。他没有进那具身体。他回到自己身体里，耽了刚好够把门堵死的时间——门上有一把看上去结实的锁，他还是用肩膀扛了家具顶上——然后他升上去、出去，满心感激罗莎莉，是她教会了他如何在心灵的奇境里航行；他掠过海面，从一架被心智控制的飞机底下穿过，直抵希洛岛。

===Original===

There *had* to be someone near the standby installation.

He searched; but there was no one. No one in the building. No one near the ruined field. No one in the village of the dead nearby. He was desperate; he became frantic; he was on the point of giving up, and then he found﻿—someone? But it was a personality feebler than stricken Koitska’s, a bare swampfire glow.

No matter. He entered it.

===Chinese===

备用设施附近*必定*有个人。

他搜索；可没有人。楼里没有人。废机场附近没有人。近旁那座死亡之镇里也没有人。他绝望了；他狂乱了；他眼看就要放弃，这时他找到了——一个什么人？可那是个比病倒的科伊茨卡还微弱的人格，一团沼火似的幽光。

管它呢。他进去了。

===Original===

---

At once he screamed silently and left it again. He had never known such pain. A terrifying fire in the belly, a thunder past any migraine in the head, a thousand lesser aches and woes in every member. He could not imagine what person lived in such distress; but grimly he forced himself to enter again.

Moaning﻿—it was astonishing how thick and animal-like the man’s voice was﻿—Chandler forced his borrowed body stumbling through the jungle. Time was growing very short. He drove it gasping at an awkward run across the airfield, dodged around one wrecked plane and blundered through the door. The pain was intolerable. He was hardly able to maintain control.

Chandler stretched out the borrowed hand to pick up a heavy wrench even while he thought. But the hand would not grasp. He brought it to the weak, watering eyes. The hand had no fingers. It ended in a ball of scar tissue. The left hand was nearly as misshapen.

Panicked, Chandler retreated from the body in a flash, back to his own; and then he began to think.

It was, it had to be, the creature he had seen in the village of the dead. A leper. One of the few who escaped from the colony at Molokai. Chandler drove himself back to that body and, though it could not work well, he could make it turn a frequency dial, using its clubbed hands like sticks. He could make it throw a switch. He then caused it to place the toothed edge of a rusting saw on the ground and strike at it with its throat in a sort of reverse guillotine. Chandler could not see that he had a choice; he dared not have that creature left where it might be seized the moment he quit its body. It was better dead.

===Chinese===

---

他立刻无声地尖叫着退了出来。他从不知道有这样的疼痛。腹中一团骇人的火，头上一阵远超任何偏头痛的雷鸣，四肢百骸里上千种较轻的酸痛与苦楚。他无法想象什么人竟能生活在这样的苦楚里；但他咬紧牙关，强迫自己再进去。

呻吟着——这人的嗓音竟如此浑浊、如此牲口一般，令人吃惊——钱德勒驱着他借来的身体跌跌撞撞穿过丛林。时间越来越紧。他赶着它喘息着、以笨拙的跑姿横过机场，绕过一架失事飞机的残骸，一头撞进楼门。疼痛无法忍受。他几乎维持不住控制。

钱德勒一边寻思，一边伸出借来的那只手去拿一把重扳手。可那只手抓不拢。他把它举到那双畏光流泪的眼前。手没有手指。末端是一团球状的瘢痕。左手也几乎一样畸形。

钱德勒惊慌起来，一闪退出那具身体，回到自己体内；然后他开始思考。

那准是、只能是他当天在死亡之镇里瞥见过的那个活物。一个麻风病人。从莫洛凯隔离区逃出的寥寥几人之一。钱德勒把自己赶回那具身体，它虽不中用，他仍能用它转动频率度盘——把它棒槌似的双手当成棍子使。他能让它扳动一个开关。然后他让它把一把锈锯的锯齿立在地面，用它自己的喉咙向锯齿撞去——一种倒过来的断头台。钱德勒看不出自己还有什么别的选择；他不敢把那活物留在原地，怕的是他一退出这具身体，它立刻就被别人攫去。死了更好。

===Original===

After that it all became easy.

In his own body he destroyed the installation in Oahu. A few minutes at Koitska’s work bench, and he had changed the frequency on his own coronet to transmit on the new band the leper’s touch had given the Hilo equipment.

He worked rapidly and without errors, one ear cocked for the sound of someone coming to threaten what he was doing (the sound never came), impatient to get the job done.

He was very impatient, for when he was done he would be the only exec.

And the execs would be only slaves.

===Chinese===

此后，一切都容易了。

在自己的身体里，他摧毁了瓦胡岛的设施。在科伊茨卡的工作台前干了几分钟，他把自己头冠上的频率，改到了麻风病人的触碰赋给希洛设备的新频段上。

他干得飞快而且无误，一只耳朵支棱着，提防有人前来破坏他的活计（那声音始终没有来），只巴望赶紧收工。

他急不可耐，因为等他干完，他就是唯一的执政者了。

而执政者们，将只是奴隶。

## XV / 十五

===Original===

Chandler strolled out of the T.W.A. building, very tired.

It was dawn. His job was done. He carried the coronet, the only working coronet in the world, in his hand. He had spent the night killing, killing, killing, and blood had washed away his passions; he was spent. He had killed every exec he could find, in widening circles from the building where his body lay. He had slit his dozen throats and fired bullets into his hundred hearts and hundred brains; he had entered bodies only long enough to feel for a coronet, and if it was there the body was doomed; and he stopped only when it occurred to him he wasn’t even doing that much any more. He had probably killed some dozens of slaves, as well as all the execs in reach. And when he stopped the orgy of killing he had made one last search of the nearer portions of the island and found no one alive, and he had then realized that one of the closest execs had been Rosalie Pan.

He knew that in a while he would feel very badly for having killed that girl (which could she have been? The one with the shotgun in the mouth? The one whose intestines he had spilled with a silver letteropener in a whim of hara-kiri?), but just now he was too worn.

He was Chandler the giant killer, who had destroyed the creatures who had destroyed a world, but he was all tired out. He poked at the filigree of the coronet absently, as a man might caress the pretty rug which once had been the skin of a tiger that almost killed him. It was all that was left of the exec power. Who held this single coronet still held the world.

Of course, said a sly and treasonable voice in a corner of his mind, the job was not really done.

===Chinese===

钱德勒踱出环球航空大楼，疲惫不堪。

天已破晓。他的活计做完了。他手里提着那顶头冠——世上仅存的一顶还能用的头冠。他一整夜都在杀、杀、杀，鲜血已把他的种种激愤冲刷一空；他心力交瘁。以他那具肉身躺着的大楼为圆心，他一圈圈向外扩，杀掉了找得到的每一个执政者。他割开过十来条喉咙，把子弹射进过成百颗心脏、成百个头脑；他进入一具具躯壳，只为时短暂地探一探上头有没有头冠，若有，那具躯壳便在劫难逃；直到他察觉自己连这一点也不再做了，方才住手。除了够得着的执政者，他多半还捎带杀了几十个奴隶。而当他停下这场杀戮的狂欢，对岛的近处做了最后一遍搜索，没有发现一个活人，这时他才意识到，离得最近的那些执政者当中，有一个曾是罗莎莉·潘。

他知道，过不了多久，他就会为杀了那姑娘而感到万分难受（她会是哪一个呢？是把猎枪口塞进嘴里的那一个？还是被他在一时兴起之下用一柄银质裁纸刀豁开肚肠、玩了一把切腹的那一个？），可眼下他实在累乏透了。

他就是那位杀巨人的钱德勒，除灭了毁灭了一个世界的族类，可他已经筋疲力尽。他心不在焉地拨弄着头冠的金银丝细工，就像一个人爱抚一张漂亮的地毯——它从前是一只险些要了他性命的老虎的皮。执政者的权势，剩下的就全在这上头了。谁握着这仅存的一顶头冠，谁就仍然握着整个世界。

当然喽，他脑海一隅有个阴险而大逆不道的声音说，这活计其实还没有真正做完。

===Original===

Not quite. Not all.

The job would not be done until it was impossible for anyone to find enough of the installations to be able to reconstruct them.

And then, said the voice, while Chandler stared at the dawn, listening, what about the *good* things the exec had done? Would he not be foolish to throw away so casually this one, unique chance to right every imaginable wrong the world might do him?

Chandler went back into the building and brewed some strong black coffee. While it was bubbling on the stove he slipped the coronet back atop his head. Only for a while, he promised. A very little while. He pledged himself solemnly that it would be just long enough to clean up all loose ends﻿—not a moment longer, he pledged. And knew that he was lying.

===Chinese===

不完全。还没有全做完。

这活计要等到任何人都不可能再找齐足够的设备、把头冠重新造出来，才算真正做完。

还有呢，那声音趁钱德勒凝望晨光、侧耳倾听的当儿说，执政者们做过的那些*好*事又该怎么算？要是他就这么随随便便地丢掉这独一无二的机会——一个能纠正世上可能加诸他的一切想象得到的冤屈的机会——他岂不成了傻瓜？

钱德勒回到楼里，煮了一壶浓黑咖啡。咖啡在炉上咕嘟冒泡的当儿，他把头冠重新扣回自己头顶。只戴一小会儿，他许诺道。很小的一会儿。他郑重立誓：只戴到把一切未了之事清理干净为止——多一刻也不戴，他立誓道。而且他心里明白，自己在撒谎。
